SOURCE_URL: https://unibradfordac.sharepoint.com/sites/student-travel-and-insurance-intranet/SitePages/Student-travel-authorisation-and-booking.aspx

Student Travel and Insurance
Travel authorisation and booking
Travel process overview
The University has appointed Diversity Travel as its travel management company. All University funded travel must be booked using Diversity Travel and it is strongly recommended to do so if travel is self-funded. Diversity Travel provide 24-hour support to those travelling.
All overseas travel, and any UK travel which costs more than £500 must be authorised prior to booking by completing a travel request form, and overseas travel risk assessment form.
Upon completion of these you will receive 2 HSW reference numbers. You will not be able to book travel with the Universities travel management company without these.
Travel policy
University's travel policy applies to all students using University and other official funds for business travel and expenses.
Before booking travel within the UK
- Travel costing more than £500 requires authorisation, you must complete an online travel request. Once approved, travel can be booked using the University's travel management company using the HSW reference generated by the approval process.
- Travel costing less than £500 can be booked directly with Diversity Travel. This must be agreed by your manager or supervisor.
Before booking overseas travel
- Complete the online travel request for students. Once approved, a risk assessment will be emailed for completion. Upon completion you will receive 2 HSW reference numbers. Provide these when booking with the University's travel management company.
- Guests travelling on University business, including accompanying partners and children of students, must submit a completed guest travel request form and a risk assessment form. Once completed send to h.e.whitehead@bradford.ac.uk to be issued with a HSW reference number for the booking.
- Review and follow all advice and requirements, including those related to COVID, on the UK Foreign and Commonwealth Office (FCO)
website. Overseas travel to destinations that the FCO advise against all travel is not permitted. Where the FCO advises against all but essential travel Senior Management must approve travel as part of the travel request. You may need to submit further information about the critical nature of travel.
- Email h.e.whitehead@bradford.ac.uk to report any trip lasting over 12 months.
- Email h.e.whitehead@bradford.ac.uk if a letter of insurance is required to obtain a travel visa.
-Apply for a UK Global Health Insurance Card (GHIC) if you do not already have one for reduced healthcare costs in Europe.
Travel VISA requirements
- It is the responsibility of those travelling to check visa and immigration requirements carefully for both your destination and all transit points, using government websites or the Diversity Travel Sherpa Tool. Especially for all non-UK / EU passport holders, you may need to apply for visas to connect in popular transit airports such as Amsterdam (KLM), Paris (Air France) or Frankfurt / Munich (Lufthansa).
- Apply for a VISA at least 10 weeks before a planned trip.
- Travel insurance will not cover any trips that are cancelled. or disrupted due to a VISA problems.
- Please advise your agents at Diversity Travel at enquiry stage if you or fellow travellers need to apply for visas.
Email h.e.whitehead@bradford.ac.uk if a letter of insurance is required to obtain a travel Visa.
Booking air or rail travel and accommodation with Diversity Travel
- Turing placement travel bookings should be booked by phoning Diversity Travel and identify as a Turing placement student. Bookings can be made by card payment. Registration with Diversity Travel is not required.
- Other travel paid for independently should be booked by using the online self booking tool or by phoning or emailing the Oak Team at Diversity Travel. Registration with Diversity Travel is required.
- Travel paid directly by the University should be booked through Diversity Travel, using their online self booking tool. Registration with Diversity Travel is required.
- Registration with Diversity Travel can be done by completing the new online user request form. A welcome email, including log in details will be issued.
- Diversity Travel offer competitive rates for rail, air and accommodation bookings. They offer academic and negotiated air fares where available, these may include flexible and refundable fares and additional baggage.
- Diversity Travel can offer National Express bookings as an alternative to rail travel. Phone or email Diversity to enquire and book.
Diversity Travel Pine Team contact information:
- Tel: +44 (0) 161 235 5414 - Monday to Friday, 08:45 - 17.30
- Emergency out of hours: 44+ (0) 161 300 8258
- Email: pine@diversitytravel.co.uk
Before you travel
- Check the FCO website for updates for your destination. If the FCO issue new advice against all or all but essential travel, travel may have to be cancelled or re-arranged. Email h.e.whitehead@bradford.ac.uk or l.tejeda@bradford.ac.uk for advice.
- Take a copy of the Chubb assistance for travellers when travelling. You may have to show this upon entry to your destination as proof of COVID emergency medical insurance cover.
- Download the University's SafeZone App for 24-hour support.

SOURCE_URL: https://unibradfordac.sharepoint.com/sites/health-safety-and-wellbeing-intranet/SitePages/SafeZone.aspx

Health and Safety
Safezone
'SafeZone'
SafeZone is a free service for all University of Bradford Staff and Students; it makes it easier for you to alert the Security team here at the University should you need help whilst on campus.
SafeZone works by sending your name and location directly to the University security control room, this allows a swift and appropriate response to provide you with appropriate support.With SafeZone on your phone, you’re connected to University Security whenever you need it, 24/7.
SafeZone is compatible with iPhone, Android and Windows phones and tablets. By installing the SafeZone app and signing up as a user, you will also receive any critical notifications from the university.
SafeZone provides all staff and students with peace of mind. In the unlikely event of any problem while on campus, you can get help quickly and easily. The App works on University of Bradford City campus and at the University nursery and is FREE using your University of Bradford email address. We would encourage everyone to sign up for SafeZone on their mobile device.
It is mandatory that staff and students install SafeZone in order for overseas travel to be authorised. This is to facilitate two-way communication in the event of an emergency occurring whilst overseas.
The SafeZone app can be downloaded to either iPhone or Android
Note: If you activate your phone outside of the University defined SafeZone boundary, your will be given the option to contact the emergency services (Police, Fire or Ambulance).
If the SafeZone system is used whilst attending some other Universities operating an alliance system the respective University Security team will reach out to you.
Non-Urgent Enquiries
For non-urgent and 'general' enquiries during 'normal' office hours the reception team can be contacted.
First Aid
If you need medical assistance? Pressing the first aid button it will connect you immediately to the University Security Team. Once you've activated this function, Security will be able to track your approximate location.
Emergencies
If you feel threatened or see something suspicious? Pressing the EMERGENCY CALL button will immediately connect you with our Security team. Once you've activated this function, the Security team will be able to track your approximate location.
Wellbeing
The wellbeing icon when pressed will signpost support service in relation to Wellbeing, the icon has been configured to provide different information dependent on whether the activation has come from a staff or student phone respectively.
Lone Working Application
Lone Worker Check-In Timer
Manual Check-In
If you are working alone, outside normal hours, or are entering a high-risk work environment, SafeZone allows you to check-in to share your location with members of the Security team. The Icon will change to a 'checked' icon, and you will receive a confirmation message that you have been 'Checked-In'.
If you do this, your location and ID will be displayed on a map to Security to see for the duration of your Check-In session.
SafeZone will automatically finish your session as you leave the University boundary, or you can Check-Out again at any time by tapping the button again.
Check-in Timer
You can also set a check-in timer, which checks you in (the same as Manual Check-In) and then starts a countdown timer to cover the duration of your work session.
The Timer will count down to zero, starting from your nominated time. At any time, you can tap the Timer to cancel or re-set the time, and you can also Check Out.
You will receive a Warning push message at '5 minutes to go', when you can re-set the time, cancel the Timer or Check Out.
If the Timer expires, SafeZone will raise an Alert, showing your location and ID on a map to Security, who will then attend your location to ensure that you are OK.
Automatic Check-In
With your permission, your phone can be set to 'Check you in' whenever you move into a specially designated region on campus. Subject to you granting Auto-Check In permission, your phone will check you in or out, based on your current location. Location is updated at a low frequency to minimise battery impact. Auto Check-In does not require SafeZone to be running, your Check-In status is managed by the phone’s operating system. You can disable this feature at any time, which will stop the phone doing location checks.
1. If you have activated the Automatic Check-ins option, you will receive a Push Notification each time your phone Checks you in as you enter compatible Regions.
2. The check in function sends location updates about every 5 minutes, so as not to have any significant impact on your battery usage.
3. A single Check In session can last for up to 12 hours.
4. You must have been Signed In to SafeZone and not Signed Out (the app can be in background, or completely 'killed')
5. Auto check in will keep working until you return to the settings screen and disable 'Allow Automatic Check In', which you can do at any time.
Report Something
A new function of the SafeZone app is the tip reporting, or as it is identified in the screen shot below 'report something'.
This allows individuals to send a message to the University Security team and report a variety of issues including:
Assault/Abuse/Harassment
Hate Crime
Disturbances
Suspicious activity/theft/vandalism etc.
The report can also be done anonymously, to observers it will look as though the individual is just messaging.
Frequently Asked Questions
What is it for?
SafeZone is a free app that allows you to send a location-based alert from your smartphone or tablet to University Security Services whenever you need an immediate emergency response, first-aid or any other urgent help/information.
What are the recommended configuration settings for the SafeZone app?
Some of these will be configured for you as part of the installation process, which will ask your permission before changing any settings.
- Location Services Enabled – you will be prompted for this when installing the App.
- Wi-Fi enabled – having Wi-Fi enabled (without even being connected to a network) will significantly improve the accuracy of the location information sent by your device.
- Wi-Fi connected to the University network – being connected to the campus Wi-Fi will help ensure that your alert will be sent, even if your mobile service provider does not have coverage where you are on campus.
Emergency Messaging Service
SafeZone uses high-speed push messaging to allow the University to send you important notifications in the case of an emergency or critical incident on campus. Notifications may be accompanied by a loud alarm sound or in some circumstances, silent with a buzz. Be sure to follow any directions you are given, and where provided, tap ‘Open Link’ for further advice.
I have a new phone - what do I do?
If you are an existing user, simply download and install SafeZone and tap the icon to start the app. Sign In with your previous e-mail address and password, then you’re all set. If you have forgotten your password, you can request an automated Password Reset from the Sign In screen.
I don't want to make a fuss but I've been threatened or I was assaulted - what can I do?
The University Security team are onsite 24 hours a day 7 days a week via a phone call and they can then talk to you and help you whatever the situation, their 'non-emergency' number is 01274 236999.
In addition to the Security team you can also contact the Student Support Services and Counselling Services for confidential discussion, please follow the links at the bottom of this page.
What if I initiate an alert outside the Campus boundary?
If you are outside of the University’s SafeZone boundary when you activate an Alert, your phone will tell you that you’re outside the zone and offer you a connection to “999” for emergency services, the app will also give your GPS coordinates that you can pass on to the emergency services.
What if I accidentally press a call button?
If you accidentally press any SafeZone Call button, you can cancel the Call by tapping the button again while the “Circle” timer is winding down. After the Call (Alert) has been sent, you can still cancel your request, but you will still be contacted by the University Security team to be sure that you are OK.
Desktop Version
A desktop version of the mobile app is now available, this can be downloaded from the University Software Centre.
Privacy
SafeZone does not track your location. You only become visible on the system once you have raised an alert or checked in.
How will I know that my call has been received?
After pressing any of the three Call buttons, your phone will display the information shown above;
What do The three icons mean:
- Obtaining your location (checked when successful)
- Transmitting your alert to security(checked when successful)
- Awaiting Acknowledgement from University Security - the Text changes from “Alert Sent” to “Alert Acknowledged” once a Security Team member has seen your request and physically sent you their acknowledgement. The icon will be checked and the phone will also vibrate when this happens. This means they are about to call you (if you don’t call them) and they are now responding to your request.
At this point, you can call security directly by tapping the Green button, or Cancel the Alert by tapping the Red button. If you cancel at this point, Security will still call you to check that you are OK as they will have received an initial alert
Overseas Travel and the SafeZone app
Emergency
- if you have an emergency whilst overseas by activating the red emergency icon you will be directed to the respective Countries Emergency Services, simultaneously an alert will be sent to the University Security Control room who will then try to contact you.
First Aid
- Should you find yourself requiring First Aid you can activate the green icon, this will dial the University Security Control Room, please note that the request for first aid treatment will be restricted to verbal advice only. No information on how to seek assistance within a foreign country can be provided.
The non-urgent icon will send an alert to the University security control room who will try to contact you to ascertain what assistance you require.
Please note this should not be used for general 'chit-chat’. You should use your already established channels for general communications with people in the UK.

Student Travel and Insurance
Student travel insurance
Student Travel Insurance cover:
The University provides student travel insurance for pre-approved overseas travel and UK travel that includes an overnight stay, that is related to study or research activities.
Please be aware the University will change its Travel Insurance provider from the 31st of October from Chubb Assist to RSA - further information below.
Who is covered?
- All registered students attending the University in the UK on approved work, study or research related travel.
What is covered?
- Emergency medical treatment, including COVID treatment if a person tests positive whilst travelling. Important: policy excess applies.
- Cancellation, curtailment and disruption, repatriation.
- Personal belongings.Important: policy excess applies.
What is not covered, and is excluded from the Travel Policy Cover:
- Electronic items, such as mobile phones, laptops, Smart watches. Please seek your own insurance cover, if required.
- Overseas travel to destinations that the UK Foreign and Commonwealth Office (FCO) advise against all travel is not permitted. Where the FCO advises against all but essential travel Senior Management must approve travel as part of the travel request. You may need to submit further information about the critical nature of travel. Travel to destinations that the Foreign Office advise against all travel to may not be covered by insurance.
- Travel disruption expenses due to strike action, labour dispute, mechanical breakdown, which existed or the possibility of which existed and for which advance warning had been given before the date on which the Journey was booked.
- Extreme sports such as, bungee jumping, skiing, snowboarding, skydiving, kite surfing, paragliding, scuba diving, skateboarding. Please seek your own insurance cover, if you intend on partaking in these activities.
- Medical insurance cover for COVID related medical costs, if a person begins an overseas trip having tested positive for COVID. Additional accommodation costs will not be covered if there is a requirement to isolate upon arrival.
- Emergency medical treatment - if advised against travel by a healthcare professional on medical grounds. Please email h.e.whitehead@bradford.ac.uk if further assistance is required.
- Emergency medical assistance for students returning to their country of residence, or if eligible to state health care.
Travel Policy - excesses:
- An excess of £150 will apply to any lost, stolen or damaged personal belongings, before a claim will be considered.
- An excess of £150 will apply to any emergency medical expenses, before a claim will be considered.
Contact information
If you experience a medical emergency or need to contact our travel insurers for help or support before the 31st of October 2024:
- Call the Chubb Assistance 24-hour emergency helpline on +44 (0) 20 7173 7796, or email medicalassistance@chubb.com. You will need to quote policy number UKBBBO39618, or
- Email h.e.whitehead@bradford.ac.uk
- Chubb provide a 24-hour Travel Insurance Customer Service from multi-lingual staff to assist in an emergency.
If you experience a medical emergency or need to contact our travel insurers for help or support after the 1st of November 2024:
- Call the RSA HELPLINE NUMBER +44 (0) 20 8763 3155 - Services can be accessed 24 hours a day 365 days a year.
- The new RSA Travel Policy Number is PA04282115
Please take a copy of the UoB - RSA Medical Assist Card when travelling.
Making a claim
Should you need to make a travel insurance claim, please contact h.e.whitehead@bradford.ac.uk or l.tejeda@bradford.ac.uk to request a claim form.
Keep a copy of all receipts, travel tickets, medical costs and police reports, you will be required to submit these with your travel insurance claim form.

Disability Services
Disability Service
Welcome to Disability Service at the University of Bradford.
Being disabled means different things to different people. You may not consider yourself to be disabled. However, a disability can be any long term condition which has a substantial affect on day-to-day activities, including education.
Our committed team is here to ensure that all students with disabilities receive the support they need to succeed in their academic journey. We offer a range of services, including advice and guidance, and personalised support plans, to help you address any challenges you may face.
As a service, we maintain the University’s core values in creating an inclusive and accessible learning environment for all students. We aim to ensure that students with diverse needs can thrive, stay on track, and succeed in their academic journey throughout their time at the University of Bradford.
Who can use the service?
Any student who has a physical or mental impairment that substantially impacts their ability to carry out everyday activities, including studying. Substantial means more than minor or trivial, and long-term means it has lasted or is likely to last 12 months or more. This is classed as a disability according to the Equality Act of 2010.
Universities have a duty under the Equality Act 2010 to make reasonable adjustments to ensure all students, including those with disabilities, have equal access to education. This helps create an inclusive environment where everyone can stay on track, thrive and succeed in their academic journey.
We can support you if you have:
- Long-term medical / neurological conditions
- Long-term mental health condition
- Neurodiversity
- Physical and / or mobility impairment
- Sensory Impairments
- Specific Learning Difficulties (SpLD)
If you think the Disability Service can help you, please get in touch.
How to tell us about your disability or condition
You can refer your self via the MyBradford – Request for Support form. You can also email disabilities@bradford.ac.uk if you would like to discuss things further.
We are available on the front desk in Student Central between 10am - 3pm Monday - Friday for quick questions or queries
Being disabled means different things to different people. You may not consider yourself to be disabled. However, a disability can be any long term condition which has a substantial affect on day-to-day activities, including education.
You may wish to provide optional supporting documents such as a Form 8, medical letter or an SpLD diagnostic report. This will help us assess your needs for reasonable adjustments. However, we do assess individual needs on a case-by-case basis so please get in touch as soon as you can.

Counselling and Mental Health Service
Urgent Help
Are you, or someone you know, in immediate danger?
- Call 999 or go to the nearest A&E
Help from the NHS
- Call NHS 111. If you are unsure, call 111. They are open 24 hours a day and can provide advice and guidance when you need help urgently but not in an emergency (A&E should always be used in an emergency)
- Call Bradford NHS First Response on 0800 952 1181 in a mental health crisis.
- Talk to your GP. Ask at your GP for an Emergency Appointment. If you are waiting for an appointment please also consider who you can talk to from the information below
Someone to talk to:
- University of Bradford Counselling & Mental Health Service
  -- Open Monday - Friday, 8.30am - 4.30pm.
  -- counselling@bradford.ac.uk / +44 (0)1274 235750.

- West Yorkshire 24-hour Mental Health Helpline - 0800 183 0558
- Qwell- Free digital chat and mental wellbeing support for adults in the UK.
- Text "Shout" to 85258 - giveusashout.org: free 24/7 text service for anyone in crisis
Places to go:
- Safe Spaces - a crisis support service for anyone in mental health distress. It provides a homely place to stay overnight for children and young people aged seven to 17.
  -- Call First Response on 0800 952 1181 at any time and ask for "Safe Spaces"
  -- Call Guide-Line on 08001 884 884 or chat online between 8am and midnight
- Andy's Man Club - a men’s suicide prevention charity, offering free-to-attend peer-to-peer support groups across the United Kingdom and online.
Other forms of support:
- How to Cope with Suicidal Thoughts
- Download our SafeZone app to your phone to request help and alert University of Bradford security staff.
- Check out West Yorkshire Police's Student Safety website
- The Complete University Guide also has a useful guide to staying safe at university.
- Look through our Self-Help resources.
If you are worried about a friend or housemate, please contact us and ask to speak to one of our Mental Health Advisors. They will be able to talk to you about your concerns and options for support.
- mhadvice@bradford.ac.uk
- +44(0) 1274 235 750
Please also contact us if you need to speak with someone following an extreme mental health event with a student or staff member.
- counselling@bradford.ac.uk
- +44 (0)1274 235 750

SOURCE_URL: https://unibradfordac.sharepoint.com/sites/registry-and-student-admin-intranet/SitePages/Extenuating-Circumstances.aspx

Registry and Student Administration
Consideration of Personal Circumstances
What is Consideration of Personal Circumstances?
During your studies, you might experience difficult circumstances which impact your ability to attend learning sessions or complete assessments. This might include, for example, a serious illness, or personal issues such as bereavement. It is important that you tell someone at the University as soon as you can about any such issues so you can get the advice and support you need.
As soon as an issue arises, let your Module Tutor, Personal Academic Tutor or Supervisor know. They will then determine how best to support you. This may include support sessions to help with your assignment, or helping you to access other support services including the Student Success Team or Counselling Service
Extensions and Extenuating Circumstances
Even with appropriate support, students may sometimes be unable to complete assignments as required due to personal circumstances beyond their control. In these instances students may request an Extension or Extenuating Circumstances
A one week Extension can be requested on occasions where you will be able to complete a coursework assignment, but need a little extra time to do so. Please be aware that not all assessments are eligible for extensions - in particular any formative assignments, examinations, lab tests, quizzes and live presentations cannot be extended. More information is available in the Extensions FAQ.
Extenuating Circumstances are for occasions when you are unable to complete an assessment and, if approved, will permit you to complete the assignment at the next available opportunity (usually the supplementary period in the summer) at the same attempt. More information is available in the Extenuating Circumstances FAQ.
When can I request an Extension or Extenuating Circumstances?
Extensions must be requested by no later than 12:00 noon on the day of your deadline. You may only apply for one deadline extension for each piece of coursework. You do not need to provide evidence to request an extension.
Extenuating Circumstances must be requested no later than 7 days after the date of your assessment/deadline. You must provide evidence for an Extenuating Circumstances application to be accepted. If you are considering submitting ECs in advance of the deadline, please make sure you are certain that you will not be able to attempt the assessment before doing so.
What will happen if my request is accepted?
When an Extension request is accepted your Faculty's Programme Administration Team will amend your Canvas submission deadline as required. You will then need to submit in the same way as normal, making sure you meet the extended deadline. Please note that if you do not submit your assignment by the extended deadline you will receive a mark of 0 as normal.
When an Extenuating Circumstances request is accepted, a note is made on your record and your mark for the assessment is recorded as 0 with a grade of “CS”. This indicates that you will be permitted to take the assessment again at the next available opportunity (usually the supplementary period) at the same attempt status. Please note that once an application is accepted no other mark will be awarded for the assessment.

SOURCE_URL: https://unibradfordac.sharepoint.com/sites/registry-and-student-admin-intranet/SitePages/Transfers,-Suspensions-and-Withdrawals.aspx

Registry and Student Administration
Transfers, Suspensions and Withdrawals
Transferring to a different course
First, you should discuss this with the Programme Leader for the course you wish to transfer onto, who will need to review your entry grades and academic performance and decide whether you can be accepted onto the programme. If you are unsure who the Programme Leader is for the course please contact the relevant Programme Administration Team
If you are studying on a visa sponsored by the University, you should also ensure that you discuss any potential change to your programme with the Visa Support Team before submitting any request.
You should then complete the Programme Transfer Agreement form. This must be signed by the Programme Leader of the programme you wish to transfer to. A copy of this should then be attached to your request via e:Vision:
Log onto e:Vision
Click on the Change of Circumstances link at the bottom.
In the drop down menu, select Programme change and click Next
Follow the instructions on e:Vision to complete the process. Ensure you attach your signed Programme Transfer Agreement form.
If you are accepted onto your new course, you will have to inform the Student Loan Company or your sponsor. Once your course transfer is agreed, we will also write to your funding body to finalise your transfer.
Suspending Your Studies
It is sometimes necessary for students to take time out of their studies. This could be for many reasons including health problems, family or work commitments.
If you would like to suspend your course you should first discuss with your faculty why you want to suspend, as they may be able to support you to manage your studies alongside other commitments.
If you decide that you would like to suspend your course, you need to contact your Personal Academic Tutor (PAT) and after discussion with them please visit e:Vision:
- Log onto e:Vision
- Click on the Change of Circumstances link at the bottom.
- In the drop down menu, select Suspend study
- Follow the instructions on e:Vision to complete the process.
Please be aware that:
When students suspend studies, the expectation is that they will normally return at the next enrolment point available for their programme of study (the following September for most programmes).
Students may not normally suspend their studies for more than a year. Students who are invited to re-enrol after their suspension is due to end, and fail to do so, are likely to be withdrawn.
The Suspension of Studies policy is available on the University Website.
Students studying on a Visa Sponsored by the University should also discuss the implications of suspending their studies with the
Visa Support Team
Withdrawing from your course
If you would like to leave the University, you should first discuss with your present department why you want to leave, as they may be able to help.
If you decide that you would like to leave, you need to contact your Personal Academic Tutor (PAT) and after discussion with them please visit e:Vision:
- Log onto e:Vision
- Click on the Change of Circumstances link at the bottom.
- In the drop down menu, select Withdrawal and click Next
- Follow the instructions on e:Vision to complete the process.
Questions
If you have any questions about Transferring Course, Suspending or Withdrawing, please contact either your Personal Academic Tutor (PAT) or the Programme Administration Team

SOURCE_URL: https://unibradfordac.sharepoint.com/sites/student-casework-intranet/SitePages/Academic-Misconduct.aspx

Student Casework
Academic Integrity and Academic Misconduct
Here you will find information for students and staff about the University of Bradford's expectations in relation to the integrity of academic work, as well as the regulation, policy and guidance relating to the investigation of Academic Misconduct.
Academic Integrity: what is it and why is it important?
Academic Integrity means acting in a way that is honest, fair, respectful, ethical and responsible when it comes to the preparation and completion of academic work at University and beyond.
We expect students to act with integrity when it comes to their assessments. This means presenting their own work, written in their own words and clearly indicating through referencing where they have read and used the work and ideas of others. This idea of acknowledging the work of others might be new to some students, but it’s a really important part of demonstrating good academic practice at the University.
Students - we need to know and be able to trust in what can do. Your ideas, your abilities, your work. And we need to ensure that, as a University, we’re giving credit and respect to the work of others. Your assessments are designed to allow you demonstrate what you have learned in a module, and more widely, as a student in your subject. Passing your assessments yourself is your proof that you deserve your award.
Academic Misconduct = Cheating
The University is absolutely clear – if students present work which they have not produced as though it is their own, this is cheating and will be investigated as academic misconduct.
Students - no matter how stuck or under pressure you feel with your assessments, don’t be tempted to cheat. Although it might seem like the only option in the moment, it truly won’t help you – either in the short term in getting through your programme, or in the longer term when it comes to demonstrating your knowledge and skills to employers. It’s also not worth the risk – a major case of academic misconduct could result in you being withdrawn from your programme. This could significantly impact your education and future plans.
It is the responsibility of all students to ensure that they understand what is expected of them in their studies and assessments and to seek help when they need to. There are lots of resources available which can help – not only with your academic work, but also with any personal challenges you might be facing. See links to the University's support services within the Information for Students section.

--- SOURCE SEPARATOR ---

SOURCE_URL: https://unibradfordac.sharepoint.com/sites/student-casework-intranet/SitePages/Attendance-&-Engagement-Appeals.aspx

Student Casework
Attendance & Engagement Appeals
What is an attendance and engagement appeal?
An attendance and engagement appeal is a process that supports students to request a re consideration of a decision to either withdraw or suspend a student, made by an Attendance and Engagement Review Panel. This proecdure should be used by all students including those studying at UBIC and other Collaborative Partner Institutions.
Who can I get help from?
We strongly recommend that you contact the Students’ Union who can support you with your appeal. Their trained advisors can offer independent advice and can be contacted by emailing ubu-advice@bradford.ac.uk; or telephone 01274 233300.
The Student Casework Team can also provide support to help you to understand the procedure, but they cannot support you to submit your appeal. You can contact them by emailing complaintsandappeals@bradford.ac.uk or telephone 01274 235108.
When can I submit my appeal?
You will receive an outcome letter, informing you of the decision made by the Attendance and Engagement Review Panel. You can submit your appeal by completing and submitting the Attendance and Engagement Withdrawal / Suspension Appeal Form, within two weeks of receiving this letter.
What do I submit?
You need to complete a Attendance and Engagement Withdrawal / Suspension Appeal Form. The form will ask you to list the evidence that you are providing that supports your appeal. Once you have submitted the form, you will be sent an email, asking you to reply with your evidence.
Make sure you include all your evidence and documentation to support your case, such as a fit note or a letter from other healthcare professionals. Please see our Supporting Evidence Guide for what types of evidence is likely to be accepted.
If you are appealing on the basis of extenuating circumstances, as well as providing evidence to support the challenging circumstances you were experiencing, you will also need to evidence why you were not able to provide this information to the Attendance and Engagement Review Panel.
We cannot consider your form if you do not state the grounds and explain the reasons why you are appealing and you should try to complete all sections of the form so that the Investigating Officer can fully understand why you are appealing and what you are unhappy with.
How do I submit my appeal?
You will need to wait until you receive your outcome letter which confirms the decision made by the Attendance and Engagement Review Panel. Once you receive this letter you should submit an appeal within two weeks by completing this form - Attendance and Engagement Withdrawal / Suspension Appeal Form
What can I do if my appeal is late?
Late appeals will not be accepted without good reason. If the appeal is late, you must explain why and provide relevant evidence to support why your appeal is late, otherwise it could be rejected for being out of time. You should provide an explanation and evidence to support your reason for delay. For example, if you were ill during the timescale to submit an appeal.
What if I am studying on a student visa?
If you are studying on a student visa, you must declare this when completing the appeal form. Make sure you answer ‘yes’ to the question ‘Are you studying on a student visa?’. By doing so we will contact the Visa Support Team to request they place a hold on revoking your student visa until the appeal procedure is concluded.
If the appeal is not submitted within the two-week timeframe, the University will automatically inform UKVI. This means that if you are studying on a Student Visa and submit your appeal late, the University will have already notified UKVI that you have been withdrawn or suspended from the course.
What about attendance, progression or examination whilst my appeal is being considered?
As you are withdrawn or suspended from study, you are not permitted to attend classes, progress to the next stage of your course or take any examinations whilst your appeal is being considered.
What if I want someone else to handle my appeal for me?
Someone else is not permitted to handle your appeal for you (for example; parents, sponsors, or employers). However, if you have a mental health issue or disability which might impinge on your ability to make an appeal, a third party may be nominated to progress the appeal on your behalf.
You must confirm in writing that you authorise a third party to represent you by emailing complaintsandappeals@bradford.ac.uk and request a Third Party Consent Form.
What happens if my appeal is upheld (is successful)?
If your appeal is upheld (is successful) you will receive a completion of procedures letter, normally within one calendar month after we receive your appeal form. A copy will also be sent to relevant staff to inform them of the decision.
Possible outcomes may mean your case will be reconsidered (in light of the new information) by the Attendance and Engagement Review Panel or that you are permitted to rejoin your course.
What happens if my appeal is not upheld (is not successful)?
If your appeal is not upheld (not successful), you will receive a completion of procedures letter, normally within one calendar month after we receive your appeal form. A copy will also be sent to relevant staff to inform them of the decision. This means that the original decision made by the Attendance and Engagement Review Panel will remain in place.
There is no further internal procedure that you can follow, as this decision is final.
However, if you remain dissatisfied with the outcome of the appeal, you may a ble to apply for a review of the appeal decision, to the Office of the Independent Adjudicator(OIA), providing the appeal is eligible under their rules.
Normally, you can only escalate your case to the OIA if you have exhausted all the stages in this procedure and have received a completion of procedures letter.
Please note that you must raise a complaint with the OIA within 12 months of receiving the completion of procedures letter. We strongly recommend that you contact the Students’ Union who can support you if you wish to escalate your case to the OIA. Their trained advisors can offer independent advice and can be contacted by emailing ubu-advice@bradford.ac.uk or telephone 01274 233300.

SOURCE_URL: https://unibradfordac.sharepoint.com/sites/student-casework-intranet/SitePages/Academic-Appeals---Students.aspx

Student Casework
Academic Appeals - Students
What is an academic appeal?
An academic appeal is a process that supports students to challenge or request a reconsideration of a decision by the Board of Examiners or relevant Postgraduate Research Committee or Panel who make decisions on a student's progress, assessment and award. This may include a request to change marks, progression decisions, or final award classifications.
If you consider that you have relevant grounds to appeal, the first thing to do is to try to resolve your appeal informally before you submit a formal appeal. Taught students are encouraged to contact their Programme Leader and Postgraduate Researchers are encouraged to contact their Faculty Director of Postgraduate Research. This should be done in writing within one week from the date the official results or decision was received.
Who can I get help from?
We strongly recommend that you contact the Students’ Union who can support you with your appeal. Their trained advisors can offer independent advice and can be contacted by emailing ubu-advice@bradford.ac.uk; or telephone 01274 233300.
The Student Casework Team can also provide support to help you to understand the procedure, but they cannot support you to submit your appeal. You can contact them by emailing complaintsandappeals@bradford.ac.uk or telephone 01274 235108.
What if I am a UBIC student?
If you are a UBIC student you can follow this procedure. However, there are some minor differences. Initially, you should contact the internationalcollege@bradford.ac.uk to see if they can resolve this under Early Resolution (stage 1). If you remain unhappy following early resolution you can submt a formal (stage 1) appeal. Your appeal will be allocated to an Independent Investigating Officer at UBIC. If you submit a review stage appeal, this will be considered by an Independent Investigating Officer at the University.
When can I submit my appeal?
For most students you will need to wait until you receive your results after the Board of Examiners or relevant Postgraduate Research Committee or Panel has ratified (formally confirmed), a decision with regard to your progress, assessment or award.
If you are a taught student, you will need to wait until you have received your official results. You will recieve an email to inform you that your results are available on e:Vision. You can access your results by selecting the Modules / Marks tab and then clicking on Access your latest Progression / Award information (this is where you will find Exam Board decisions), on your e:Vision student account. Postgraduate researchers will be emailed their results. Once you receive your results you should submit an appeal within three weeks.
In exceptional circumstances, with the approval of the Associate Dean of Learning and Teaching (or nominee), students may appeal against a failed module or placement mark before the mark has been formally confirmed. You will still need to wait until after your results have been considered by the Assessment Committee, as this is a group of academics who meet to confirm your marks are accurate.
This is normally only available to international students whose results indicate they may be withdrawn and students on placement where that failure prevents them from progressing within their award.
Associate Dean of Learning and Teaching by Faculty
- Faculty of Engineering & Digital Technologies FEDT-AcademicMisconduct-Complaints@bradford.ac.uk
- Faculty of Health Studies FHS-BusinessSupport@bradford.ac.uk
- Faculty of Life Sciences folscomplaintsandappeals@bradford.ac.uk
- Faculty of Management, Law and Social Sciences fomlss-complaintsandappeals@bradford.ac.uk
What do I submit?
You need to complete a Formal Stage Appeal Form. Make sure you include all your evidence and documentation to support your case, such as a fit note or a letter from other healthcare professionals. Please see our Supporting Evidence Guide for what types of evidence is likely to be accepted. Your evidence should be current, independent, and covers the full period of time being requested. Please do not submit photographs of a sensitive or distressing nature, we do not accept this as a form of evidence.
We cannot consider your form if you do not state the grounds and explain the reasons why you are appealing and you should try to complete all sections of the form so that the Investigating Officer can fully understand why you are appealing and what you are unhappy with.
Make it clear on the form the grounds (or reasons) why you are appealing as you are not permitted to add additional grounds or add substantive points later in the process.
If you have witness statements, include them with your appeal submission as they will not be accepted at a later time.
How do I submit my appeal?
Submit your appeal to complaintsandappeals@bradford.ac.uk. You will need to wait until you receive your results after the Board of Examiners or relevant Postgraduate Research Committee or Panel has ratified a decision with regard to you progress, assessment or award. Once you receive your results you should submit an appeal within three weeks.
What can I do if my appeal is late?
Late appeals will not be accepted without good reason. If the appeal is late, you must explain why and provide relevant evidence to support why your appeal is late, otherwise it could be rejected for being out of time. You should provide an explanation and evidence to support your reason for delay. For example, if you were ill during the timescale to submit an appeal.
What if I am studying on a student visa?
If you are studying on a student visa and you are appealing a withdrawal or suspension decision, you must contact the Visa Support Team to inform them you have submitted an appeal (by emailing visasupport@bradford.ac.uk). If we accept your appeal for consideration, we will also contact the Visa Support team to request they place a hold on revoking your student visa until the appeal procedure is concluded.
What about attendance, progression or examination whilst my appeal is being considered?
In limited circumstances, and as long as you are not affected by professional, statutory or regulatory requirements, you may be able to to re-register for the next stage of your course. This would be on the understanding that if your appeal was not upheld (not successful) the original decision would stand and you would then be withdrawn or transferred from your course.
Be aware that you are not permitted to progress onto the next stage when you have not achieved enough credits to do so, and the outcome of the appeal could not remedy this. For example, some professional courses do not allow any referrals as all modules must be passed at each stage of study prior to starting the next stage or only permit up to 30 theory module credits to be trailed into the next stage.
In addition, a successful appeal cannot override the professional, statutory or regulatory requirements for the award. For example you may be permitted another attempt at an assessment but you would still not be permitted to progress onto the next stage of your course.
If you are a taught student and have an appeal pending consideration, you can undertake supplementary assessment, but this is at your own risk and only where such cases are not affected by professional, statutory or regulatory requirements.
Please discuss whether you are eligible to progress onto the next stage or undertake supplementary assessment, whilst your appeal is being considered, with your Personal Academic Tutor, Programme Leader or Supervisor, who will be able to advise you on the options which are available.
What happens if my appeal is upheld (is successful)?
If your appeal is upheld (is successful) you will receive a copy of the appeal outcome letter, normally within one calender month after we recieve your formal stage appeal. A copy will also be sent to relevant staff to inform them of the decision. Your results profile will be reconsidered by the Board of Examiners or relevant Postgraduate Research Committee or Panel, usually by Chair's Action. Your transcript and / or results will be updated and you will be informed of the progression or award decision.
You must contact your Personal Academic Tutor to ensure you have access to all relevant modules and the opportunity to resubmit these is arranged.
What happens if my appeal is not upheld (is not successful)?
If your appeal is not upheld (is not successful) you will receive a copy of the appeal outcome letter, normally within one calendar month after we receive your formal stage appeal. A copy will also be sent to relevant staff to inform them of the decision. This will mean that the original decision that has been made will remain.
If you are dissatisfied with the appeal outcome, you may request a review of the decision by completing a Review Stage Appeal Form. You should try to complete all sections of the form so that the Investigating Officer can fully understand why you are appealing and what you are unhappy with. You must state the grounds and explain the reasons why you are appealing.
You should include evidence or documentation to support your case. Please see our Supporting Evidence Guide for what types of evidence is likely to be accepted.
Submit your appeal to complaintsandappeals@bradford.ac.uk, within two weeks of receiving the formal stage appeal outcome letter.
Your appeal will be considered by a member of the Student Casework Team and the decision will be approved by a Pro Vice-Chancellor (or nominee). You will receive a copy of a completion of procedures letter, normally within one calendar month after we receive your review stage appeal. A copy will also be sent to relevant staff to inform them of the decision.
If your appeal is upheld (successful) your results profile will be reconsidered by the Board of Examiners or relevant Postgraduate Research Committee or Panel, usually by Chair's Action. Your transcript and / or results will be updated and you will be informed of the progression or award decision.
If your appeal is not upheld (not successful) please see the section below.
What if my appeal is still not upheld (not successful)?
If your review stage appeal is not upheld (not successful), there is no further internal procedure that you can follow, as this decision is final.
However, if you remain dissatisfied with the outcome of the appeal, you may a ble to apply for a review of the appeal decision, to the Office of the Independent Adjudicator (OIA), provided the appeal is eligible under their rules.
Normally, you can only escalate your case to the OIA if you have exhausted all the stages in this procedure and have received a completion of procedures letter.
Please note that you must raise a complaint with the OIA within 12 months of receiving the completion of procedures letter.
We strongly recommend that you contact the Students’ Union who can support you with your appeal and if you wish to escalate your case to the OIA. Their trained advisors can offer independent advice and can be contacted by emailing ubu-advice@bradford.ac.uk; or telephone 01274 233300.

Student Casework
Student Complaints
The information and policies accessible from this page relates solely to the conduct of student complaints.
Allegations of bullying, harassment or discrimination are managed under the University of Bradford’s Dignity and Respect Policy and Procedure
This page provides an overview of the complaints process. If you intend to make a complaint you are advised to read the procedures which provides more extensive information about the process.
Who can complain
Complaints can be accepted under the Student Complaints Procedure from all students currently registered on a programme of study delivered at the University of Bradford or the University of Bradford International College (UBIC).
Students who have recently graduated, or been suspended, excluded, withdrawn or expelled from a programme of study delivered at the University of Bradford can also submit a complaint. However this must be submitted within three months of the date of graduation, suspension, exclusion, withdrawal or expulsion.
If you are a UBIC student you can follow this procedure. However, there are some minor differences. Initially, you should contact the internationalcollege@bradford.ac.uk to see if they can resolve your complaint under Early Resolution. If you remain unhappy following early resolution you can submt a formal complaint. Your complaint will be allocated to an Independent Investigating Officer at UBIC. If you submit a review stage complaint, this will be considered by an Independent Investigating Officer at the University.
Students who are studying at other Collaborative Partner Institutions should follow the complaints procedure of the partner institution in the first instance. In the event that students studying at Collaborative Partner Institutions are unhappy with the outcome of this process they may seek a review of their complaint under this procedure.
What are the stages of the complaint procedure
The Student Complaints Procedure is divided into three parts:
- Early Resolution, which emphasises resolution at the ‘local’ point where the issue arose before it escalates into a formal complaint.
- The Formal Stage, in which the complaint will be formally investigated, and a formal response issued.
- The Review Stage, in which a formal review of the complaint is undertaken.
Who can I get help from in preparing my complaint submission?
The University recognises that involvement in the Student Complaints Procedure can be a difficult and a worrying experience. Support, assistance and advice on all aspects of the complaints procedure, including the preparation and submission of your complaint, is available to students from the Students’ Union Advice Service, you can email them in the first instance (ubu-advice@bradford.ac.uk) or telephone (01274) 233300. The University strongly encourages students to access this support.
Disabled students, including students with mental health issues, who, for impairment reasons, might experience difficulty in accessing any aspect of the Student Complaints Procedure, may seek assistance from the Disability Service (disabilities@bradford.ac.uk).
What is the early resolution stage?
The University aims to resolve most complaints informally. Therefore, in the first instance resolution should be sought from the Faculty or Professional Service Directorate in which complaint arose. Students are encouraged to raise complaints as soon as possible, and ideally within ten working days of the incident, last act or omission which is the cause of the complaint. However, the nature of such complaints can vary widely and therefore complaints at the Early Resolution Stage can be submitted within one calendar month of the incident, last act or omission which is the cause of the complaint.
Complaints at the Early Resolution Stage may be raised orally or in writing. The matter will usually be dealt with by discussion. It is intended that the matter be resolved at this point.
What happens if my complaint isn't resolved to my satisfaction?
If your complaint is not resolved to your satisfaction you may submit it to the University for consideration via the Formal Stage of the procedure. This should normally be done within one calendar month of the end of the Early Resolution Stage or within one calendar month of the incident, last act or omission which is the cause for complaint.
Your complaint should be submitted to the Student casework Manager via complaintsandappeals@bradford.ac.uk. Complaints should be submitted in writing and should detail the grounds for the complaint, together with a statement of what has been done by the student to seek Early Resolution, what the response or resolution at this stage was and why this response or resolution was deemed inadequate. Where the Early Resolution stage was not invoked, you should detail why the complaint was deemed to be of a nature which makes Early Resolution unsuitable. Any supporting evidence which you have to support the complaint should be appended. You should also clearly indicate the form of resolution or redress sought.
Late submission of complaints at the Formal Stage will only be accepted where in exceptional circumstances which are supported by documentary evidence which is made available to the University (for example, if you were too unwell to be able to submit within the required timescales).
The timescale for receiving an outcome on your complaint will depend on the complexity of the complaint under consideration; however you should normally expect a written outcome at the Formal Stage within one calendar month of the submission of the complaint. If it appears that this timescale will be exceeded you will be notified of the revised timescale.
You will receive a written response to your complaint which will outline the outcome of the Formal Stage complaint and any remedy or redress offered by the University.
What happens if my complaint is still not resolved to my satisfaction?
If the response to your complaint following completion of the Formal Stage of the procedure is still not acceptable to you, you may invoke the Review Stage of the Complaints Procedure. A request to invoke the Review Stage should be made in writing to the Student Casework Manager (via complaintsandappeals@bradford.ac.uk) within ten working days of the Formal Stage outcome letter. Requests submitted in writing. Before submitting a complaint at the Formal Stage, you are encouraged and advised to contact the Students’ Union Advice Service for independent advice and support (ubu-advice@bradford.ac.uk).
Late submission of requests for the Review Stage will only be accepted where in exceptional circumstances which are supported by documentary evidence which is made available to the University (for example, where a student was too unwell to be able to submit within the required timescales).
The request should explain why the outcome at the Formal Stage was considered unsatisfactory and should include copies of all documentation provided to you at the Formal Stage along with any other documentary evidence pertinent to the request. The request should also set out the grounds on which you are requesting review at the Review Stage. The grounds permissible are:
- That there was an irregularity in the operation of the procedure at the Formal Stage which resulted in disadvantage to the complainant.
- That, on the facts available at the Formal Stage, the decision and outcome were not reasonable.
- That new evidence has become available which had not previously been disclosed. Consideration of such evidence will only take place in exceptional circumstances and the complainant will be required to explain why such evidence could not be disclosed at an earlier stage of the complaint.
The timescale for a review will depend on the complexity of the complaint under consideration. However, you should normally expect a written outcome at the Review Stage within one calendar month of the submission of the request for the review. If this timescale will be exceeded, you will be notified of the revised timescale.
You will receive a written response outlining the outcome of the Review Stage complain and any remedy or redress offered by the University. You will also be issued a Completion of Procedures letter. This concludes the process within the University.
Next steps
If you are unhappy with the outcome of the Student Complaints Procedure, or a decision not to allow the Formal or Review Stage to proceed, you may seek to have this considered by the Office of the Independent Adjudicator for Higher Education. This is done through the Completion of a Scheme Application Form, which may be obtained from the Complaints Manager, or the OIA. The Scheme Application Form must include a copy of the Completion of Procedures letter and should be received by the OIA no later than 12 months after the date of this letter.

Student Casework
Student Conduct (Student Disciplinary and Fitness to Practise)
About the Student Disciplinary and Fitness to Practise Procedures
Student conduct at the University is regulated by Ordinance 16: Conduct of Student Members of the University. This ordinance includes a non-exhaustive list of the various behaviours that students may commit which are felt to be unacceptable and may result in disciplinary action being taken against the student or students involved. The Student Disciplinary Procedure describes how these matters will be resolved, and who will make the relevant decisions at each stage of the process. This procedure applies to all students of the University.
Additionally, students on courses which are regulated by Professional Statutory or Regulatory Bodies (PSRBs) are subject to Fitness to Practise requirements; the specific code of conduct to which these students must adhere varies depending on their course of studies, but all such cases are managed in accordance with the Student Fitness to Practise Procedure.
Both of these procedures are approved by the University's Senate, and are reviewed and updated regularly. The current version of each procedure was approved in February 2023.
This site is intended to explain these processes to colleagues and to students, and to share helpful resources which may be of use, whether you are supporting a student going through the disciplinary process, are interested in acting as an investigator, or going through a student disciplinary case yourself and want to get a better idea of what to expect.
Which process to use?
Taking a student through the Disciplinary or Fitness to Practise Procedure isn't always necessary or appropriate.
The table on the below sets out some of the different approaches that can be taken with regard to students whose behaviour may be causing concerns.
This table is intended to be a guide and is not an exhaustive list of things dealt with under these procedures. Additionally, every case is different, and though some cases may initially appear to be best dealt with under one procedure, upon looking at the details it can become apparent that another procedure is better suited.
Please note that the Fitness to Practise Procedure only applies to students registered on specific regulated programmes (e.g. nursing, social work, radiography.
Misconduct under Ordinance 16 may be dealt with under the Fitness to Practice Procedure and taken more seriously than would be the case for other students where the student is on a relevant programme and where it is felt appropriate to do so due to the nature of the misconduct.
Health, Wellbeing and Fitness to Study Procedure
- Students with difficult issues in their personal lives
- Students who cannot attend but can engage with their studies (e.g. due to temporary mobility problems)
- Where issues arise from a temporary mental health crisis or a longer-term mental health condition
- Where students have chronic health issues which impact on their studies
Fitness to Practise Procedure
- Where a student puts a patient safety at risk
- Where a student fails to recognise the impact of their behaviour
- Dishonesty or fraudulent behaviour
- Misuse of social media e.g. identifying patients in posts or photos, unprofessional behaviour
- Misuse of controlled drugs
- Other unprofessional behaviour
Normal PAT-Student pastoral support
- Addressing low level misbehaviour
- Providing advice relating to the expectation to behave in a professional manner
- Providing guidance following a failed placement
- Developing a Placement Support Agreement
-  Signposting support available elsewhere
Disciplinary Procedure
- Serious violence
- Sexual misconduct
- Bullying/harassment
- Potential reputational damage to the University
Typical outcomes
Each case has its own set of individual circumstances which will be taken into account when any decision regarding the outcome is made. This can make it difficult to give an idea of what a likely outcome will be for a particular ‘type’ of misconduct, assuming it's found that the relevant allegations are true. Such considerations would be similar in a Student Fitness to Practise case, but would of course be impacted by the standards of the profession and the accompanying variations between appropriate outcomes in these two procedures.

Language Centre
BASALT
The Language Centre offers a number of different entry routes for students whose first language is not English to prepare them for degree programmes .
The Bradford Academic Skills and Language Test (BASALT) is an in-house test that applicants to the University can use to provide evidence of their level of English. Successful achievement of the IELTS scores equivalents they need will allow applicants to meet their English Language conditions and progress directly onto their programme of study.
When and where BASALT can be taken?
The BASALT is administered on the University of Bradford campus throughout the year.It is suitable for undergraduate and postgraduate programmes, but for visa purposes it is not accepted for pre-degree programmes (such as a presessional English Language course or the Certificate of International Studies) for which UKVI-approved IELTS certificate is needed.
We can also offer the test in a number of test centres around the world.
What does the BASALT include?
The BASALT includes the following components:
- Part 1 Reading;
- Part 2 Listening;
- Part 3 Writing;
- Part 4 Speaking.

Language Centre
Academic English Language Support
Semester 1 Academic English support classes start 30th September 2024
- Mondays 12-1pm Academic Writing
- Tuesdays 12-1pm Academic Speaking
- Tuesdays 1-2pm Academic Vocabulary

The Language Centre offers a range of free support classes, and one-to-one and small group tutorials to help you get the most from your studies at the University of Bradford.
We can support you with all aspects of your academic English language skills.
Please contact thelanguagecentre@bradford.ac.uk with any queries.
Support classes
Weekly classes let you develop your academic English throughout semesters 1 and 2.
Classes take place Monday to Friday on campus at lunchtime.
Enrol on our Canvas page for further information.
Workshops
Weekly Workshops are available on Wednesday afternoons 1.00 - 3.00 PM on Teams.
Enrol on our Canvas page to access the Teams links.
We are offering additional workshops throughout the week to focus on the skills necessary at various points in the semester. Enrol on our page to get up-to-date information about all workshops and classes offered.
One-to-one appointments
We offer 30-minute one-to-one appointments with an English for Academic Purposes lecturer to work on any aspect of academic English language. Small group appointments can also be arranged on request.
Appointments are made available two weeks in advance, so keep checking the booking system.
These advice sessions give you the opportunity to obtain feedback and help on many areas of your academic study, including: 
- understanding how to meet the requirements of an assignment or class task
- checking your use of English tenses
- specific tips on improving how to plan/organise your writing /read academic texts
- how to improve your academic vocabulary
- preparing to deliver more confident oral presentations
- and much, much more!
Please remember that while we can offer advice, we do not proofread your work.

Language Centre
Presessional English Language Programme
Pre-sessional English Programme(PREP) is an entry route onto degree courses for students whose first language is not English.
The PREP is designed to support international students’ transition to UK HE by improving their English language ability, academic skills, and cultural understanding. Students may join the programme at a number of entry points throughout the year depending on their level of English. Students with conditional offers can attend the Pre-sessional and do not have to re-take IELTS. The final block is also suitable for international students who have achieved the required IELTS grade for their course but wish to work on their academic English.
The Language Centre can also provide bespoke courses to meet the needs of specific cohorts of students.
For more information contact: thelanguagecentre@bradford.ac.uk
Entry requirements and fees
There are three entry points throughout the year depending on students’ level of language: PREP 20 runs from April until September; PREP 10 from the beginning of July until September and PREP 6 from the end of July until September. Fees are very competitively priced, and include course materials. For more information please visit our website
Course content
Each course provides 20 hours of tuition each week from qualified and experienced teachers of English in groups of up to 16 students. Over the course students’ level of English and academic skills are developed with a focus on linguistic accuracy in the lower levels moving on to reading and writing a range of academic texts, participating in seminars, giving presentations and understanding lectures and taking notes.
Why study Presessional
- Students receive a thorough induction and orientation to life at UoB (see Presessional English Language Programme (6 week) Induction Schedule)
- Weekly peer-assisted learning sessions are integrated into the course to support the transition into UK HE.
- A varied social programme to support students’ cultural transition (see Presessional English Programme Summer Social Programme)
