Postgraduate Researchers
PGR Progression
For PhD students who started February 2022 onwards and their supervisors and Doctoral Thesis Advisory Panel members
This page provides key Information for Postgraduate Researchers (PGRs) on progression milestones throughout your doctoral study.
It also provides an overview of key stages to supervisors and the Doctoral Thesis Advisory Panel (DTAP).
The University of Bradford is committed to ensuring you progress throughout your doctoral studies and receive the support and expert guidance needed to be successful.
With this in mind, all new PGRs registered on PhD from February 2022 will follow supportive progression milestones to ensure you have the best experience during your doctoral studies.
In addition to your supervisory team, you will also be appointed a Doctoral Thesis Advisory Panel (DTAP). The DTAP will consist of 2 (internal) assessors, that will be identified at enrolment and should remain with you throughout the PhD wherever possible.
You will meet with your DTAP following submission of your first progression report, to ensure you have made a meaningful start to your PhD and discuss any initial concerns you may have.
The DTAP will then review successive progression reports and hold a formal review meeting with you to discuss progress.
The DTAP will review your progression report within 2 weeks of the submission deadline.
Further details of the progression milestones can be found below and also in the University Regulations for Research Degrees, found on the Regulation section of the University website
Full Time PhD PGR Journey
Throughout your doctoral study, you will be required to submit confirmation of your monthly supervision meeting to the PGRA Team. These form part of the overall picture of your progression and must be submitted monthly. In addition, you will be required to complete:
- 3-Month Progression
- 9-Month Progression
- 21-Month Progression
Faculty Research Conference before the end of your second year
- 33-Month Progression
If you do not submit by 36-months:
- 39-Month Progression, with quarterly progression submissions until thesis submission
Part Time PhD PGR Journey
Throughout your doctoral study, you will be required to submit confirmation of your bi-monthly supervision meeting to the PGRA Team. These form part of the overall picture of your progression and must be submitted bi-monthly. In addition, you will be required to complete:
- 6-Month Progression
- 18-Month Progression
- 42-Month Progression
Faculty Research Conference before the end of your fourth year
-  66-Month Progression
If you do not submit by 72-months:
- 75-Month Progression, with quarterly progression submissions until thesis submission
Doctoral Thesis Advisory Panel (DTAP)
The Doctoral Thesis Advisory Panel (DTAP) will consist of 2 (internal) assessors, who will be identified at enrolment and should remain throughout the doctoral journey wherever possible.
The DTAP will assess progression submissions.
The DTAP are approved by the DPGR. The panel should have the necessary methodological and/or topic expertise and be independent of the student’s supervisory team.
This means:
- No intellectual input throughout
- There will not normally be any joint publications
- No familial, sponsorship or other relationship
- The candidate may, however, know the DTAP – may be a member of the same research group, met at a conference etc.
-  The same principles should apply with regard to any potential relationship which might exist between either of the DTAP and the supervisor(s).
- Additional statements are not normally accepted to justify a potential conflict of interest due to a relationship between either of the DTAP, the supervisor(s) and the student
The PGRA Team will record the DTAP as part of the PGRs student record. Any changes to the DTAP, due to staff leaving or staff absence, must be communicated to the PGRA Team so the records can be kept up to date.
Submission of Progression Review Reports
Submission of Progression reports are made through CANVAS.
Similarity Checker: you can use the similarity checker to submit your draft progression reports multiple times to check their academic integrity using Turnitin. This work will not be assessed.
Final Submission: you will be given ONE opportunity to submit your final progression report for final assessment and it is your responsibility to ensure you submit the correct work.
Extension Requests for Progression Submissions
Extension requests to the submission deadline for all progression reviews are considered for a standard period of 1-month from the original expected submission date.
Extension requests must be submitted with the knowledge of your supervisory team.
You can only request one extension per progression review.
Extension requests must include supporting documentation, e.g. a medical note, copy of death certificate, counselling report/letter, police report etc.
Only in exceptional circumstances would an extension be considered after the submission deadline, and they will not be considered if received after 7 days of the submission deadline.
Extensions must be applied for using the Extension Form
If an extension to the submission date is approved, this will NOT also be applied to any future progression reviews that are due. It is expected that you work closely with your supervisory team to ensure any future progression reports are submitted on time.
Further extension requests to the same progression point will not normally be considered, unless there is evidence of new, unrelated extenuating circumstances.
Assessment of Progression Reports
Progression reports will be assessed by the Doctoral Thesis Advisory Panel. Your progression report will be reviewed, and the DTAP will meet with you to conduct an oral assessment of the submission.
Supervisor(s) are permitted to attend the progression meeting and may be invited by the DTAP to offer comments at any appropriate point during the oral assessment. The Supervisor(s) may request permission to clarify a particular point during the course of the assessment.
The PGR should be consulted by the DTAP prior to the oral assessment to confirm whether the Supervisor(s) should be present. If the PGR indicates that they would prefer the Supervisor(s) not to attend they will not do so. It is the responsibility of the DTAP to advise the Supervisor(s) accordingly.
Outcomes will be referred to the Faculty Postgraduate Research Progress Panel before confirmation of the outcome is formally released.
If the outcome of any progression review identifies that progress is not been made at the expected level, you will need to meet with the Faculty Postgraduate Research Progress Panel for further discussion and one of the following outcomes will be recommended:
1. Additional work required
2. PGR should be re-registered on Master of Philosophy
3. Recommendation of withdrawal
Outcomes of Progression Reviews 
3-month (Full Time) 6-month (Part Time)
- The project is on track – meets all expectations for this stage
- The project is on track, but some aspects need improvement for the next review
- Expectations not fully met in terms of progress or/and quality; a recovery plan has been agreed, to be reviewed at the 9-month/18-month review.
9-month (Full Time) 18-month (Part Time)
- The project is on track – meets all expectations for this stage
- Further work is required to meet expectations for this stage, to be represented to the Faculty Postgraduate Research Progress Panel for approval
- The PGR is required to re-register for MPhil with a revised workplan for submission
- The PGR is required to withdraw
21-month (Full Time) 42-month (Part Time)
- The project is on track – meets all expectations for this stage
- Further work is required to meet expectations for this stage, to be represented to the Faculty Postgraduate Research Progress Panel for approval
- The PGR is required to re-register for MPhil with a revised workplan for submission
- The PGR is required to withdraw
33-month (Full Time) 66-month (Part Time)
- The project is on track – meets all expectations for this stage
- Further work is required to meet expectations for this stage, to be represented to the Faculty Postgraduate Research Progress Panel for approval
- The PGR is required to re-register for MPhil with a revised workplan for submission
- The PGR is required to withdraw
- Confirmation at this millstone will confirm transfer to writing up at 36-months (Full Time) and 72-months (Part time).
39-month (Full Time) 75-month (Part Time)
- The project is on track – meets all expectations for this stage and will proceed to submission
- Further work is required to meet expectations for this stage, to be represented to the Faculty Postgraduate Research Progress Panel for approval
- The PGR is required to re-register for MPhil with a revised workplan for submission
- The PGR is required to withdraw
Faculty Postgraduate Research Progress Panel
The Faculty Postgraduate Progress Panel will consider all progression review outcomes.
If the outcome of any progression review identifies that progress is not been made at the expected level, you will need to meet with the Faculty Postgraduate Research Progress Panel for further discussion and one of the following outcomes will be recommended:
1. Additional work required
2. PGR should be re-registered on Master of Philosophy
3. Recommendation of withdrawal
You will be contacted following submission of your progression report should a meeting be required.
The Unsatisfactory Progress Procedure (UPP)
If at any stage during your research you aren’t making satisfactory progress, your faculty may initiate the Unsatisfactory Progress Procedure. This will involve setting objectives to help you progress with your research at the expected standard.
For PhD students who started October 2021 or earlier
This section provides key Information for Postgraduate Researchers (PGRs) on progression throughout your doctoral study. The information on this page applies to any PhD candidate registered prior to February 2022.
Below is a summary of the key points of the progression process.
In order to confirm your progression to PhD a recommendation must be made by the Dean (or their nominee) of the Faculty to the Research Degrees Committee:
- For full-time students this will be 12 months after initial registration.
- For part-time students this will be 24 months after initial registration.
Only in exceptional circumstances, is progression permitted before 12 months for full-time and 24 months for part-time.
The detailed arrangements for assessment prior to progression vary from one academic area to another. The following are minimum requirements set by the University:
- Students should submit a full written report on the research to-date and a detailed timetable for further work.
- The Supervisor is required to submit a full written report on the progress made by the student.
- The progression will be considered by a panel (Progression Review Panel) consisting of the Director of Postgraduate Research or their nominee and one or more members of academic staff.
- Reports from the student and the Supervisor are to be available two weeks prior to the meeting of the Progression Review Panel.
-  The student should be given the right to comment on any aspect of their Supervision in the absence of the Supervisors.
The progression viva must not go ahead until the panel/examiners are approved by the DPGR. The panel should have the necessary methodological and/or topic expertise and be independent of the student’s supervisory team.
This means:
- No intellectual input throughout
- There will not normally be any joint publications
-  No familial, sponsorship or other relationship
- The candidate may, however, know the examiners – may be a member of the same research group, met at a conference etc.
- The same principles should apply with regard to any potential relationship which might exist between either of the examiners and the supervisor(s).
- Additional statements are not normally accepted to justify a potential conflict of interest due to a relationship between either of the examiners, the supervisor(s) and the student
The panel should make one of the following recommendations:
- Candidate be permitted to progress to PhD.
- Further work is required by the candidate before progression is approved (to be approved by Chair's Action).
- Further work is required by the candidate before progression is approved. A further review will take place at a future time.
- Unsatisfactory Progress Procedure (previously Capability Procedure) to be initiated (either Preliminary or Formal).
Exceptionally, procedures could be instigated which could lead to the termination of the student's registration.
Further points to note are:
- One member of the panel is required to write a report on the review.
- A copy of the completed form should be given to the student (this is circulated to the student by the Faculty).
- Students must be informed of the decision of the Progression Review Panel.
- A student who is not permitted to progress registration may appeal against the decision.
Professional Doctorate - stage transfer
The requirements for the taught component of the Professional Doctorate (DBA and DPharm), including supplementary assessment, are set out in the Section 24 in the University's Regulations for Research Degrees (available on the Academic Quality Unit website.
Please also refer to the relevant Programme Specification for each Professional Doctorate particularly for the credit awarded to each module, found on the Learning, Teaching and Student Experience website.

Postgraduate Researchers
PGR Journey
Getting Started
This page will help you find key information, understand the structure of your degree and ensure you, and your supervisors, can help you settle in your new status is a postgraduate researcher (PGR).
Induction
Our induction events for Postgraduate Researchers (PGRs) are designed to: 
- ensure that you are well prepared for your research degree
- familiarise you with doctoral-level study and begin your expert skills development
- help you meet other researchers within your research area and across the University
Key information
- Compulsory induction events for all new PGRs can be found in the schedule - all induction events are compulsory unless otherwise stated. Details of all induction sessions can be found on the PGR induction website.
- There is no need to book, simply turn up (at the venue shown or at the virtual session linked) at the time shown.
- Contact the BRDF team if you have any queries about induction.
Your First Month
To guide your first month we have developed a first month checklist for PGRs and a complementary first month checklist for supervisors, this is available on the  Getting Started section. Work your way through this list during your first month to ensure you have read all the information and resources.
Progression
Progression on your research degree will be considered at various stages throughout your registration.
PGR Support Across the University
You may find that you need some additional support throughout your research degree, that your supervisory team or other immediate colleagues are unable to help with. Further details about the broad range of services available across the University can be found in the following support document.
Writing for Publication
Your Supervisors should discuss with you opportunities for you to disseminate your research and thus develop your communication skills. The transmission of research outputs and the development of personal transferable skills is strongly encouraged.
It is worth bearing in mind that academic journals typically have a long lead time from first submission of a paper to actually seeing it in print. It is therefore worthwhile aiming to submit a paper while your research is still in progress although others will no doubt follow on after completion.
Intellectual property rights of students
Use of prior work and published work in thesis
- PGRs are permitted to incorporate in their thesis a limited amount of unpublished work undertaken prior to registration for their research degree
- PGRs are permitted to incorporate their own published work in a thesis on condition that the fact is recorded in the thesis together with the title, place and date of publication
- PGRs are not permitted to incorporate in a thesis material which has been included in a thesis submitted for a degree at this or any other University or approved degree awarding body except for the purpose of drawing attention, for reference purposes only, to such material, including calculations or the results of experimental work. Where such material is incorporated the fact must be recorded together with the title of the thesis, the date of the award of the degree and the name of the university or other degree-awarding body making the award.
A Statement of Principles Relating to the IPR of Student Research was approved by Senate in June 2009 and can be found below.
This includes a section about restricted access of theses in the library and university repository.
This should be read in conjunction with the University Regulations relating to IPR in work produced by students.
The Unsatisfactory Progress Procedure (UPP)
If at any stage during your research you aren’t making satisfactory progress, your Faculty may initiate the Unsatisfactory Progress Procedure. This will involve setting objectives to help you progress with your research at the expected standard.
Annual Leave
Designated study periods for PGRs incorporate the full academic year, excluding bank holidays and University closure days. You are expected to make good progress with your research; however, it is important for your wellbeing that you take breaks from time to time too.
Full-time and Part-time students are allowed 27 days annual leave per year; this is linked to your academic year based on your intake. e.g. if you started 1 June 2024, your annual leave entitlement will run from the 1 June 2024 to the 31 May 2025. Any annual leave must be approved by your supervisor:
Annual leave requests must be submitted prior to annual leave taking place.
Unused annual leave cannot be carried forward to the next academic year.
Annual leave must not impact the PGRs ability to meet submission of formal assessments i.e. progression points or thesis submission.
Annual Leave Guidance and the annual leave request form can be found on the documents section in the Postgraduate Researchers site.
Absences
It is recognised that PGRs can suffer illness or other adverse personal circumstances which affect their ability to attend and engage with their programme of study.
The University must be notified of any absence period and this must be requested via the routes outlined in the PGR Annual Leave and Absence Guidance found on the documents section in the Postgraduate Researchers site.
Please use the absence request form to formally notify the University of absence.
Suspension
In normal circumstances the student should apply for their own suspension.
PGRs who wish to suspend from their programme of study should initiate the process by using the electronic form in e:Vision as soon as possible.
Upon receipt of your suspension request, the PGRA Team will contact you and your supervisors for relevant paperwork and supporting documents that must be provided to progress the suspension request with the Director of Postgraduate Research. The suspension request will then require formal approval at Faculty Postgraduate Research Sub-Committee.
Reasons for suspensions might include:
- illness;
- personal difficulties of a serious nature;
- maternity/ paternity/ adoption leave;
- bereavement.
Suspensions of studies must be applied for at the time the when the difficulties arise, not retrospectively. The exception to this is if you require a suspension following an emergency situation; in these instances we would expect a request to be submitted within 1-month. Fee liability charges are calculated from the date written confirmation of suspension is received at the University, or a later date if advance notification is provided. The University does not backdate suspensions. Non-attendance at the University does not constitute an approved period of suspension.
Only in exceptional circumstances may a Principal Supervisor apply on behalf of the student to the Faculty Postgraduate Research Sub-Committee for suspension.
Please also note that you will be expected to return at the end of your suspension period. You will have your enrolment launched and fees activated. It is therefore critical that you maintain contact with your Faculty and confirm your intentions.
Further guidance
If you are in receipt of sponsorship or any other external funding it is important that you notify such agencies of you suspension of study. Please include their approval with your University's suspension request.
Moreover, UKRI funded students must apply to the Research Council for permission to suspend their studies. It is essential that this is applied for at the time difficulties are experienced and not after the deadline for final thesis submission.
Student Registry Services (Research) are responsible for sending suspension requests to all Research Councils. Generally the support documents required for the suspension request are the same as listed above, although specific Research Council guidelines should be followed. Please email your Faculty Administrator for advice.
If you are studying with us on a Student Visa (previously Tier 4 Visa) we further advise that you contact the International Student Support and UKVI Compliance to discuss the implication of suspending your studies with them directly before applying to suspend your studies.
Extension
The PGR in consultation with their Principal Supervisor may apply to the Faculty Postgraduate Research Degrees Sub-Committee for an extension to the registration period.
Extension requests are expected show that some unforeseen circumstances have arisen which have adversely affected the PGRs progress, against which precautions were not possible.
Please note, where applicable, suspension of study should be applied for at the time difficulties arise which adversely affect the PGRs progress, these.
Extension requests should be submitted to the PGRA Team who will prepare the request for Faculty Postgraduate Research Degrees Sub-Committee. The extension request form can be found in the documents section in the Postgraduate Researchers site.
Following approval at Faculty level, all extension requests must also be approved by the Research Degrees Sub-Committee before a formal outcome of the extension request can be provided.
An extension is treated as a serious matter and should not be regarded as a matter of course.
An application for an extension must be made before the expiry of the PGRs normal period of registration. This is particularly important if the extension request is required to support a visa extension and Confirmation of Studies (CAS). It is strongly advised that the request to extend is submitted at least three months in advance of expiry of registration and should not be made retrospectively, i.e. after the registration end date has passed. For example if the final date of registration is 30 September, the extension request should be undertaken before the summer break.
The request needs to include the following information:
- a statement by the PGR giving the reasons why the extension is required and the proposed new expected date for submission;
- a thesis planner indicating the timetable of research for the requested extended submission date;
- a statement in support of the request for the extension to the submission date by the Principal Supervisor.
Any request to extend must contain all the above elements (statement by PGR, statement by supervisor and thesis planner) and the approval process will inevitably be delayed if full information is not provided. It is also important that the "end date", that is the final expected thesis submission date, is clear and consistent across all of extension request documents. Lastly it is essential that the planner is realistic and allows the Supervisor time to provide meaningful feedback on the thesis.
The request should be submitted electronically to thePGRA Team
On full receipt of the required information and approval by both Faculty Postgraduate Research Sub-Committee and Research Degrees Sub-Committee, PGRs will receive formal notification by letter from the Postgraduate Research Administration Team. If a new student card is required please pop to the Hub so a new one can be printed.
Further guidance
As above, if you are an international PGR who requires an extension of studies to support an extension of visa, it is essential that you begin the process well in advance of either your end date passing and/or visa expiring. Advice and guidance in extending your visa is given here:
UBU's Advice Centre and ubu-visa@bradford.ac.uk
You can use your approved extension documents to support your visa application but please note applying for visa extension is a separate approval process.
If you are in receipt of sponsorship or any other external funding it is important that you notify such agencies of your extension of study. Please include their approval with your university's extension request.
Moreover, UKRI funded PGRs who require extension of studies have to apply to the relevant Research Council for permission. It is essential that that this is applied for at the time difficulties are experienced and not after the deadline for final thesis submission.
Fees are payable during an approved extension period. The fees team will be able to advise the fees you will incur should you extend your registration.
Student Registry Services (Research) are responsible for sending extension requests to all Research Councils. Generally the support documents required for the extension request are the same as listed above, although specific Research Council guidelines should be followed.
Please email your Faculty Administrator for advice.
Writing up stage - completion of thesis and examination
Writing-up status refers to a period of time at the end of a PGRs registration when they have completed any experimentation and/or data collection.
All PGRs should aim to submit their thesis when their minimum period of registration expires. Therefore, the writing-up period should be viewed as a period to finalise the thesis for submission, undertake a viva and graduate.
More information about minimum and maximum periods of registration.
PhD Students
Full-time Students
- Full-time doctoral students normally transfer to writing-up status after their minimum period of registration (three years), if they do not submit their thesis.
- The writing-up period for Full-time doctoral students is usually one year only.
- For PGRs who joined February 2022 intake onwards; transfer to writing up will be confirmed as part of the 33-month progression review and applied at 36-months of study.
- For PGRs who started before February 2022, the transfer is done via a form (Transfer to Writing up) which can be found in the documents section in the Research Students SharePoint site. Once approved the form is sent to the Postgraduate Research Administration Team to change the mode of attendance code, unless, exceptionally, the Faculty/Department concerned indicates that a particular student is not ready for transfer to writing-up status.
Part-time Students
- Part-time doctoral students may apply for transfer to writing-up status after their minimum period of registration (four years), if they do not submit their thesis.
- For PGRs who joined February 2022 intake onwards; transfer to writing up will be confirmed as part of the 66-month progression review and applied at 72-months of study.
- For PGRs who started before February 2022, the transfer is done via a form (Transfer to Writing up) which can be found in the documents section in the Research Students SharePoint site.
- This transfer will only be approved if their Faculty/Department are able to confirm that they have completed the experimentation/data collection phase, are "writing-up" and will actually submit their thesis during this final stage of studies.
- It is common for part-time students to transfer only in year five or six of their programme.
- The writing-up period for Part-time doctoral students therefore varies, but is up to a maximum of three years.
For all PhD students who registered from February 2022 onwards, confirmation of your eligibility to transfer to writing up stage will be confirmed at the 33-month (66-month part-time) progression assessment. If you are a part-time PGR who is ready to transfer before 66-months, i.e. from 48-months onwards, please speak with your supervisor so the 66-month progression milestone can be brought forward.
MPhil Students
Full-time Students
- Ful-time MPhil students normally transfer to writing-up after their minimum registration period of registration (12-months), if they do not submit their thesis.
- The writing up period is a maximum of 12-months
- Writing up is confirmed via a transfer to writing up form completed by the supervisory team and PGR. This can be found in the documents section in the Research Students SharePoint site. The form is sent to the Postgraduate Research Administration Team to change the mode of attendance code, unless, exceptionally, the Faculty concerned indicates that a particular student is not ready for transfer to writing-up status.
Part-time Students
- Ful-time MPhil students normally transfer to writing-up after their minimum registration period of registration (24-months), if they do not submit their thesis.
- The writing up period is a maximum of 24-months
- Writing up is confirmed via a transfer to writing up form completed by the supervisory team and PGR. This can be found in the documents section in the Research Students SharePoint site. The form is sent to the Postgraduate Research Administration Team to change the mode of attendance code, unless, exceptionally, the Faculty concerned indicates that a particular student is not ready for transfer to writing-up status.
Please note: all students should submit in advance of their writing-up period ending, as full use of University Library, email and access to internet and networking facilities will be given during this time. The Faculty/Department must inform the student of their policies with regard to Faculty/Department-based facilities.

Career and Employability Services
Career and Employability Services
We offer a welcoming, supportive environment where you can access impartial careers information, advice and guidance from professionally qualified staff.
We are delighted to welcome students to our modern Careers space for face-to-face appointments. We also offer remote video and phone appointments, and our events workshops are a combination of on-campus and online activities. See more details on our appointments page.
Find links to our services, information and resources for current students, graduates, staff and businesses below, and please get in touch if you have any questions or comments.
See our About Us page for more details about our team and our statements of service.

SOURCE_URL: https://unibradfordac.sharepoint.com/sites/chaplaincy-services-intranet

Chaplaincy Services
CHAPLAINCY STATEMENT OF SERVICE
INTRODUCTION
The Chaplaincy is positioned within the Student Services area of Student Central, but its remit and its work extends beyond it; for example, the Chaplaincy is available for staff as well as students and it offers support to the different sections of the University community concerning University policies which involve religion and belief.
MISSION
In its scope and aims, the Chaplaincy encompasses both religious and non-religious worldviews. The Chaplaincy aims:
- to provide pastoral support to any member of the University, staff, or student.
- to develop ways in which the spiritual development and well-being of students and staff of all faiths and none may be promoted and supported.
- to advise the University on matters regarding spirituality, religion and belief which affect its life and work, maintaining good practice in relation to equality and diversity matters.
- to work closely with the Centre for Inclusion and Diversity (CfID) to promote the awareness of different religious and faith issues which impinge upon the teaching, learning and research of the University.
- to strengthen links between the University and faith groups within other local institutions and the wider local community.
WHAT WE OFFER
The Chaplaincy provides opportunities for spiritual and religious expression, development and nurture and opportunities to explore different worldviews.
Specifically, we provide:
- A team of chaplains and interfaith advisors, most of whom are volunteers, offering support and covering different faiths and traditions.
- A chaplain who is available at set times. On campus usually on Tuesdays, Wednesdays, and Thursdays.
- An emergency on-call service to the Co-ordinating Chaplain outside office hours, via the designated mobile phone number.
- Information about how to contact a key member of those faiths and traditions not covered by the team.
- Confidential pastoral support and consultation.
- Opportunities for discussion and reflection about spirituality, beliefs, ethics, and life events.
- Occasional and seasonal meditations and worship which relate to the University context.
- Up-to-date contact information about local spiritual resources and places of worship: churches, mosques, and temples, etc.
- Co-ordination of the Staff Forum (via Teams) for Faith Spirituality and Belief.
- Information about beliefs and customs of the major religions and worldviews, focused especially upon how they affect people interacting in a university setting;
- Regular weekly drop-in opportunities to join the FAB club (Faith and Belief) for informal support and to discover more about UBU religious student societies.
- Provision of Weekly Communion Service with the Fountains Church team.
LEVELS OF SERVICE:
You are entitled to expect that the Chaplaincy will:
- serve students and staff of all faiths and none.
- provide confidential pastoral support in accordance with established understandings of confidentiality. If we are not able to provide suitable support, we will suggest others who would be able to provide the required support.
- respond adequately to questions concerning beliefs, spirituality, and ethics.
- provide opportunities to celebrate Faith events, discussions and other worldview events which are of a high quality, and which are accessible and inclusive for all.
- foster mutual respect and understanding between different belief systems.
EQUAL OPPORTUNITIES
The Chaplaincy is committed to valuing diversity and does this specifically by promoting respect for all different faiths, traditions, and beliefs, and serving staff and students equally, regardless of race, religion, gender, disability, marital status, social class, age, or sexual preference. All forms of unlawful direct or indirect discrimination or unequal treatment or unethical behaviour will be challenged. The Faith Spacse are designated as Safe Space for all.

Postgraduate Researchers
PGR Connect
PGR Connect is a PGR-led peer support scheme that aims to offer a support network for our postgraduate researchers. The scheme strives to foster a sense of belonging and community, help PGRs adjust to or progress in their studies, and mitigate the sense of isolation that can sometimes develop due to the unique way postgraduate researchers study and work.
For further information please see the PGR Connect site.

SOURCE_URL: https://unibradfordac.sharepoint.com/sites/it-services-intranet/SitePages/teams-for-students.aspx

IT Services
Microsoft Teams for students
Microsoft Teams is part of the University's Microsoft 365 suite - along with Outlook for email, OneDrive for file storage, and other sevices.
You can use Teams to collaborate with your fellow students. Your lecturer may also use Microsoft Teams to deliver online teaching sessions, or to facilitate group work.
How can I access Microsoft Teams?
On a computer or laptop
You can simply use the Teams web app in your browser by going to https://teams.microsoft.com - however, to get the fullest benefit of teams, we recommend installing the Teams desktop app. To install the desktop app:
1. In a browser, go to: https://office.com/apps 
2. Sign in with your University username with '@bradford.ac.uk' appended - for example, jrblogg2@bradford.ac.uk - and your University password.
3. Select Teams from the list of apps displayed.
4. Go to the three dots at the top right, and then select the Download the desktop app
When join a Teams meeting - by selecting the link in the email invite - you will have the option to open the meeting in the Teams app, or alternatively within a browser window. If you choose to open the meeting in a browser window, make sure you select 'Allow' when your browser prompts you for access to your mic and camera (you'll then be able to turn these on or off as you wish in the actual meeting, by using the meeting control buttons).
On a mobile or tablet
If you want to join Teams meetings from your smartphone or tablet, install the Teams app on your device by following these steps:
1. Search your device's app store for Microsoft Teams and install the app.
2. Open the Teams app on your device.
3. Sign in with your University username with '@bradford.ac.uk' appended - for example, jrblogg2@bradford.ac.uk - and your University password.
What devices do I need to use with Microsoft Teams?
- You’ll need a microphone to be able to speak in calls and meetings. Use headphones with a microphone if you can. Remember, it's good practise to keep your microphone muted until you need to speak, so that it doesn’t pick up sounds around you.
- To listen, use your headphones - or your device’s speaker if you don’t have headphones.
- To share your video, you’ll need a webcam, or a camera that’s integrated into your device.
Set your devices to work with Microsoft Teams by going to the Settings at the top right and then Devices
If you experience a delay with your video or audio during meetings, this will most likely be due to poor network connection. Turning off your camera can help to improve bandwidth. It’s also best to use a wired connection where possible, rather than WiFi.
Teams meeting control bar functions
Depending on the permissions set by the meeting organiser, you can use the buttons on the meeting control bar to:
- Share or stop sharing your video.
- Mute or unmute your microphone.
- Share your desktop or individual windows.
- Raise your hand to indicate you have something to contribute.
- Chat with participants.
- See a list of participants.
- Leave the meeting.
- Access more advanced features.
What else can I use Microsoft Teams for?
Teams is a platform for communicating and collaborating with other people - both internal to the University, and external. Using Teams you can:
- Chat: One-to-one, or in a group.
- Collaborate within teams and channels: A ‘team’ is a space you can set up to enable a group of people to work together. ‘Channels’ are spaces you can create within a team to organise discussions and content into themes. Within each channel you can collaborate on files, chat, and hold online meetings.
Important: When you create a team, ensure you set it as Private. ‘Private’ means that only you, and the people you add as members (this includes any external people you add), can see the content and chat in the team - whereas ‘Public’ means that staff and students in the University would be able to join the team without requesting permission and would be able to view and edit your content.
- Share and collaborate on files: Files can be shared either within channels, or within the chat feature, and Microsoft files (Word, PowerPoint etc) can be edited by multiple people at the same time.
- Make calls: Call internal members of the University by finding them in the search bar in Teams and then selecting the video or audio call option.
- Have online meetings: You can schedule a meeting with other people via the Calendar button in Teams. And if you’re invited to a Teams meeting, just select the Join Microsoft Teams Meeting link in the invite you've received when it's time to join.

IT Services
Printing, scanning and photocopying
Important
When using our services, you must abide by the IT Services Acceptable Use Policy
Printing credit allowance for students (and check your balance)
Student printing is free (but not unlimited) and is capped at £20 per year starting from 1 September.
This facility is strictly for academic purposes only and must not be used for printing non-academic studies related content.
To help you to keep track of your £20 allowance, here are the costs applied for printing, per sheet:
- A4: Colour 8p, Greyscale 3p, Colour duplex 12p, Greyscale duplex 5p
- A3: Colour 15p, Greyscale 6p, Colour duplex 27p, Greyscale duplex 10p
To stay within your £20 limit, and to save on paper, please be mindful and only print what you need. The table below shows how costs can add up, depending on the number of pages you print:
- 1 page (A4): Colour 8p, Greyscale 3p, Colour duplex 12p, Greyscale duplex 5p
- 5 pages (A4): Colour 40p, Greyscale 15p, Colour duplex 60p, Greyscale duplex 25p
- 10 pages (A4): Colour 80p, Greyscale 30p, Colour duplex £1.20, Greyscale duplex 50p
- 25 pages (A4): Colour £2, Greyscale 75p, Colour duplex £3, Greyscale duplex £1.25
- 50 pages (A4): Colour £4, Greyscale £1.50, Colour duplex £6, Greyscale duplex £2.50
- 100 pages (A4): Colour £8, Greyscale £3, Colour duplex £12, Greyscale duplex £5
- 250 pages (A4): Colour £20, Greyscale £7.50, Colour duplex £30, Greyscale duplex £12.50
- 1 page (A3): Colour 15p, Greyscale 6p, Colour duplex 28p, Greyscale duplex 10p
- 5 pages (A3): Colour 75p, Greyscale 30p, Colour duplex £1.36, Greyscale duplex 50p
- 10 pages (A3): Colour £1.50, Greyscale 60p, Colour duplex £2.70, Greyscale duplex £1
- 25 pages (A3): Colour £3.75, Greyscale £1.50, Colour duplex £6.76, Greyscale duplex £2.50
- 50 pages (A3): Colour £7.50, Greyscale £3, Colour duplex £15.50, Greyscale duplex £5
- 100 pages (A3): Colour £15, Greyscale £6, Colour duplex £27, Greyscale duplex £10
- 250 pages (A3): Colour £37.50, Greyscale £15, Colour duplex £67.50, Greyscale duplex £25
How can I check my printing credit balance?
To check your printing credit balance, go to: https://papercut.bradford.ac.uk:9192 and sign in with your University username and password. Note: You need to be on campus with your device connected to eduroam (campus WiFi) to access this link.
What if I reach my printing credit limit?
You’ll see a message that you have no more credit available when you try to print. In exceptional circumstances only, a small amount of credit may be additionally applied to your account as a one-off; however, this is at the discretion of IT Services. Please ask at the IT Bar in the Library.
Note: If you have a Learner Support Profile (LSP) that supports the need for increased printing to help with your studies, please contact the Disability Service to discuss arrangements for permanent adjustments to your printing credit.
Print from a University Computer
1. Within the document you want to print, select File - Print (or press Ctrl+P on your keyboard).
2. Select the PaperCut print queue.
3. If you want to change printing preferences (such as Colour or Duplex modes), select Printer Properties and make any desired changes.
4. Select Print when you’re ready.
5. Releast the print at the printer
Release your printing at a printer
1. Go to a printer and hold your University card against the card reader beneath the control panel display. If you’re asked to sign in at this point, enter your University username with @bradford.ac.uk appended (for example jrblogg2@bradford.ac.uk) and your University password.
2. Press Print Release on the home screen.
3. Select what you want to print from the list displayed by pressing on the check box next to the specific printing, and then press Print or Print All.
4. Sign out by pressing the Home icon at the left of the screen, and then Sign Out at the top of the screen.
Print wirelessly
General notes:
- You can print files from a mobile device to be printed at the printer within 24 hours.
- Documents will be printed in their original format (that is, documents in colour will print in colour, and multi-page documents will print double-sided). If you require any formatting or other changes, you will need to save the document in one of the following supported formats before sending to print: Microsoft Office, PDF, JPG, GIF, PNG, BMP, TXT.
- You can modify a copy of your document for the purposes of printing to receive your copies in your preferred format.
Android

  c. Sign in with your University username with @bradford.ac.uk appended and your University password.
  d. Select Android Set-up and then follow the instructions to download and set up wireless printing on your device.
2. To print:
  a. Open the app from which you want to print a document.
  b. Select the menu (three dots) in the top right corner and then select Share.
  c. Select Print, select the print driver you’d like the print to go to, and then select Print.
  d. Release the print at the printer.

  d. Select iOS Set-up and then select Download.

  f. Select Close when download has finished.
  g. Select the iOS Settings icon to open settings.
  h. Select General > VPN & Device Management.
  i. Select PaperCut Mobility print for iOS under the downloaded profile header.
  j. Select Install and then enter your passcode to continue.
  k. Select Install and then Done to finish the installation.
2. To print:
  a.Open the app from which you want to print a document.
  b. Select the Share icon at the bottom of the document you want to print.
  c. Select the Print icon and select the print driver you’d like the print to go to.
  d. Select any options you require, and then select Print.
  e. Release the print at the printer (see the ‘Release your printing at a printer' section above).

Mac

Chromebook

Print on your own paper
Note: Printing defaults to black and white (monochrome), and double sided.
1. In most applications, choose File - Print.
2. Select the PaperCut print queue.
3. Select Properties and then select the Printing Shortcuts tab.
4. From the 'Paper source' drop down list, select Tray 1.
5. Make any other changes as required.
6. Select OK and then Print.
Note: After 24 hours, any document sent to print, but not released (as per the steps below), will be automatically deleted.
When you're ready to print:
1. Open Tray 1 on the right-hand side of the printer.
2. Place your paper on the tray and adjust the paper guides to fit as necessary.
3. Hold your University card against the card reader to the left of the screen on the printer.
4. Press Print on the home screen.
5. Select what you want to print from the list displayed by pressing the check box next to the specific print job, and then press Print or Print All.
6. Sign out by pressing the Home icon at the left of the screen and then Sign Out at the top of the screen.
Scan to email
1. Place the copy on the glass plate and close the lid (multiple loose sheets can be placed face-up on the document feeder).
2. Hold your University card against the card reader beneath the control panel display.
3. Press Scan on the home screen and then select the Scan To My Email option. The email will be sent to your University email account only which is pre-populated and cannot be changed. You can change the email subject and document name (which defaults to 'Scanned document').
4. The default setting for the document will be single sided, PDF, with a small file size and 200dpi resolution. To change these default settings, press Options.
5. Press the Send button to begin scanning.
6. Sign out by pressing the Home icon at the left of the screen, and then Sign Out at the top of the screen.
Scan to OneDrive
Note:
- On your first scan attempt, you’ll need to sign into your OneDrive to grant access to your OneDrive. You’ll receive an email with a link to sign in. In the email, select the Login to OneDrive for Business link and sign in using your University username with @bradford.ac.uk appended (for example jrblogg2@bradford.ac.uk) and your University password.
- In the ‘Permissions request’ dialogue box that appears, select Consent on behalf of your organisation - Accept. You’ll then see a Fantastic! You did it! box appear.
1. Place the original on the glass plate and close the lid (multiple loose sheets can be placed face up on the document feeder).
2. Hold your University card against the card reader beneath the control panel display.
3. Press Scan on the home screen and then select the Scan To My OneDrive option. You can change the document name if needed. The default setting for the document will be single sided, PDF, with a small file size and 200dpi resolution. To change the defaults press Options.
4. Press the Send button to begin scanning.
5. Sign out by pressing the Home icon at the left of the screen, and then Sign Out at the top of the screen.
Your scan will now be placed in the /Apps/Scans for Papercut MF folder on your OneDrive. After every subsequent scan to OneDrive, you’ll receive an email notification with a link to the scanned file.
Photocopy
1. Place the original on the glass plate and close the lid (multiple loose sheets can be placed face up on the document feeder).
2. Hold your University card against the card reader beneath the control panel display.
3. Press Copy on the screen.
4. Select from the options on the screen to make any changes required (for example, colour, double sided or size). Note: Photocopying defaults to black and white (monochrome). For colour, press the Color / Black option, select Color and then press OK.
5. Select the number of copies required at the bottom right of the screen.
6. If you wish to see what your document will look like, press the Touch to scan and preview area on the screen. After scanning, an image of your document will be displayed in the preview area.
7. Press Copy.
8. Sign out by pressing the Home icon at left of the screen. Then press Sign Out at the top of the screen.

SOURCE_URL: https://unibradfordac.sharepoint.com/sites/it-services-intranet/SitePages/wifi.aspx

IT Services
eduroam (WiFi)
Important policies
You must abide by the IT Services Acceptable Use Policy, the Mobile Device Policy and Terms of Use and the JANET eduroam Policy
Connecting to eduroam on campus
You only need to connect to eduroam once. Subsequently, you'll be able to connect on campus without authentication. If your device defaults to a different network, you can ensure your device connects to eduroam by going to your WiFi settings, selecting the network that you don't wish to connect to in future and choosin Forget this network
Upon completion of your studies or employment at the University, you must remove the eduroam profile from your devices.
Does your visitor need WiFi access?
If you have a visitor who needs WiFi access, please see the details on the eduroam Visitor Access page.
Connecting to eduroam at another institution
The JANET web site displays a list of UK institutions where eduroam is available
If you visit another site, you should connect automatically, however you may need to change the configuration of your device if the site uses an older encryption version. Please check the website of the institution you are visiting. University of Bradford uses the encryption scheme WPA2 and AES, however some sites use WPA and TKIP.
A 'home' site is the institution where you are registered. A 'visitor' site is the institution you visit.
Notes
- The wireless network (SSID) address to connect to at Bradford is eduroam
- Bradford operates a JANET Roaming Service Tier 2 visited service.
- The eduroam service at Bradford does not use any application or interception proxies.
- Further information about eduroam and the JANET Roaming Service (eduroam within the UK) is available from the eduroam federation’s website and the JISC website

SOURCE_URL: https://unibradfordac.sharepoint.com/sites/it-services-intranet/SitePages/usernames-and-passwords.aspx

IT Services
Your IT account
You must abide by the IT Services Acceptable Use Policy and the Password Policy.
How to sign into the University's online services
- Canvas, Computers in cluster rooms, e:Vision, eduroam (WiFi), Email, Horizon, Library Record, Microsoft 365 services, PebblePad, ServiceNow, University Portal, University intranet
  - Sign in with your University username with @bradford.ac.uk (e.g., jrblogg2@bradford.ac.uk) and your University password.
  - Note: Your email has dots between initials and surname (e.g., j.r.bloggs2@bradford.ac.uk).
- Library eResources
  - Sign in using only your University username (e.g., jrblogg2) and University password.
Manage your IT account
Go to https://myaccount.microsoft.com at any time, where you can:
- Change your password (see the next section below).
- View your recent sign-in activity, where you can check for unusual activity to see if someone is trying to guess your password, if an attacker successfully signed in to your account, and from what location.
- Add or update your multi factor authentication methods - used for verifying your sign-ins and resetting your password if you forget it.
- Manage the devices which are connected to your University account and disable them if they are lost or stolen.
Tip: If you are already signed in to a Microsoft 365 service, for example, Outlook on the Web or OneDrive, you can get to this page quickly by selecting your profile at the top right of the screen and choosing View account
Password advice
In addition to the guidance below, please see section 3 of the Password Policy
Dos:
- Change your password frequently.
- Only include letters and numbers (no special characters, like exclamation marks)
- Make it at least 12 characters (there must be at least one uppercase character, lowercase character, and number in the first eight characters).
- Passphrases, including groups of random words are recommended as good strong password choices.
- Don’t write it down.
- If you think someone knows your password, change it immediately and report it
- Understand that the University will never ask you to confirm your password by email or text. Emails and texts requesting these details are phishing attempts from people who are trying to steal your account details. Delete them without responding.
Don'ts:
- Never give your password to anyone (even IT Services staff).
- Don't use your name, username, or any other easily obtained information in any form. For example, 'jrbloggs' cannot have the password 'jrbloggs' or 'jrBloggsisAce'.
- Don't include non-alphanumeric characters (for example, question marks or exclamation marks).
- Don't use spaces or non-English characters.
- Don't use the same password that you use for personal accounts.
- Don't use your University username and password to sign into websites or services you don't recognise.
- Don't sign into sites if they don't display the padlock symbol next to the web address field.
- Don't leave a signed in computer unattended - and make sure you sign out properly when you are finished.
- How to set a new password
How to set a new password
If you know your password and just want to change it, go to https://myaccount.microsoft.com and select Change Password
If you ever forget your password you can reset it by selecting the 'Forgotten your password?' link that can be found on the University sign-in page, or by going to https://passwordreset.microsoftonline.com. You'll then need to follow this process on the screen:
- Enter your University username with @bradford.ac.uk appended (for example: jrblogg2@bradford.ac.uk), then enter the validation characters displayed on the screen, and select Next
- Select how you want to receive your verification code from the list that will be displayed.
- Enter the verification code that will be sent by whichever method you chose above, and select Next
- Enter a new password, and enter it again to confirm it.
- Select Finish. You should see a confirmation message on the screen
Note: If you have configured and connected any devices for accessing your University email or the campus WiFi (eduroam), once you've set a new password you’ll also need to go into the settings on the device and update your password there.
If you see a message that says you are not registered for self-service password reset (SSPR), please call IT Services on 01274 233333

IT Services
Software
- **Adobe Express**

- **Assistive and productivity software**
  - Assistive software is available that can aid productivity and help with different kinds of needs.

Leaving the University
This information applies to you if your University status has been marked as 'Completed' and you've received an email telling you that your IT account will close.

Important note about Horizon: Your access to Horizon - which hosts specialist software applications and desktop versions of Microsoft Office - is discontinued from the date you receive the email. However, please note that you can still access the web versions of Word, Excel, PowerPoint, and OneDrive on campus cluster computers throughout the 90 day period - just sign into the computer, open Edge (web browser) from the Start menu, and navigate to www.microsoft365.com
Your email, files, and software
Let the people you want to remain in contact with know an alternative email address, or how else they can contact you, after you've left (you may wish to consider adding an automatic reply on your University email account for the remainder of the time it's still open).
Please review the files you've saved to your University OneDrive (and your M: drive, if you've ever used this), and copy any that you need to keep elsewhere.
If you've downloaded any software for home or personal use, please check to see if your subscription will end, and copy any files you need to keep.

Please return all your Library books and pay any outstanding fines.
Once your Library account is clear you are eligible for reference and study use of the Library, and you can borrow up to four books at a time (a fee of £35 per year applies for borrowing).

Career and Employability Services
Summer Experience
About the programme
Summer Experience is our internship programme working with local businesses and organisations. Applications are accepted from undergraduate students from the University of Bradford who are in their first or second year of a three-year course or penultimate year of a four-year course.
You will be paid a training allowance of £448.70 per week (subject to National Insurance and Tax reductions).
The internships can take place anytime from late May (after the exam period) to September and will be six weeks full-time work (or part-time equivalent).
This is a great opportunity to gain meaningful work experience that will enhance your CV.
Who is Summer Experience for?
Any current first or second year undergraduate student of the University of Bradford (you must be returning for at least one more academic year).
You must be eligible to work full time in the UK during the vacation period. If you are an international student, please check your Visa status for working during vacations, for most students studying a typical 3 or 4 year undergraduate programme you are likely to have eligibility. If you are unsure about your eligibility to work, please seek advice from the International Student Adviser and check the UK Council for International Student Affairs website.
We welcome applications from disabled students/students with dyslexia to help provide valuable work experience.
If you have any questions about your eligibility to apply for these projects please get in touch via email: summerexperience@bradford.ac.uk
How does it work?
Summer Experience projects are full-time placements based within local and regional businesses, charities and organisations, which typically start at the end of May or into June or July. There is a minimum requirement to work six weeks but there may be an opportunity to work longer.
In addition to providing you with an excellent opportunity to gain valuable work experience within a local business or enterprise, the summer experience scheme also provides you with advice and support, before, during and after your placement.
At the end of your placement, all students take part in a celebration and awards ceremony. This is an opportunity for us to thank all the participants in the Summer Experience programme. It’s a great opportunity to bring together everyone involved and celebrate the successes of the programme.
Live Vacancies
Check out all the opportunities on Handshake Career Centre
Follow the University of Bradford to be notified of all new vacancies.
You can also register your interest for upcoming vacancies now by emailing your CV to summerexperience@bradford.ac.uk
How do I apply?
1. Summer Experience placements will be advertised this page and via the Handshake Career Centre. Please be aware that we will continue to add opportunities as and when they become available so keep checking back to make sure you are aware of all the placements on offer.
2. Once you have selected a project you would like to apply for, you will be asked to submit a CV and cover letter to summerexperience@bradford.ac.uk clearly stating which position you are applying for and why. There will be a deadline indicated on the job advert (you can apply for as many Summer Experience opportunities as you like).
3. To ensure you have the best chance of securing an interview for the placement, please make sure that you seek support and help from Career and Employability Services before submitting your application.
4. All applications will be received by the Summer Experience team and forwarded to the host organisation. The organisation will then get in touch with you should you be shortlisted for interview.
Payment
All of the projects advertised require you to work a total of 210 hours which you will be paid £448.70 per week. Subject to National Insurance and Tax reductions, this means that even if you work part-time you will still work the same number of hours as a full-time project.
During the internship
You will be visited by a member of the Summer Experience team during your internship to ensure the project is running smoothly and we are on hand by telephone or email should any problems or queries arise.
You will be required to complete a self reflective workbook about your placement that details what you have been involved with, what you have learnt - skills, knowledge etc and how this placement may help your future career development plans.
What types of projects have been run previously?
Previously students have worked on a variety of projects, including:
- Market research
- Business Analysis
- Graphic Design
- Computer Programming
- Multi media
- New Product Development
- Process re-engineering
- ...and more
