SOURCE_URL: https://unibradfordac.sharepoint.com/sites/it-services-intranet/SitePages/Assistive-and-productivity-software.aspx

IT Services
Assistive and productivity software
Assistive software is available that can aid productivity and help with different kinds of needs.
The software mentioned in the categories below is either licenced by the University for use or is open source and freely available.
Reading and writing, dictation, and text-to-speech
Assistive Software Tools:
- **TextHelp Read & Write:**
  - What it does: Assists with reading or composing text. Reads documents out loud. Text prediction. Screen masking. Summary highlighters.
  - How to access:  
    - Staff: Available to install from the Software Centre (in the Start menu) on staff devices.  
    - Students and staff: Available in the A–Z list of applications within the Horizon service.
- **Editor:**
  - What it does: Checks grammar, spelling, tone, and more.
  - How to access: Select the Editor button on the Home tab in the Microsoft Office application you are using (for example, Outlook or Word).
- **Immersive Reader:**
  - What it does: Shows text in a window where you can have it read aloud or formatted to enhance reading (such as words divided into syllables), color-coded for parts of speech, or with extra spacing between words and lines.
  - How to access: In most Microsoft applications (for example, Word), select the View tab and then select Immersive Reader.  
    See how to enter into Immersive Reader mode in the Edge browser.
- **Read Aloud:**
  - What it does: Reads content aloud to improve reading comprehension by hearing content at your own pace.
  - How to access: In most Microsoft applications, select the Review tab and then select Read Aloud.  
    See how to enter into Read Aloud mode in the Edge browser.
- **Speak (text-to-speech):**
  - What it does: Reads text aloud in Word, Outlook, PowerPoint, and OneNote.
  - How to access: Add the Speak command to your Quick Access Toolbar in Word, Outlook, PowerPoint, and OneNote.
- **Dictate:**
  - What it does: Lets you use your voice to author content in Microsoft Office apps instead of typing.
  - How to access: Access this feature in Word, Outlook, PowerPoint, and OneNote.
- **Narrator (Windows):**
  - What it does: A screen reader app that reads dialog boxes, buttons, user interfaces, and text.
  - How to access: See the complete guide to Narrator.
Transcription and captions
Assistive Transcription Tools:
- **Transcriptions in Teams meetings:**
  - What it does: Live transcription captures everything being said in a Teams meeting, in real time, and includes each speaker's name and timestamp. The transcript file is also available to view in the meeting recap after the meeting has ended.
  - How to access: Enable live transcription in a Teams meeting using the option on the meeting control bar in Teams.
- **Captions in Teams meetings:**
  - What it does: Live captions are displayed in real time as text in the meeting window.
  - How to access: Enable captions in a Teams meeting using the option on the meeting control bar in Teams.
- **Transcribe in Word:**
  - What it does: Converts speech to a text transcript with each speaker individually separated. Parts of a recording can be revisited after the conversation or meeting by playing back the timestamped audio. The transcription can be edited to make corrections. The full transcript can be saved as a Word document or snippets of it can be inserted into existing documents.
  - How to access: You can transcribe speech by recording directly in Word, or by uploading an audio file.
Mind mapping, ideation, note-taking, planning, and organisation
Assistive Productivity Tools:
- **MindGenius:**
  - What it does: Helps to get ideas onto paper and to consider them from different viewpoints. Acts as an alternative to linear thinking and helps with creativity. A more visual way to express your thoughts. Different map layouts are available.
  - How to access:
    - Staff: Available to install from the Software Centre (in the Start menu) on staff devices.
    - Students and staff: Available in the A–Z list of applications within the Horizon service.
    - Learn more: How to guides for MindGenius.
- **Whiteboard:**
  - What it does: Makes it easy to collaborate, brainstorm, plan, and share with others on a digital canvas, all in real time.
  - How to access: Available as part of the Microsoft 365 suite of applications. Also built in and available with every Teams meeting.  
    Learn how to use Whiteboard.
- **OneNote:**
  - What it does: Lets you capture, organize, and share your notes across your devices.
  - How to access: Available as part of the Microsoft 365 suite of applications.  
    Learn how to use OneNote.
- **To Do:**
  - What it does: Enables you to add tasks for yourself, and prioritise and manage them.
  - How to access: Available as part of the Microsoft 365 suite of applications.  
    Learn how to use To Do.
- **Planner:**
  - What it does: Enables a group of people (members of a Microsoft team or an Office 365 Group) to manage tasks within the group.
  - How to access: Available as part of the Microsoft 365 suite of applications.  
    Learn how to use Planner.
- **Collections in Microsoft Edge:**
  - What it does: Helps you keep track of your ideas on the web, whether you're collecting notes for research or lesson plans, or just want to pick up where you left off the last time you were browsing.
  - How to access: The Collections button at the top right of the Edge browser.  
    Learn how to use Collections in Edge.
- **Focus assist:**
  - What it does: Allows you to avoid distracting notifications when you need to stay focused. Helps minimise visual distractions by turning off animations, background images, and more. You can control which icons appear on the taskbar, reduce the number of items in view, select what to show in the Start menu, define which notifications you see and hear, and limit late-night notifications.
  - How to access: On a Windows computer, go to Start → Settings → System → Focus assist.  
    Learn how to use Focus assist.  
    Learn how to make it easier to focus on your tasks (Microsoft).
- **Additional note:**
  - You may also find the recording and transcription features within Microsoft Teams useful for help with note-taking.
Image-to-text conversion
Assistive Scanning Tool:
- **Microsoft Lens:**
  - What it does: Converts pictures of printed text to digital text.
  - How to access:
    - Microsoft Lens for Android.
    - Microsoft Lens for iOS.
Enhance the visibility of content of your screen
Accessibility Features for Visual Assistance:
- **Make Windows easier to see:**
  - What it does: Adjust the size, colour, and contrast of text, apps, and other elements of the screen. Change the size and colour of your mouse pointer. Apply colour filters. Magnify all or part of your screen to see content better.
  - How to access: On a Windows computer, go to Start → Settings → Ease of Access → Display.  
    Learn about how to make Windows easier to see.
Wellbeing
Wellbeing Support Tool:
- **Wellbeing tab in Viva Insights:**
  - What it does: Block out time to focus. Catch up on messages, learn, and take breaks. Mindfully close out your workday. Silence messages and emails during your off-hours. Do a guided meditation. Reflect on how you're feeling.
  - How to access: Available as part of the Microsoft 365 suite of applications.  
    Learn how to use the Wellbeing tab in Viva Insights.

SOURCE_URL: https://unibradfordac.sharepoint.com/sites/peer-assisted-learning-intranet/SitePages/Peer-Assisted-Learning-Scheme-(PAL)-Overview.aspx

Peer Assisted Learning (PAL)
Peer Assisted Learning Scheme (PAL) Overview
Overview
PAL Leader recruitment for 24/25 will open soon. Get in touch with the pal team if you are interested or have any questions.
Peer Assisted Learning (PAL) at Bradford aims to support the student learning experience by utilising and enabling peer interaction and support.
Schemes are based on good practice from the HE sector, can be tailored to suit particular groups of students and are coordinated centrally by the Student Experience and Success Team (SEST) in partnership with programme teams.
PAL involves students in higher levels (e.g. second or third years) volunteering as our PAL Leaders to facilitate regular small group learning and support sessions with new students, which are timetabled within programmes of study.
PAL at Bradford has two central aims, these are to support:
1. New student transition into and engagement with university and study life
2. The employability and development of students that lead the peer support
It also strives to develop a sense of community and belonging for students in their programme/department, enabling inter-level interaction and an environment in which peer to peer learning can take place.
A crucial principle underpinning the scheme is that it is student centred and determined. Students decide what to cover in sessions and these are facilitated by the student PAL Leaders. Sessions are co-participatory and involve group and social learning/support. They can focus on academic aspects such as programme content and study skills and/or can offer social interaction and support. PAL therefore offers a unique learning environment in that it is personalised and gives students an informal space, led by them, to work through materials, ideas and questions together.
In 2012/13 a Peer Assisted Learning (PAL) scheme was piloted by SEST and the Department of Computing. The project was funded by the National HE STEM Programme. Since then, progress has been really positive and each year more schemes have been established.
For 2023/24 we have schemes running across all 4 faculties. It's great to see so many students volunteering to make a difference and staff support from the programmes has been essential in establishing and developing these schemes.
It is hoped that PAL will continue to grow so that even more students will have the opportunity to engage in peer support.
The Student Experience & Success Team manage all PAL activity in partnership with programme teams.

--- SOURCE SEPARATOR ---

SOURCE_URL: https://unibradfordac.sharepoint.com/sites/peer-assisted-learning-intranet/SitePages/PAL-for-Students.aspx

Peer Assisted Learning (PAL)
PAL for Students
New students
An important part of your student experience is working with and learning from your peers. Many departments at the University run a Peer Assisted Learning (PAL) scheme. These aim to support your transition into and engagement with university and study life as well as d evelop a sense of community and belonging.
PAL involves students in higher levels (usually their second or third year) – our PAL Leaders – facilitating group learning and support sessions with new students, which are timetabled within programmes of study. Students are encouraged and supported to take ownership of PAL and so work together to decide what to cover in the sessions. This approach offers a unique learning environment in that it is personalised and gives students informal opportunities, led by them, to work through needs, ideas and questions together.
PAL therefore offers a friendly and informal space to can also help students to:
- Share and learn from experiences
- Strengthen understanding of programme and university related knowledge
- Develop transferable skills and attributes such as group working and confidence
What happens in PAL sessions?
PAL Leaders plan for and run PAL sessions based on their experiences as a new student and on the needs and suggestions of their group. So you set the agenda for sessions and it is your student only time and space to work with and learn from others.
Your PAL Leaders are not there to teach you or give you the answers to all your questions. They are there to facilitate new students to search for their own solutions together as a group. Students have used PAL sessions to cover a very wide range of areas, these include:
- Referencing
- Note taking
- Essay writing
- Library and finding resources (journals etc.)
- Using CANVAS
- Understanding key course concepts and skills (e.g. java programming, pharmacy calculations, accounting, SPSS etc)
- Time management
- Group work
- Presentation skills and practising presentations
- Pebblepad
- Reflections/ reflective writing
- Preparing for assessments and exam techniques
- Understanding marks and feedback
- Support with project work
- Providing peer feedback
- Placement & careers
- Extra curricular activity
Does my programme have PAL?
Find out if your programme has a PAL scheme. You can suggest setting a new PAL scheme up if your programme is yet to develop one by contacting the pal team.
PAL Leaders
Being a PAL Leader is a great opportunity to help others whilst gaining experience and developing your skills too. Being a PAL Leader can offer the following benefits:
- A great way to add to your CV and provide concrete examples to help you to stand out from others
- Transferable skills and personal development (e.g. communication, teamwork, facilitation, problem solving & leadership)
- Consolidation of your course knowledge by revisiting core ideas and skills
- Opportunity to share your experiences as a student with others
- Enjoyable and rewarding experience – the chance to make a difference!
- Certification and HEAR acknowledgement
Each year we also fund a number of PAL Leaders to attend the National PASS/PAL Conference.
In addition, we recognise PAL Leaders and schemes as part of the Student Leaders Awards each year. We have awards for the 'PAL Leader of the Year', 'PAL Partnership of the Year' and also 'PAL Scheme of the Year'.
Want to be a PAL Leader?
If your programme runs a PAL scheme and you're interested in becoming a PAL Leader, please email the pal team.

--- SOURCE SEPARATOR ---

SOURCE_URL: https://unibradfordac.sharepoint.com/sites/peer-assisted-learning-intranet/SitePages/Benefits-of-PAL.aspx

Peer Assisted Learning (PAL)
Benefits of and recognition for PAL
PAL has numerous potential benefits: for new students, PAL Leaders and to staff or a department.
Evidence
Growing evidence from the HE sector demonstrates the value of academic peer learning. For example those involved in PAL can show greater engagement, improved grades and better retention (Keenan 2014). This has been reinforced by a recent (2024) collaborative report on student-led peer learning and support that the University of Bradford was part of the report team for.
At Bradford our own evaluations have highlighted how students value the opportunity to learn from the experiences of peers and how PAL has supported:
- Support with transition
- Engagement with studies
- Support for learning, course and study skills
- Sense of belonging
- Employability and future focus
- Developing confidence
More broadly, students who are engaged and have a greater sense of belonging are more likely to stay on their course and to achieve success (Foster et al. 2011 and Thomas 2012).
Recognition for PAL
Winner of the 'Vice-Chancellors Teaching Excellence Award ' 2015 for PAL (now Bradford Teaching Fellow).
Noted in 2014 QAA Higher Education Review of the University of Bradford.
PAL Scheme Manager is a member of the International Association for Peer Learning and Support' Steering Group..
PAL Leader role a recognised Bradford Employability Award for the HEAR.
PAL specifically noted in the University’s TEF Silver award 2017 Statement of Findings.

Setting up a new PAL scheme
Schemes are open to programmes at foundation, undergraduate or postgraduate level and are managed centrally by SEST, in partnership with programme staff.Schemes follow a central and institution wide model that also allows the team to design an approach to best suit a particular programme. SEST recruit and train PAL Leaders, supporting them and staff throughout the schemes.
New schemes usually need to be requested by the end of February for a September start but we may be able to offer alternative timings.
If you are a student or staff member who would be interested in developing a PAL scheme in your programme or Faculty, then please email the pal team.

IT Services
IT Services ticket escalation procedure
The following is the best route to escalate an open ticket that you have logged with IT Services. This should only be used in cases where you feel that a ticket is not being given appropriate attention, and where the impact on the University or on an individual is high.
- When should a ticket escalation be initiated?
- When you've had no response to (or any update) on an open ticket, and where the impact on the University or on an individual is high.
- When there is adverse impact to your day-to-day activities.
When you are extremely dissatisfied and would like IT Services management contact.
Escalation requests will have a designated owner, and you'll be kept informed of the progression and resolution plan throughout, until the ticket is resolved.
How to escalate a ticket
Please remember to have the ticket number to hand.
Escalation Contact Levels:
- **Level 1:**
  - Who to contact: IT Servicedesk
  - How: Call 01274 233333
- **Level 2:**
  - Who to contact: IT Servicedesk Team Leaders
  - How: Call 01274 233333 and ask to speak to a Team Leader
- **Level 3 (after 8 hours):**
  - Who to contact: Cath Riddiough (Service Desk Manager)
  - How: Call 01274 234886, or email c.riddiough@bradford.ac.uk
- **Level 4 (after 12 hours):**
  - Who to contact: Russ Hind (Head of Service Delivery)
  - How: Call 01274 233316, or email r.hind@bradford.ac.uk
- **Level 5 (after 48 hours):**
  - Who to contact:
    - Juliette Atkinson (IT Director): Call 01274 232177, or email j.atkinson7@bradford.ac.uk
    - Angie Beswick (Associate IT Director): Call 01274 233136, or email a.beswick@bradford.ac.uk

Postgraduate Researchers
PGR Record Management in e:Vision
Record management in e:Vision provides greater oversight of research degree milestones, including progression, submission, and supervisor meeting records. You will now receive frequent email reminders for upcoming milestones.
Monthly supervisor meeting forms must now be completed in e:Vision, at the end of the supervision meeting. This supervision record provides an indicator of engagement throughout the research degree and it is imperative that it is submitted monthly (every other month for part-time PGRs).
We hope you find the PGR record in e:Vision user-friendly, but we want to help you navigate the look and feel. There are handy videos for quick reference and written guides if you want more detailed information.
If you don't immediately see the Research Management tab in e:Vision, launch a new browser, and clear your cookies and cache (and select “all time”).
Additionally, the PGR Administration Team are available for e:Vision drop-in queries between 14:00 -15:00 Monday to Friday. Please bring your device with you to show us your issue and we'll help guide you through.
Urgent issues with e:Vision that are not addressed by the guidance, or resolved in a drop-in session, should be logged with the IT Service desk.
PGR e:Vision Resources
You will see an overview of your research record when you click on the Research Management tab when logged into e:Vision.
To see greater detail, or complete any actions in e:Vision, click the blue “Student Management” button.Editing events in e:Vision

This will then display any hidden columns and you will be able to access editable events by clicking the “edit” button. The “edit” button is located under the Action column.

Registry and Student Administration
Awards Team
We support the production of student documentation.
This includes:
- replacement of degree and award certificates
- certified certificates
- transcripts
- verifications pre 2003
- award letters
- posting uncollected certificates to students
-  process award and progression decisions
p- roviding graduates with relevant documentation which may be required for job applications and visa purposes
Please note, we do not release certificates and transcript until students have cleared their debt status.
Contact us
- Email: awards@bradford.ac.uk
- Phone: +44 (0) 1274 233002
Frequently asked questions
I need a certificate and copy of my transcript?
If you need to place an order for your transcript, or need a replacement certificate or certified copies of your certificates, visit our online store.
Once you've placed your order, please email a copy of your photo identification (for example, passport or driving licence) to awards@bradford.ac.uk.
What is a transcript?
A transcript is an academic record containing a statement of your learning and records achieved from your studies.
The transcript will contain the marks and grades achieved from each of the modules you have studied, as well as the credit value.
A transcript is provided to every graduate from the University which may be required for visa or job application purposes.
How can I access my transcript?
If you graduated after 2007 and need to share your transcript, sign in to Gradintel. If you have never signed into Gradintel before, or you can't sign, in please email the Awards Team.
If you graduated after 2007 and need a signed and stamped copy of your transcript, please order this through the online store.

Opportunities Abroad
Short International Programmes
Short International Programmes are a fantastic way to engage with international opportunities without committing to a full semester or year abroad. These last anywhere from 1-7 weeks and can provide you with many of the benefits of a longer experience. There is a huge amount of diversity in destinations and activities you can part in to develop your academic, employability or intercultural skills. These programmes cover a wide range of disciplines and subjects and are open to undergraduate and postgraduate students. You will need to apply for each one individually.
Previous short International Programmes have included the Study in China Programme, Nanjing Youth Programme, various Summer Schools around the world or completing an Elective with Work the World.
General Short International Programmes Guidelines
- For how long? Generally 4 weeks
- Who can apply? All students, including PG (home, EU or International)
- Where can I go? Any country in the world
- What can I do? Summer Schools; Electives; Short Internships, Cultural & Language Programs
- Can I get funding? Yes, the Turing Scheme can provide funding.
*The Short-Term Travel Award is intended to contribute to the international flight cost of the academic/work-related activities undertaken by the student. Please see the Short Term Travel Application Form in the documentation section for more information.
How to Apply
1. Confirm with your department that you are eligible for a Short International Programme and when you are allowed to go.
2. Meet with the International Opportunities Team to discuss where you can go and which programmes are available. If you are thinking of applying for a Summer School or Elective then research appropriately. If you need help with creating or updating your CV and Cover Letter then book an appointment through Handshake Career Centre.
3. Apply using the Short Term Travel Awards Application Form.
4. Discuss your Short Term Travel Programme with your Departmental Exchange Coordinator. Please make sure that they have signed your application form, we cannot accept unsigned forms.
5. Email the Application Form to studyabroad@bradford.ac.uk
Summer Schools
Summer Schools often take place at universities or companies worldwide during the summer. They cover a broad range of subjects that are applicable to many of our students' degree programme. We have compiled a list of Summer Schools available to you which you can find at the bottom of this page - please note that this list is not exhaustive and you can find more opportunities on the Summer Schools in Europe website. You can also contact any of our Erasmus+ Partner Universities to enquire about their Summer School programmes.
What are students say about Summer Schools:
"The programme was very tailored well. I had the chance to develop my theoretical understanding, but at the same time I attended company visits and I debated with CSR directors and managers of large companies like Hugo Boss or Bosch about the challenges of this area."
-Bianca Drotleff, BSc International Business and Management, Hamburg School of Administration, Summer 2018.
Electives (Faculty of Health only)
Final Year Nursing or Midwifery Students can choose to complete their elective abroad by working in a hospital for three weeks through Work the World or another provider. Our students have completed their electives in Tanzania, Ghana and Nepal to name just a few countries. Health Students who are interested in this Opportunity Abroad should firstly contact the Departmental Exchange Co-ordinator for more information.
What our students say about Electives Abroad "My greatest achievement during my placement in Nepal was feeling like I made the labouring women’s experiences better than would have been. Being able to hold their hand and rub their back through contractions made me feel like I was making such a positive difference. The experience was life changing both on a personal and professional level."
-Victoria Hands, BSc Midwifery Studies, Summer 2018.
Any Questions?
Come and have a chat to see how we can help you.
Location: Career and Employability Services (Student Central, Level 0)
Individual Appointments are also available
- Email us studyabroad@bradford.ac.uk
- Book through Handshake Career Centre.

SOURCE_URL: https://unibradfordac.sharepoint.com/sites/opportunities-abroad-intranet/SitePages/Study-Abroad.aspx

Opportunities Abroad
Study Abroad
Studying abroad is the most well-known of the international opportunities available to students at Bradford. It typically encompasses full-year exchanges with one of our partner universities.
If you choose a semester abroad, you will pay fees as normal to the University of Bradford. There are no additional charges.
If you choose a full year abroad, you will pay a significantly reduced tuition fee for that additional year
There are two different schemes through which you can study abroad, both with their own destinations, application process and funding levels. These are explained below:
Who can apply for study abroad?
- Full Year: 2nd-year students (UG only)
- Single Semester: 1st-year students (Management UG only)
- Work Placement SEM1 / Study Abroad SEM2: Available on request, please book an appointment to speak with us about this possibility.
- Programmes: Check to see if you can study abroad using our list.
When does it happen?
- Full Year: Sandwich (additional) year between your 2nd and 3rd years at Bradford (or between your 3rd and 4th years in a 4-year programme).
- Single Semester: The 2nd semester of your 2nd year. This requires module matching/mapping. (Management courses only)
Option 1: Turing Scheme (via ISEP Network)
Turing Scheme and The ISEP Network
The Turing Scheme is the UK Government's new global programme to work and study abroad. It will soon replace the ERASMUS programme above after 2023. For the time being, both options are available to our students.
The funding from the Turing Scheme will be administered to those applying through the ISEP Network; a varied group of different universities across the globe which allow student mobility between one another.
This option is the most popular for Bradford students as accommodation and food are all arranged for you. This means that you can study and enjoy your experience without sweating the small things.
General Turing Scheme/ISEP Guidelines
- For how long?: Full Academic Year (1 semester option for management).
- Who can apply?: 1st and 2nd-year students (UG only)
- What can I do?: Study relevant modules to your degree and outside of it.
- Can I get funding?: See funding page
How to Apply
1. Confirm with your tutor that you can Study Abroad.
2. Meet with the International Opportunities Team to discuss where you can go and which programmes are available, then research your options online.
3. You should then complete the Study Abroad Application Form.
4. Discuss your module selection with your Departmental Coordinator.
5. We will look over applications every month and be in contact regarding the next steps.
Option 2: Worldwide Exchanges
Our current Worldwide Exchange partner institutions include universities from Australia, Canada, Asia, Europe and the USA.
General Worldwide Exchange Guidelines
- For how long? Full Academic Year (1 semester option for management)
- Who can apply? 1st and 2nd-year students (UG only)
- Where can I go? Australia, Canada, Asia, and the USA. 
- What can I do? Study relevant modules to your degree course.
- Can I get funding? See funding page
How to Apply
1. Confirm with your tutor that you can Study Abroad.
2. Meet with the International Opportunities Team to discuss where you can go and which programmes are available, then research your options online.
3. You should then complete the Study Abroad Application Form.
4. Discuss your module selection with your Departmental Coordinator.
5. We will look over applications every month and be in contact regarding the next steps.
Any Questions?
Come and have a chat to see how we can help you.
Location: Career and Employability Services (Student Central, Level 0)
Individual Appointments are available.
- Email us studyabroad@bradford.ac.uk
- Book through Handshake Career Centre

SOURCE_URL: https://unibradfordac.sharepoint.com/sites/opportunities-abroad-intranet/SitePages/Work.aspx

Opportunities Abroad
Working Abroad (Internships and Placements)
Working abroad through internships and placements is a fantastic way to engage with international experiences while also boosting your employability skills. Any student on any course and at any level can take part and receive funding for a placement over their summer break or directly after graduation
The International Opportunities department is not responsible for finding you a placement abroad, athough we will show you where to find them. Once you have found a suitable position we will support you through the travel and funding process.
There are a number of different schemes through which you can acquire funding for your placement, these tend to depend on the country the placement is in and whether it is an in-person or virtual opportunity.
It can be difficult to secure a position abroad without existing connections due to VISA complications.
We can also offer split sandwich years between a UK-based Placement semester and Study Abroad semester.
Who can take part
Anybody All students no matter their programme or level is eligible for funding if they find a suitable placement.
When does it happen?
- Summer break
- After graduation
- Sandwich Year
- Can be anywhere from 2-12 months
Where can I go?
Anywhere! With all of our funding schemes, every country outside the UK has grant coverage.
If you're an international student, you can even get funding to work in your home country.
Where can I find Internships and Placements?
- **Erasmus Intern:**  
  - While Erasmus+ Intern is normally for opportunities in the EU, there are often a few overseas options advertised. Erasmus+ Intern allows you to tailor the searches on this website by academic field and whether the placement is paid or unpaid.
- **Goinglobal:**  
  - Goinglobal is a great place to find opportunities from a huge variety of countries across the globe. You can search for specific nations, career areas, and online vs remote. Make sure you use the provided University of Bradford link for off-campus access.
- **Handshake:**  
  - The University of Bradford's student job portal has many different work placements and internships with major global companies. You will need to activate your account to view the opportunities.
- **Placement Slovakia:**  
  - This site looks specifically at placements in Slovakia. All placements listed here include food and accommodation. Slovakia is also a very affordable country to live and work in.
- **Anywhere else:**  
  - You do not have to find internships through these sites. If you have contacts in other companies abroad through family, friends, or previous employment, you are still eligible for funding.
How much funding can I get?
You can see full details on our funding page
The Schemes
Overseas Placements
Any country in the world
Turing Scheme
Specific countries and placements available
Speak with your department exchange coordinator
How to Apply
1. Talk to your Departmental Exchange Coordinator to secure support.
2. Search for your placement. See the Opportunities Abroad Home Page for live opportunities, plus visit the recommended websites above.
3. Arrange an appointment with Career and Employability Services for CV and covering letter support. You can arrange an appointment through Handshake Career Centre.
4. Book a one-to-one appointment with the International Opportunities Team through Handshake Career Centre.
5. Once you have the acceptance letter from a host organisation, you can speak to us at studyabroad@bradford.ac.uk and we can help you through the grant process.
Important Additional Information about Overseas Placements
Visas and Working Worldwide
- Permanent EU Residents: If you are applying for an Overseas Placement then please be aware that the country that you are choosing to work in will require you to apply for a Visa before entering.
- Non-EU Residents: If you are applying for an Overseas Placement then please be aware that the country that you are choosing to work in will require you to apply for a Visa before entering.
Part of your application will involve applying for the appropriate Visa. It is your responsibility to apply for the appropriate visa before you travel to the Host Organisation.
Any Questions?
Come and have a chat to see how we can help you.
Location: Career and Employability Services (Student Central, Level 0)
Individual appointments are also available:
- Email us: studyabroad@bradford.ac.uk
- Book through Handshake Career Centre.

ISEP - International Exchange Programme, Worldwide Exchanges, ERASMUS+
What are my options?
There are lots of opportunities to go abroad. You can study, work or do a short international programme in more than 150 countries across the world.
Study
Study options for the full year include Erasmus+ in and out of the EU, plus ISEP and Worldwide Exchange in countries including the USA, Australia, Japan and the UAE.
Funding is available for flights, accommodation and living expenses.
Work
Work a full year, over summer or for up to a year after graduation in the EU, through Erasmus+, or outside of the EU with Overseas Placements.
Funded research placements for undergraduates and postgraduates are also available.
Grants and scholarships are available for flights, accommodation and living expenses.
Short Programmes
Go abroad for 1-7 weeks to take part in a summer school, elective placement or other short international programme in countries such as China, Tanzania and Germany.
Short Term Travel Awards of up to £1330 are available.

Opportunities Abroad
Erasmus+ Partner Institutions: Faculty of Engineering and Informatics and Faculty of Health Sciences
Below you will find a list of Erasmus+ Partner Institutions for the Faculty of Engineering and Informatics and for the Faculty of Health Studies. If you are thinking about an Erasmus+ Exchange at one of these Institutions then you will need to look at their module selection, term dates and any other important information associated to their application. Health and Safety
Health and Safety Procedures
To support you further, our Health and Safety team have produced several procedure documents. These documents provide you with a step-by-step process to follow when completing a variety of activities within University premises
Each procedural document takes into consideration the legal and legislative requirement alongside the practicalities of real time on campus activities. The procedural documents also provide further advice about how to implement and adhere to the requirements of the University policies guidance.
Our Health and Safety documents are continuously evolving, as a result some policies also may incorporate procedures , we therefore we recommend you check both our policies and procedures pages for information.

--- SOURCE SEPARATOR ---

SOURCE_URL: https://unibradfordac.sharepoint.com/sites/health-safety-and-wellbeing-intranet/SitePages/Policies.aspx

Health and Safety
Health and Safety Policies
In accordance with UK Health and Safety legislation the University must make and have suitable arrangements for managing Health and Safety.
As part of this process the Health and Safety team have produced several policies which aim to prevent, minimise and control risk within University premises and its associated activities.
Each policy takes into consideration the legal and legislative requirements alongside the practicalities of real time on campus activities. Before publishing our policies they are robustly reviewed by a number of sources which include our appointed Trade Union Representatives.
Our policies are continuously evolving, as a result some may incorporate procedures alongside the policy, we therefore we recommend you check both our policies and procedures pages for information.

SOURCE_URL: https://unibradfordac.sharepoint.com/sites/research-students-intranet/SitePages/Frequently-asked-questions.aspx

Postgraduate Researchers
Frequently Asked Questions
When can I enrol for my Postgraduate Research Degree?
We welcome applications for post graduate degrees all year round and have October, February and June cohorts.
Can I request an invoice for tuition fees in advance?
Yes, you can request a pro-forma invoice, please email your request to sas-fees@bradford.ac.uk
How is the studentship set up?
If you are a new student you will need to complete the following procedure before you receive any studentship payment:
- Complete your enrolment at the University
- Provide bank details to the Postgraduate Research Administration Office in the PGR Lounge, Carlton Building using the form provided to you via email
The Postgraduate Research Administration Team will provide you with a bank details form to complete and prepare a letter to confirm your studentship. Once we have received your bank details and you have enroled, we will be able to send payment details to our finance team who will set up and process your payments.
When and where will I be paid my studentship?
Payment is normally made direct to your bank account on the 6th of each month (or on the first working day after the 6th if this falls on a weekend or bank holiday).
I haven't received my monthly studentship...
If you have not received a studentship payment you are expecting, please e-mail your PG Research Administrator and they will look into it. Contact details are on the Home page of this site.
Why, in e-Vision, do I appear to be in Year 2 when I am now in Year 3 or higher?
The Year in e-vision does not correspond to the year of study. A student has a ‘Year 3’ when they have submitted.
What do you look for when appointing an examining team?
The main things that the University looks for when appointing your examining team are experience and externality. Thorough checks are carried out to ensure that the team has the correct knowledge, experience and externality. For example, an examiner that has published with the student in question would not be approved. Faculties are encouraged to provide supporting documentation for unconventional applications; however, we must continue to protect and uphold the University’s integrity and reputation.
Where can I submit my thesis?
Thesis submissions are made electronically.
When you are ready to submit your thesis, please contact your Faculty PGR inbox for more detail.
They will be able to provide you with guidance of how to submit your thesis via CANVAS and open a submission box for this purpose.
How and when can I pay my tuition fees?
You can pay your fees in full before or at enrolment, if you would like to pay in person you can do so by visiting Payzone who are located in the Richmond Building on campus. Should paying in person not be a viable option, you can pay online or over the telephone (cardholder only) +44 (0) 300 023 0503.
If you are paying a tuition fee of more than £500.00, you can pay in instalments.
Do I have to attend inductions?
Yes, the faculty and University induction sessions are a requirement for all new postgraduate research students across the University, whether part time or full time (induction is not compulsory for students who are studying extramural)
How do I access the Postgraduate Research Lounge (PGR Lounge)?
In order to access the PGR Lounge you must be a current, enrolled, PGR student. If you are experiencing access issues, please contact your faculty administrator and we will endeavour to assist you.
Who can help me access my office / lab / workshop?
Access to Faculty areas is granted by the Faculty via your supervisor. Once you have met with your supervisor, they will be able to contact access control to request the relevant access rights.
Where can I get help about open access?
For detail regarding open access visit the Library website.
Do I have to complete annual progress report?
Yes, this is an essential part of your research journey and provides a good indication of your progress. Continuation of your research is subject to satisfactory completion of progress reporting.
Why have I been charged another writing-up fee?
The writing-up fee is charged on an annual basis to both full-time and part-time students and covers use of the University facilities (access to the Library is granted until the viva date upon request and access to email continues until 90 days after Graduation).
The amount per year is minimal so is not reduced pro rata when a student submits their thesis during the academic year nor when a student suspends their registration during the writing-up period.
Why have I been charged a full tuition fee when I have been registered for long enough to pay the writing-up fee?
Transfer to writing-up has not yet been confirmed by your Faculty. Please speak to your supervisor in the first instance
What is a re-submission fee?
If the outcome of your viva examination was to 'revise and represent', you will be required to pay a re-submission fee. This fee covers the re-examination and administrative costs of re-submission, you will be notified in writing the re-submission fee, however, details can be found on the tuition fees section of the website.
How much will my tuition fees be next year?
See details of the next academic year's fees. Should you be unable to find the information that you require please contact the sas-fees@bradford.ac.uk for further assistance on your fee related query.
I have submitted my thesis and I am a full-time student; can my Council Tax exemption continue while waiting for my viva?
Council Tax is a charge set by local authorities to help pay for certain public services such as libraries, schools and police. The University can provide a letter confirming your status as a full-time (or part-time) student and while you are in the "writing-up" phase of study, (which is normally for a period of 12 months from the end of the research phase of study).
Once you have submitted the first draft of your thesis, are waiting for your viva or are completing any corrections following examination, we are unable to provide a council tax exemption certificate. However, the University can prepare you a Confirmation of Study letter. It is then your responsibility to establish eligibility with the Council Tax Office.
Can the University of Bradford recommend someone to proofread my work?
The University of Bradford is unable to offer a proof reading or checking service, however, Academic Skills Advice has resources you may access, for further information please visit Academic Skills.
How can I apply for a thesis embargo?
Students can apply for a thesis embargo by completing the relevant form (see forms section of the website). The form must be returned to your supervisor who will notify your faculty, your faculty will arrange for a Publications Adjudications Panel who will approve or deny the request. Please note that an embargo is treated as a serious matter, however, the University acknowledge that there are circumstances that require such action.
Can my supervisor be appointed as an examiner?
No, your supervisor cannot be selected as an examiner; the main reason for this is that it poses a conflict of interest.
I have a disability; can I make a request for reasonable adjustments to be made for my viva examination?
Yes you can, for anyone with a diagnosed disability you can make a request for reasonable adjustments in your viva examination. You should contact the Disability Service, well in advance of your submission for examination, so they can assess your needs.
Any further questions?
Should you not find the answer to your question, please do get in touch as your question will help us to update this site. Please e-mail your PGR Faculty Administrator, contact details may be found on the Home page of this site.

--- SOURCE SEPARATOR ---

SOURCE_URL: https://unibradfordac.sharepoint.com/sites/research-students-intranet/SitePages/Completion.aspx

Postgraduate Researchers
Completion
Approval of thesis title and sub-title
Not less than three months before the submission date the proposed title and sub-title of the thesis should be submitted to the Postgraduate Research Administration Team for approval by the Faculty Postgraduate Research Sub-Committee. This applies to all research degrees. Thesis title approval forms are completed in e:Vision.
The sub-title must be not more than 30 words and must include the words in full for any abbreviations used in the title. Outside of explaining abbreviations, it is not recommended to repeat the keywords already used in the title. If no sub-title is required, please just state N/A when completing the Approval of Thesis Title Form.
Students must submit up to 10 Keywords for the purpose of indexing. Please do not exceed the 10 word maximum.
Approval of Thesis Title
Please note: you should retain the title approval letter you receive from the Postgraduate Research Administration Team to ensure when you submit your thesis that the title, sub-title and keywords match. Any discrepancies in this information will lead to delays in the Postgraduate Research Administration Team sending out your thesis for examination.
Length of thesis
The maximum length of the thesis is as follows:
Doctor of Philosophy 80,000 words (absolute maximum: 100,000)
Professional Doctorates Please check the programme specification for your year of entry for guidance on the word count.
Master of Philosophy 40,000 words
Appendices and bibliography are excluded from the above upper limits.
Please note: Examiners have in the past been very critical of excessively lengthy theses.
Style and layout of thesis
The following requirements are specified with regard to style and layout:
Important: Year of Submission:
Please use the year that the thesis were originally submitted prior to your first Viva Voce examination. This is important as it advises the reader in which year your research was current. You should not change the date even if your final submission occurs in a different academic year.
Font: 12 point Arial.
Line spacing: Minimum 1.5 up to double line spacing.
Left-hand margin: 40mm
Other margins: Recommended minimum 20mm, but may be more.
Pagination: The title page must not be numbered. Starting with the Abstract, pages must be numbered in lowercase Roman numerals centred at the foot of each page, up until the last page before the start of Chapter One.
From the start of Chapter One, each page must be numbered in the normal style, eg. 1, 2, 3. Page numbers must be centred at the foot of each page.
Contents: A paginated list of content must be included at the front.
Title page
The title page must give the following information in the order listed:
- The approved title of the thesis;
- The approved sub-title of the thesis;
- The total number of volumes if more than one, and the number of the particular volume;
- The full name of the candidate. It is important that forenames (in full) should be in lower-case letters and the surname or family name only in capital letters;
- The degree for which the thesis is submitted;
- The full name of the Faculty/Department in which the candidate is registered followed by 'University of Bradford';
-  The year of submission;
- The title page must not be numbered
Abstract
The abstract should be placed immediately after the title page. It should not exceed 250 words, summarising the contents. It should be headed with the candidate's name, the title of the thesis and up to ten subject keywords. After the degree has been awarded, the University may publish the title and abstract of the thesis and authorize others to do so. Page numbering in lower-case Roman numerals starts from the abstract onwards up until Chapter One.
Acknowledgement and dedications
If you wish to include an acknowledgement and/or dedication these should be included in the thesis after the abstract.
Main Body of the Thesis
From the start of Chapter One, each page must be numbered in the normal style - 1,2,3 etc, and page numbers must be centred at the foot of each page, continuing up until the end of your thesis, including any appendices.
Citation and Referencing Guides
Please be aware that different academic disciplines have different preferred styles for referencing. Please discuss with your supervisory team and also the Library's Citation and Referencing Guides are useful.
An example of thesis layout "Sample Thesis" can be found in the Documents section of the Research Students SharePoint site.
You may also find MS Word help, which is available online, a useful tool when it comes to formatting your thesis in line with the requirements.
Submission of the thesis
Thesis submissions are made electronically.
When you are ready to submit your thesis, please contact your Faculty PGR inbox for more detail. They will be able to provide you with guidance of how to submit your thesis on CANVAS and open a submission box for this purpose.
Deadlines
Submission of the thesis is expected during the writing up period. The length of registration for a Full-time PhD student is 4 years, from your date of registration. For a part-time student it is 7 years from the date of registration. These are the last possible dates you should work towards for the submission of your thesis. This is recorded on your student card and is displayed in e:Vision.
You should contact the Postgraduate Research Administration team three months in advance of when you expect to submit your thesis to begin preparation for your submission and examination.
The thesis must be laid out according to University standards. Any thesis not produced according to University standards will be rejected. To avoid disappointment , please ensure your thesis complies with University's Research regulations.
Front cover and spine
The thesis must have on the front cover:
- the initials and surname of the candidate.
- the title of the thesis (exactly the same as the approved title).
- the name of the degree for which the thesis is submitted.
- the year of your original submission i.e. pre Viva Voce examination.
The approved sub-title must appear on the title page underneath the title, but not on the cover of the thesis. Please do not include any other qualification titles you have received within the title/cover pages of the thesis.
What to include with your thesis:
- One copy of your electronic thesis
- One copy of any other material which is being submitted with the thesis.
- Thesis Submission Form, this is completed in e:Vision and certifies the work is an original work produced by you.
For PGRs who wish to print a copy of their thesis for their own records, on the spine we recommend the following written downwards so that it can be read as if a book laid flat, face upwards:
- the name of the candidate (surname or family name and initials).
- the name of the degree.
- the year of the original submission.
In addition, you may require:
- A Declaration of English Language Assistance.
- A Certificate where part or all of the research was undertaken away from University, signed by the External Supervisor or responsible person, that the thesis consists of the student's own work (this is normally done as a statement signed by the appropriate person and is bound within the thesis).
- If the work was done in co-operation with others, the candidates contribution must be certified in a statement by the candidate as to the share which the candidate has personally taken in the work (this is normally done as a statement signed by the student and is bound within the thesis).
Staff candidates with two External Examiners may wish to consider providing the Internal Chair (link person) with a copy of the thesis. This copy can be given direct to the member of staff concerned. At minimum the Staff candidate must provide the Link Person with their Thesis Abstract and Table of Contents.
Students must have available at their Viva Voce a copy of the thesis, paged in exactly the same way as the formal copy submitted to the PGRA Team. This copy is for your own reference during the Viva.
Additional material and appendices
Where applicable, diagrams, maps and similar documents may be submitted in a portfolio of any size but must bear the particulars as shown on the thesis cover and spine.
Where it is necessary to include a substantial amount of support material within a thesis, such information may be contained in an appendix which depending on the size, can form a second volume and needs to be clearly titled when submitted electronically. It should be stated in the thesis, at the appropriate point, what software has been used.
COVID-19 Impact Statement
At submission of the thesis, PGRs may include a statement summarising the research that had originally been planned and how disruption to their research, caused by the pandemic, has altered those plans. PGRs should seek guidance and support in writing this statement from their supervisory team, or their faculty Director of PGR. This statement should set the context of the submitted research within the assessment requirements for the research degree for which the PGR is registered. The statement should include a timeline of planned research that has no longer been possible together with a timeline of work successfully undertaken. The statement should include a summary of the work that has been undertaken in an attempt to mitigate the disruption to the PGR’s original, pre-pandemic schedule.
Please note, completion of the forms are not compulsory. However, if a PGR believes their work has been affected by the COVID-19 pandemic, we recommend this is completed with the guidance of the PGRs supervisor or Faculty Director of Postgraduate Research. In submitting a statement, PGRs agree that the contents will be provided to their examination team. Forms can be found on the Documents section of the Research Students SharePoint site.
Re-submission of the thesis after the first viva
If your Examiners have recommended an outcome of Revise and Re-present of your thesis, the process above detailing pre-viva submission must be followed again. You must keep the year of submission the same as the original submission, for example, if you first submitted in 2012 and you are re-submitting in 2013 please also put 2012 on your re-submitted thesis.
The Postgraduate Research Administration Team are responsible for submitting revised theses to Examiners - no person is allowed to send a copy of a revised thesis to the Examiners prior to formal re-submission.
For further details on revisions to the thesis, please see Amendments to Thesis further down the page. Once again, re-submission of your thesis will be managed electronically.
Final submission post-viva / exam
Please see information regarding Final Submission further down the page.
Appointment of examiners
The request for the appointment of examiners needs to be made at least 3-months before the expected thesis submission. The required form can be found on the documents section in the Postgraduate Researchers site, along with guidance.
Two Examiners are normally appointed for each candidate for a research degree, one of whom is an External Examiner and the other a member of the academic staff of the University who shall not be the candidate's Supervisor. In all cases an Independent Chair is required.
A list of currently approved chairs can be obtained from the Postgraduate Research Administration Team.
Supervisor's responsibilities
The Regulations for Research Degrees state that the appointment of Examiners must be proposed by the Supervisors and approved by the relevant Director of Postgraduate Research for the Faculty. In turn, the recommendation for the appointment of Examiners is approved by the Faculty Postgraduate Research Sub-committee and the Research Degrees Sub-Committee.
The completed 'Recommendation for Appointment of Examiners' Form should be submitted to the Faculty Postgraduate Research Sub-committee (via the Postgraduate Research Administrative team) for approval as early as possible and no later than two months prior to submission of the thesis. Details received after this time may lead to delays in finalising examination arrangements.
Prior to submitting the completed recommendation for Appointment of Examiners Form the Supervisor/s and/or Director of Postgraduate Research for the Faculty should ensure all proposed examiners are willing to act in this capacity and the balance of the examining team (subject expertise and/or examining/supervisory experience) is fully considered.
Please also note, for proposed External Examiners, we also request a full academic CV be attached to the Appointment of Examiners Form (this should include a list of publications, together with research student supervisory and examining experience).
Further information
The role and responsibilities of the Examiners are explained fully in Guidance for Internal and External Examiners for Research Degrees (on the Academic Quality Unit website).
Full guidelines for research examiners can be found in the Guidance for Examining Teams for Research Degrees document located on the documents section of the Documents section of the Research Students Sharepoint site.
The formal requirements underpinning the appointment of Examiners are also set out in the Ordinances and Regulations for Research Degrees.
Examiners for staff candidates
Members of academic staff who are candidates for a higher degree by research must have two External Examiners. In these circumstances there is no Internal Examiner but an Independent Chair known as a Link Person is normally appointed.
The request for the appointment of examiners needs to be made at least 3-months before the expected thesis submission. The required form can be found on the documents section in the Postgraduate Researchers site, along with guidance.
The Role of the Link Person
The Link Person is not an Examiner. Their role is to liaise with the External Examiners and the candidate in co-ordinating arrangements for the Viva. In particular, they should ascertain whether the student wishes the Supervisor(s) to be present at the Viva and then inform the Supervisor(s) whether or not they should attend.
The Link Person should chair the Viva and ensure it is conducted according to University Regulations, and where appropriate liaise with the External Examiners, candidate and Supervisor(s) after the Viva.
Research Assistants
Research Assistants are classified as members of academic-related rather than academic staff and, as such, the normal arrangements for the appointment of examiners apply (that is appointment of an External and an Internal Examiner).
More detailed information may be found in the Guidance for Internal and External Examiners of Candidates for Research Degrees, available on the Learning, Teaching and Quality Enhancement website.
Supervisor(s) role at the viva
Under the Regulations for Research Degrees, the Supervisor(s) are permitted to attend the Viva and may be invited by the Examiners to offer comments at any appropriate point during the examination process. The Supervisor(s) may request permission to clarify a particular point during the course of the Viva.
The candidate should be consulted by the Internal Examiner prior to the Viva to confirm whether or not the Supervisor(s) should be present. If the candidate indicates that they would prefer the Supervisor(s) not to attend the Viva then they will not do so. It is the responsibility of the Internal Examiner to advise the Supervisor(s) accordingly.
The Supervisor(s) and the candidate are required to withdraw from the Viva before a formal decision on the thesis is taken.
If the Supervisor(s) do not attend the Viva they should be contactable during the examination in order that the Examiners may, if they wish, seek clarification on any particular issues, which may have arisen in relation to the work submitted.
Viva Voce decision and examiners' decision
The Viva Voce (Oral Examination) - arrangements
The Viva Voce can be held face to face, or online via MS Teams. This decision will be informed by the Postgraduate Researcher at the stage the examining team is appointed, and all those involved in the examination must be in agreement about how it is delivered.
It is the responsibility of the Internal Examiner to make arrangements for the Viva Voce (Viva for short, but also known as Oral Examination) and to confirm the arrangements directly with the student (for staff candidates, the Internal Chair will make and confirm the arrangements). The External Examiner/s should also be fully consulted and informed of the arrangements. In terms of the presence of the supervisor, please see below:
- Under the Regulations for Research Degrees, the Supervisor(s) are permitted to attend the Viva and may be invited by the Examiners to offer comments at any appropriate point during the examination process. The Supervisor(s) may request permission to clarify a particular point during the course of the Viva.
- The candidate should be consulted by the Internal Examiner prior to the Viva to confirm whether or not the Supervisor(s) should be present. If the candidate indicates that they would prefer the Supervisor(s) not to attend the Viva then they will not do so. It is the responsibility of the Internal Examiner to advise the Supervisor(s) accordingly.
- The Supervisor(s) and the candidate are required to withdraw from the Viva before a formal decision on the thesis is taken.
- If the Supervisor(s) do not attend the Viva they should be contactable during the examination in order that the Examiners may, if they wish, seek clarification on any particular issues, which may have arisen in relation to the work submitted.
Please note, the Postgraduate Research Administration Team may not know the Viva arrangements upon submission of your thesis, your Faculty PGR Administrator will contact you to confirm arrangements via your bradford.ac.uk e-mail account once these have been confirmed by the internal examiner.
The Viva will normally be arranged within eight weeks of submission of thesis to the Postgraduate Research Administration team. In terms of timeliness of examination, supervisors and PGRs should refer to the Appointment of Examiners process, which emphasises that it is essential that examiners are approved in advance to formal thesis submission.
The Viva is normally held on University premises or online, although it may be held away from the University if particular circumstances make such an arrangement more convenient for those concerned. Please note, all parties involved with the Viva must agree to this taking place off site and permission must also be sought by the Internal Examiner (or Internal Chair in the case of staff candidates) in advance from Faculty Postgraduate Research Sub-Committee. The Faculty Postgraduate Research Sub-Committee request should include confirmation that all parties agree, indicate a suitable venue and confirm that examination conditions can still be met.
At the Viva, the External Examiner will act as the principal examiner. The Internal Examiner will assist the External Examiner and exercise a major function in ensuring that the Viva is conducted in a supportive atmosphere with due regard to relevant University procedures, policies and regulations. The latter functions are met for Staff Candidates by the Internal Chair.
The length of the Viva is at the discretion of the examiners. Typically it may last from 1.5 to 3 hours. Only in exceptional circumstances should the candidate be examined for in excess of 3 hours and regular comfort breaks should be offered.
Examining teams will also be informed prior to the examination if there are any reasonable adjustments that need to be accommodated based on the PGRs Learner Support Profile.
Viva Outcomes
After the assessment has been completed students will be told informally by the examiners the outcome of the examination. A joint report will be prepared by the examiners and students will receive a copy, and formal notification of the outcome of their examination once it has been approved at Faculty Postgraduate Research Sub-Committee.
More information of possible outcomes is given in the Research Regulations - (this also includes information about the Appeals process for Research Students).
Lastly, the most common pass outcomes - minor corrections, major corrections and re-submit revised thesis - together with follow-up processes are further summarised below.
Amendments to the thesis
The detailed requirements for amendments to your thesis are set out in the Postgraduate Research Degree Regulations.
Please pay particular attention to the points below:
Minor Corrections
Normally minor corrections (outcome 1.2 on the Examiner's report form) are those which are undertaken to the satisfaction of the Internal Examiner only within 3 months.
When submitting the revised thesis for consideration by the internal examiner, candidates must:
1. Provide a table of changes linked to the list of corrections provided by the examiners, indicating where in the thesis these have been adapted, e.g. page number.
2. Clearly demonstrate throughout the thesis where amendments have been applied, e.g. track changes, highlight text etc.
Major Corrections
Normally, major corrections (outcome 1.3 on the Examiners report form) are those which are undertaken to the satisfaction of the Internal Examiner and External Examiner within 6 months and may include more extensive editorial changes to the thesis which would not require re-examination by the External Examiner. On the specific recommendation of the examiners, this period may be extended up to 12 months.
When submitting the revised thesis for consideration by the internal and external examiners, candidates must:
1. Provide a table of changes linked to the list of corrections provided by the examiners, indicating where in the thesis these have been adapted, e.g. page number.
2. Clearly demonstrate throughout the thesis where amendments have been applied, e.g. track changes, highlight test etc.
Supervision
Supervisors have a duty of care to the student in providing advice where the examiners have recommended that amendments be made to the thesis. This will be equally applicable in the case of a student permitted to revise and re-present the thesis and in the case of a student required to make minor or major corrections.
Revision of the Thesis
Where a student is required to revise and re-present the thesis, the Internal Examiner should forward to the Principal Supervisor a full statement describing those areas in which the thesis was found to be unsatisfactory and the Supervisor will then pass this to the student and give advice as appropriate. Please note that we make a charge to students who are required to be re-examined. At 2020/21 rates this is £500.
Additional points are:
- The Director of Postgraduate Research must ensure appropriate supervision is available during the revision stage.
- Where additional work has to be done (e.g. processing new data) the Faculty/Department must ensure the student is provided with appropriate facilities.
- The Postgraduate Research Administration team are responsible for submitting revised theses to examiners - no person is allowed to send a copy of a revised thesis to the examiners prior to formal re-submission.
Final Submission
Deadlines
As a general principal, the sign-off of all examination paperwork (this is arranged by academic Schools) has to be with Postgraduate Research Administration team at least 3-4 weeks before the start of any graduation ceremony period. The exact deadline in every academic year for each set of ceremonies is always circulated to Faculties/Departments in advance. Furthermore, Faculties (via the PGR Administration Team) select students for ceremony, as they will be aware of submission/viva dates (in advance of examination).
Please see 'Preparation for Graduation for the deadline for the next ceremony.
Final Submission
Final submission of doctoral theses are now electronic.
Submissions will be made electronically via CANVAS. Please contact your Faculty PGR inbox for more detail. N.B. Electronic submissions must follow University style and layout guidelines. An example thesis can be viewed in the Documents section of the Research Students Sharepoint site. In addition, we will require the electronic thesis/deposit agreement form.
- One permanently bound copy of the thesis with hard covers - perfectly bound (fixed black binding in which the sheets are sewn) with cloth-covered boards in black as per Research Regulations.
- One electronic copy of thesis, this electronic version will ultimately be available online unless restrictions apply. Please ensure the electronic copy is in Word format only.
  -- It is a requirement that the Word copy is in an editable format for administrative purposes, for example, to enable any unauthorised copyright material to be removed for the purposes of electronic publication.
- An Electronic Thesis/Deposit Agreement Form granting the University the right to make the thesis available online
Permanently bound copies of the thesis
There is no regulatory requirement to submit a Hard Bound version of your final thesis, however, should you wish to provide a print copy to the library or your faculty you may do so. However, we would ask that you consider the environmental and cost implications of doing so first. You will still need to submit an electronic version as noted above, in addition to the below:
- One permanently bound copy of the thesis with hard covers - perfectly bound (fixed black binding in which the sheets are sewn) with cloth-covered boards in black as per Research Regulations.
Please refer to the section on style and layout.
Front cover and spine
The lettering on the permanently bound copy is done in gold on black hard covers.
The bound thesis (both temporary and permanent) must have on the front cover:
- the initials and surname of the candidate as per first binding;
- the title of the thesis (exactly the same as the approved title);
- the name of the degree for which the thesis is submitted;
- the year of submission.
On the spine there must be the following printed downwards so that it can be read when the book lies flat, face upwards:
- the name of the candidate (surname or family name and initials); 
- the name of the degree;
- the year of submission.
The approved sub-title must appear on the title page underneath the title, but not on the cover of the thesis. Please do not include any other qualification titles you have received within the title/cover pages of the thesis.
Preparation for graduation
Requirements for Research Students
All Postgraduate Researchers MUST
- Submit one Final electronic copy of their thesis in Word format via CANVAS.
- Submit a electronic deposit agreement form.
Failure to do the above will result in the withholding of your degree certificate until the above requirements are met
N.B Bound thesis are no longer required and final thesis submissions will be made electronically only.
Next Step
Ceremony invites will be sent out from the Ceremony and Events team via email in due course.
Information on gown hire; photographs; tickets and Frequently Asked Questions can be found at our graduation website.
