SOURCE_URL: https://unibradfordac.sharepoint.com/sites/career-and-employability-services-intranet/SitePages/Assessment-centres.aspx

Career and Employability Services
Assessment centres
An assessment centre is often the last stage in the recruitment process for many graduate roles. If it is part of the process, you will usually be invited to attend an assessment centre after the initial application stage. It may last from half a day to several days, and provides an opportunity for the employers to get to know more about you, to see how you perform at certain tasks and judge your interaction with others. Employers are checking to make sure you have the right competencies and are a good fit for the role.
An assessment centre may involve:
- A range of assessment techniques (such as interviews, psychometric testing, individual and group exercises) allowing the assessors to see you handling different situations.
- Assessment on a number of competencies or skills such as leadership ability, team working, interpersonal skills, communication and problem-solving.
- Several candidates being observed together by the assessors.
Preparation for assessment centres
See our interviews page for details on how to research the company, job and sector. There is more information on assessment centres on our online careers resource Build My Career and check our workshop schedule for upcoming mock assessment centre sessions.
What to expect- FAQs
How long is the assessment centre?
- You will be told in advance the timings of the event so that you can make travel plans.
- Assessment centres are focused events lasting from a short session (of up to four hours) to two full days.
How many candidates will be invited?
- Up to 30 other candidates could be in attendance, but you may be divided up into small groups for particular exercises.
- Some employers invite much smaller numbers.
What do I wear, and is there anything I should bring with me?
- Dress as you would if you were attending an interview.
- You will be told if you need to bring anything extra e.g. clothing for outdoor exercises.
- You may receive a checklist from the employer stating what to bring to the assessment centre. Make sure you bring everything listed, including any official documents requested, otherwise you may not be able to take part. You should also bring a notebook and pen, and a watch to time yourself during tests and activities
How do I behave?
- You will be under scrutiny as part of the selection procedure at all times, so act naturally but be aware of the need for good manners, polite behaviour and to act in a pleasant and friendly way towards other candidates and company personnel.
- Try to relax and be yourself, but ensure that you demonstrate good manners, polite behaviour and act in a pleasant and friendly way towards candidates and company personnel. Assessors often ask for the opinions of the staff who have met you.
- Do not treat others as if they are in competition with you, as you will also be assessed on your ability to get on with your potential colleagues.
- You will be expected to socialise well with other candidates as well as with management, and you are likely to be observed at break times and at social events. If alcohol is available, don’t feel obliged to drink and don’t overdo it; it will be noted, and you may regret it.
Do they pay travelling expenses?
- Most do. If so, you will be informed in the invitation letter.
- If expenses are not mentioned, it is acceptable to make enquiries.
- Make sure you keep receipts.
- Some organisations impose a spending limit i.e. second-class rail or the cost of petrol plus hotel accommodation of up to £80.
- If expenses aren't covered you will need to decide if you want/can afford to go ahead.
What if I have a disability?
- Make the employer aware of any needs you have so that appropriate adjustments can be made.
- These could include extra time given to candidates with dyslexia to complete activities, adjustments to the physical environment, specialist equipment, materials in alternative formats, etc.
- Contact the recruiter as soon as possible to discuss your individual requirements.
Types of activities
Below are some of the more common tasks you may face as part of your assessment centre. You may not have to do all these activities and you may encounter tasks and activities not listed here.
Presentation by the employer
Often, an assessment centre will start with a presentation by the employer describing the organisation, their graduate scheme and career progression opportunities. Generally, you are not assessed during this activity but pay close attention to what is said as it may be useful to you at a later stage (perhaps during one of the activities). Look interested and ask questions too.
Ice-breaker exercises
These are initial exercises to get everyone working together. These might involve a short presentation or group task to build rapport.
Group exercises
In these exercises, your group may be given a problem to solve with a specific objective. It usually isn’t straightforward, and every approach may have disadvantages as well as advantages. The problem may be a physical puzzle to solve, such as building a bridge, or a theoretical one, such as a scenario-based emergency situation where you are presented with facts of the case and must logically work out the best option.
Committee exercise / debates
Within a committee exercise, group members are designated roles and the task may be to come up with a consensus plan that the committee agrees on and puts forward. In this case, the topic might be very vague, so the discussion has to be kept focused if the committee is to achieve something. Within a debate, a topic question may be given and the members are free to discuss the pros and cons of different angles concerning it.
Case study
This could be a group or individual exercise in which you are given official reports, tables of figures, newspaper cuttings, memos, etc. and a problem to solve.
Alternatively, the group may be given a business case study, which has to be analysed and understood before making suggestions for subsequent action. You may be asked to make a presentation about the key issues and suggested strategy, demonstrating that you or the group can respond well to assessors posing questions.
There is often not one specific ‘correct’ answer in this exercise. Recruiters are assessing your ability to analyse problems, prioritise tasks and put forward effective arguments with clarity and tact.
In-tray / e-tray exercise
This task is designed to simulate the administrative features of a job. For example, you may be given a pile of papers representing a post tray containing emails, faxes, memos, letters, telephone messages, reports and computer printouts. Alternatively it could be entirely PC-based (known as an e-tray exercise), where you may be asked to organise and reply to emails in Outlook, amend an Excel spreadsheet, or summarise an article in Word (or a combination of all the above and more).
Report writing / essay
You may be required to analyse problems and present your summary and findings in a written report or essay. You will be assessed on how you use your skills to present focused information that others will understand, the reasoning for your decisions and to test your written skills.
Presentation
This may be about a case study (see above), or about a specific topic given to you either prior to or on the day, with a short time to prepare. These can be individual, or in groups. If an individual presentation is required, but no specific presentation topic given ahead of schedule, it’s worth having a presentation prepared about a topic of your choice. If you know that the subject will be given at the assessment centre, think about how to structure a presentation, consider what type of visual aids you might use, and find out what resources are available.
To prepare for a presentation about yourself, use the Elevator Pitch Builder on Build My Career and the presentation skills advice and information from Academic Skills.
Psychometric Tests (follow link for detailed information)
Interview
Usually all Assessment Centres include an interview with one assessor or a panel.
- The competencies the recruiters have identified as being important for the job will usually provide a framework for the interview.
- Be prepared to have your answers challenged as the interviewer(s) probe you further, test your ability to think on your feet and put across your ideas effectively.
- See the interviews section for more information.
Competencies being assessed
During the activities at the assessment centre, there will be a number of recruiters present making notes about you and the other candidates. They will be looking for various competencies necessary for the role, and the below table describes some of these competencies, and at what what stage they are likely to be assessed.
Competency
Activity
- Adaptability, creativity, initiative
  - Working in group exercises where creative solutions to a problem are required, in tray / e-tray exercises where new tasks may be added during the exercise.
- Leadership, assertiveness, confidence, negotiation
  - Group exercises, discussions and case studies where groups need to agree on a course of action.
- Written communication, basic computer competency, IT skills, information handling, numeracy
  - All written and practical tests
- Business and commercial awareness
  - Report writing and essays, presentations, case studies and discussions.
- Problem solving, analytical thinking, clarity of thinking, judgement, logical thinking, reasoning
  - Many of the activities will require you to demonstrate solid problem solving skills.
- Planning and organising, strategy, action planning, prioritising
  - In-tray and e-tray exercises, debates and case studies.
- Teamwork, co-operation, interpersonal relationships
  - All group exercises, plus your general behaviour throughout the assessment centre.
- Time management, working under pressure
  - Many of the tasks will be challenging and strictly timed.
- Verbal communication, presentation skills
  - Presentations, group discussions and interviews.
Please note this list is not definitive as each assessment will be designed to test your suitability for a specific role, so read the job description carefully for the kinds of competencies you will be required to demonstrate.
For more on competencies, see our developing your skills pages.
Top tips
- You are likely to do better at some activities than others but don’t worry too much - a strong performance in one area can balance out a weaker performance in another. Although there may be a minimum score set for each of the competencies the employer is looking for, you don’t have to do brilliantly in everything to be selected.
- Always pay careful attention to any instructions - if in doubt, ask for clarification.
- Give everything your best shot – get fully involved in group activities, be assertive and persuasive but diplomatic too. In group activities, show that you are listening to others by smiling, nodding and maintaining eye contact.
-  Remember that assessors are looking at how you behave, so even if you don't complete a task, you could still be judged favourably if you were positive and showed good team working skill.
- Acknowledge and build on good points made by other group members as well as putting forward your own case.
- Friendly conversation with other candidates will help you to relax – all (or none) of you may be successful at this particular assessment centre.
After the assessment centre
- Remember, your assessment centre finishes only when you leave the organisation, so be professional at all times. Finally, reflect on how it went. Highlight what went well and what could have been improved on, and then think what action you can take to improve in future.
- If your assessment centre has gone well and you are very keen on the organisation, you may want to send an email telling the recruiter how much you enjoyed the experience (and remember to use a formal and professional writing style, i.e. full sentences, and don't use abbreviations, slang or text speak).
Next steps and further information
- Build My Career: There is detailed information on a range of companies and their interview processes, along with assessment centre tips and practical advice.
- Advice: If you'd like more information on preparing for assessment centres and the tasks you might face, feel free to book an appointment with a career consultant.
- Workshops: We run mock assessment centres as part of our careers workshop programme. Check the schedule and book your place.

--- SOURCE SEPARATOR ---

SOURCE_URL: https://unibradfordac.sharepoint.com/sites/registry-and-student-admin-intranet/SitePages/Student-Records.aspx

Registry and Student Administration
Student Records
We deal with all student record enquiries working with students, faculties and other relevant departments to resolve issues and queries.
This includes:
- ensuring student records are accurate and up to date
- processing exam results through the pass list process
- processing appeal outcomes
- sending change of circumstance information to the Student Loans Company
- general student record queries including confirmation of end dates
Contact us
- Email: records@bradford.ac.uk
 -Phone: +(44) 1274 238911
Frequently asked questions
When do you confirm my registration to Student Finance?
Both Undergraduate and Postgraduate students need a registration confirmation sending to Student Finance at the beginning of each academic year. This informs them that the student has registered for the upcoming session.
For new students, your registration confirmation is sent after you’ve completed the Welcome / ID check. For returning students, this will be after you have completed your re-enrolment task in e:Vision.
The confirmation is sent electronically to Student Finance, and this will release your maintenance loan. You will usually receive your payment within five working days.
I have an approved Student Finance loan at another university, but I am now studying at Bradford - what do I need to do?
Please send us your Student Finance student support number (SSN) with your name and UB number (you can find your SSN on your entitlement letter or Student Finance portal).
We will then submit a ‘Transfer In’ change of circumstance notification to inform Student Finance that you are now studying at Bradford.
I am transferring to another university - do you need to inform Student Finance?
No. It is the responsibility of the incoming university to submit a ‘Transfer In' change of circumstance notification to Student Finance.
I want to transfer / suspend / withdraw from my course
Before you make any changes please ensure that you have discussed your decision with your personal academic tutor. If you are an overseas student, please also contact the visa support team for further advice.
If you wish to transfer you need to get approval from the new program team before you complete the change of circumstance form in e:Vision.
I have discussed my options, how do I complete a change of circumstance?
1. Sign into e:Vision.
2. Select the Change of Circumstances tab.
3. From the drop down menu make the relevant selection.
4. Follow the instructions to complete the process.
I am suspended and want to return to my studies, what do I do?
If you are currently suspended and wish to return to your studies, please contact your program admin team on 01274 238124.
Once your request has been approved by your faculty your record will be updated. The Records team will submit a Change of Circumstance notification to Student Finance if applicable.
Please note: Student Finance will aim to process this in 20 working days in off peak periods and 30 working days during peak periods from August to the end of November.
I think my end date is incorrect.
Your end date is set when you start and reflects the date you complete the taught element of your programme. It does not usually change unless you need to suspend or repeat a part of your studies.
Please note we do not extend end dates for students doing supplementary assessments or who have extensions to submission dates.
My end date has passed and I need access to complete my supplementary assessment.
Please contact your programme administrator on 01274 238124 who will contact the library and access control to request extended access on your behalf.
I have changed my name, what do I do?
Please send us evidence of your name change so we can update your record. This could include a marriage certificate, deed poll, or updated ID document.
If you are an overseas student your request will be referred to the visa team before the change can be made.
I have been withdrawn and want to make an appeal, what do I do.
If you wish to make an appeal please contact the complaints and appeals team on complaintsandappeals@bradford.ac.uk.
I am studying a course which has integrated placements. I have made a student finance application and the SLC are now asking me for evidence of those placements, what do I do?
When making your finance application, you should only indicate that you are on placement if you are registered on a sandwich course undertaking a full sandwich year placement that year. This is because sandwich years attract a reduced fee liability compared to normal study years. If your course has placements which are integrated and alongside your taught modules, you do not need to indicate you are on placement.
If you are unsure, please check with the SLC, or contact money@bradford.ac.uk who can advise on SLC applications.

Student Experience and Success
Overview
The Student Experience & Success Team aims to understand and support student success at the University of Bradford. This includes a range of activity to support student retention, engagement and transition.
We liaise and work with staff and students across the institution, conducting projects and research and providing specialist advice, activities and schemes.
All of our work draws on relevant pedagogy and is situated within evidence and best practice from the sector. Much of what we do links to institutional priorities including our Access and Participation Plan.
We particularly champion student-led activity and the role that students can play on their own and other students’ development. Peer support is therefore a priority focus for the team. Another of our key projects is to support student transition into university via our Step Up to HE programme.

--- SOURCE SEPARATOR ---

SOURCE_URL: https://unibradfordac.sharepoint.com/sites/student-experience-and-success-intranet/SitePages/Peer-Support.aspx

Student Experience and Success
Peer Support
Encouraging and enabling students to support each other and work together in their learning community is a key part of our approach to the student experience and to student success at Bradford.
We therefore offer a number of opportunities for peer support. All are based on best practice from the sector, are situated within a learning and teaching context and so are underpinned by relevant pedagogy and practice that fosters active learning and student empowerment, and are evaluated on an annual basis to determine impact and to ensure they remain beneficial to students.
Schemes primarily aim to support:
- Student transition into/through and engagement with university and study life
- The employability and development of students that lead the peer support
- A sense of community and belonging for students
Students are encouraged and supported to take ownership of the peer support schemes, with students working together to decide what to cover in the sessions. This approach offers a unique learning environment in that it is personalised and gives students informal opportunities, led by them, to work through needs, ideas and questions together.
Our schemes can offer support in a variety of contexts, such as at programme level for particular groups of students.
Peer support currently on offer includes:
- Peer Assisted Learning (PAL), including Peer Led Lectures (PLL) and Peer Support Sessions
- Peer Mentoring

SOURCE_URL: https://unibradfordac.sharepoint.com/sites/timetabling-intranet/SitePages/Check-in.aspx

Timetabling, Attendance and Room Bookings
Student Engagement Guidance
Swiping Misconduct
Students should be aware that activities such as the below may constitute fraudulent behaviour under Ordinance 16 of the University and result in sanctions under the Student Disciplinary Procedure:
- Use, or possession, of your Student ID by an individual other than yourself.
- Swiping into classes and then leaving the session.
- Other activities designed to manipulate student attendance data.
How is student attendance recorded?
Taught Undergraduate & Postgraduate:
- The University provides you with a student card.
- Use this card to swipe into your timetabled sessions.
- Card readers are fixed near teaching room entrances (lecture theatres, labs, seminar rooms).
- Swipe your card to check in using your student ID.
- If you swipe your card more than 15 minutes too early or late, you will not receive a present mark.
- If you forget your card, you will not be able to sign in using a manual register. From September 2024, this will only be available for rooms without a scanner.
Research Students:
- There is a seperate process for PGRs. You can find information on this within the Student Engagement Guide.
- Specific information on engagement requirements can be found on the PGR sharepoint page.
Do you have an issue with your UB card?
If you have lost or damaged your card, or you don't get the green tick on the card reader when you swipe then please go to the MyBradford desk. They can issue you with a replacement card (this will incur a small replacement fee) or re-activate it if needed.
Do you need a copy of your attendance?
To obtain a copy of your attendance please email the relevant programme admin team for your faculty or the Student Engagement Officers.
- Engineering & Informatics: fedt-attendance@bradford.ac.uk
- Health Studies fhs-attendance@bradford.ac.uk
- Life Sciences fls-attendance@bradford.ac.uk
- Management & Law fml-attendance@bradford.ac.uk
- Social Sciences fss-attendance@bradford.ac.uk
- Student Engagement Officers studentengagementofficers@bradford.ac.uk
What happens if I don't attend?
Students are monitored using the Student Engagement Process. This is a three stage approach which aims to re-engage students and challenge low attendance.
Low attendance and engagement may lead to:
- Student withdrawal recommendations.
- UKVI student visas may lose sponsorship which affects graduate visa routes.
- Impact on student loans, fees, and other financial aspects..
Please review the Student Engagement policy and guide.
Professionally accredited programmes such as nursing, have specific guidance on attendance requirements. You can consult their programme handbook or programme team.
Placement Attendance is not recorded through the card swipe in machines on campus. Any absences or issues with attendance must be reported to the placement team. More guidance can be found here
Student support
- For queries relating to how attendance can affect student loans, fees, or other financial aspects of students' study contact the Student Life Services
- Student Life Services also provide support for students with disabilities, welfare, counselling and financial support.
- For queries relating to overseas students please contact the Visa Support Team on 01274 23 6900 or visasupport@bradford.ac.uk
Please note: Remember to carry your student ID card for all taught sessions. Without it, you won’t be able to check in and may be marked absent.

Timetabling, Attendance and Room Bookings
Reporting Absence / Authorised Absence
Reporting Absences
You are expected to attend all scheduled teaching activities as shown on your academic timetable.
If you cannot attend several sessions due to unavoidable reasons (such as illness or emergencies), you may wish to notify your lecturer or module leader promptly.
To have your absence authorised you will need to complete the absence request form. You should carefully include the dates of absences, the reason and any evidence to support your request.
Not all absences will be approved as an authorised absence, therefore refer to the guidelines
. Keep in mind that authorised absences will still appear on the student’s attendance record as absences, and submitting the absence reporting form does not mean immediate approval.
If your absence extends over several weeks, we may advise you to consider suspending your studies instead.
Should you require further support or wish to discuss any barriers to your engagement with your course then please contact your Personal Academic Tutor or the Student Engagement Officers.
Reporting Absence Email Addresses:
- Engineering & Informatics fedt-attendance@bradford.ac.uk

- Life Sciences fls-attendance@bradford.ac.uk

MyBradford
MyBradford - change of circumstances
Change of Circumstances
Changing or transferring your course
First, you should discuss this with your Faculty, who will need to release you from your course. If you wish to change into a different Faculty, you will also have to speak to the admission tutor for the new course.
If you are accepted onto your new course, you will have to inform the Student Loan Company or your sponsor. Once your course transfer is agreed, we will also write to your funding body to finalise your transfer.
If you would like to change to a new course you will need to inform your Faculty by submitting your request via e:Vision:
1. Log onto e:Vision
2. Click on the Change of Circumstances link at the bottom.
3. In the drop down menu, select Programme change and click Next.
4. Follow the instructions on e:Vision to complete the process.
Please complete the Programme Transfer Agreement form.
Suspending your course
If you would like to suspend your course you should first discuss with your faculty why you want to suspend, as they may be able to help.
If you decide that you would like to suspend your course, you need to contact your personal academic tutor and after discussion with them please visit e:Vision:
1. Log onto e:Vision
2. Click on the Change of Circumstances link at the bottom.
3. In the drop down menu, select Suspend study.
4. Follow the instructions on e:Vision to complete the process.
Withdrawing from your course
If you would like to leave the University, you should first discuss with your present department why you want to leave, as they may be able to help.
If you decide that you would like to leave, you need to contact your personal academic tutor and after discussion with them please visit e:Vision:
1. Log onto e:Vision
2. Click on the Change of Circumstances link at the bottom.
3. In the drop down menu, select Withdrawal and click Next.
4. Follow the instructions on e:Vision to complete the process.
Extenuating Circumstances
During your studies, you might face exceptional circumstances which impact your ability to complete assessments and/or perform at your expected level. This might include, for example, serious illness or distressing personal issues (e.g. bereavement). It is important that you tell someone at the University as soon as you can about any such issues so you can get the advice and support you need.
If you feel that you have exceptional circumstances that may impact your performance and ability to complete assessments, please visit th Extenuating Circumstances page.

Health and Safety
Security
To ensure your safety and security whilst on campus we have a dedicated SIA approved security team who can provide both security and first aid assistance.
The Security team are on Campus 24 hours per day and conduct regular patrols of all areas of campus throughout the day and night. Should you have any concerns regarding suspicious persons or security related incidents please contact our team who will be happy to assist with your enquiry.
For your protection, our campus has an extensive system of CCTV cameras, intruder alarms and access controls your safety and security.
To support our Security team, we also have a dedicated Higher Education Police Liaison Officer deployed at the University from West Yorkshire Police.
Security staff can be contacted by telephoning:
- General Enquiries + 44 (0)1274 236999 from a mobile or external telephone, or 6999 from an internal telephone.
- Emergencies + 44 (0)1274 238 888 from a mobile or external telephone, or 8888 from an internal telephone.
- Richmond Reception: Mon-Fri 08:30 - 17:30, + 44 (0)1274 234 894 from a mobile or external telephone, or 4894 from an internal telephone.
Crime related incidents can also be reported via ServiceNow using our Accidents, Incidents or Near Misses form. You can also report a security related crime by contacting our Security Team direct or by requesting a University member of Staff to report this on your behalf via ServiceNow.
Access to our campus is also controlled via an Access Control system, Staff and Students are provided with smart ID cards to enable them access via the smart card readers situated throughout campus.
In addition to our extensive security systems and patrols we also encourage you to download our SafeZone app which makes it easier for you to alert the on campus Security team.

SOURCE_URL: https://unibradfordac.sharepoint.com/sites/it-services-intranet/SitePages/mobile-security-advice-for-students.aspx

IT Services
Mobile security advice for students
Important
- Use a PIN or password to lock your device.
- If your device has a SIM inside you should also set a SIM lock. This is an additional 4 digit pin that controls usage of the SIM card independent of the device which prevents thieves from putting the SIM in another unlocked device to make calls on your account.
- Ensure your important data is backed up somewhere, such as a PC or in the cloud.
- If you have an Android device it is recommended that you install reputable anti-virus software on your device.
- If you have an Android device make sure it is set up with the Android device manager. This will enable you to remotely locate or wipe the device if it is lost or stolen.
- iOS users can do the same with iCloud and 'Find My iPhone' to track, lock and wipe devices.
- Always use a strong password for your app store account. These passwords are high value due to them usually being linked to an account with credit card details. Try to use a password that is at least 8 characters, and contains upper case, lower case, numbers, special characters such as punctuation and doesn't contain a word found in the dictionary.
- Security mark your device with an ultraviolet pen.
- Thieves operate on campus. Do not leave your valuables unattended for even a short time.
- Be aware of your surroundings when using your mobile device. If you are off campus and feel threatened you can go to one of the student safe spots for help.
- If the worst happens and your device is lost or stolen, wipe it and report it to your mobile phone network and the police as soon as you can.

SOURCE_URL: https://unibradfordac.sharepoint.com/sites/health-safety-and-wellbeing-intranet/SitePages/Asbestos.aspx

Health and Safety
Asbestos
Asbestos is a naturally occurring silicate fibrous material and although a popular building product in the past, the use and disposal of such materials is now heavily legislated.
Although present in small amounts, asbestos can be found within the infrastructure throughout campus and we maintain an extensive database of all known sources of asbestos.
Our team work in conjunction with colleagues in the Property & Campus Services Team to ensure that all asbestos related hazards have been assessed prior to the commencement of any scheduled works.
If you require an asbestos survey to be carried out by our team please complete the Asbestos Survey Request Form on ServiceNow
If you discover any damage to the building infrastructure this should be reported immediately to either the Property & Campus Services Team or Health and Safety Team on +44 (0)1274 236057 or email healthandsafety@bradford.ac.uk
For further information also view our Health and Safety Policies, Guidance and Procedures pages.

SOURCE_URL: https://unibradfordac.sharepoint.com/sites/language-centre-intranet/SitePages/Information-on-each-section-of-the-BASALT.aspx

Language Centre
Information on BASALT
Part 1 - Reading
- Reading 1 - Detailed reading
  - Duration: 40-45 minutes
  - Input: 1 text of 3-4 pages from a textbook or other source aimed at the general academic reader
  - Question Types:
    - Multiple choice
    - Short answer
    - Gap fill
  - Purpose: To test your ability to understand the key ideas and their supporting details in a text of general academic interest.
- Reading 2 - Skimming
  - Duration: 20 minutes
  - Input: 1 text of 3-4 pages from a textbook or other source aimed at the general academic reader
  - Question Types:
    - Matching 7-9 paragraph summaries with paragraphs
    - There are the same number of paragraph summaries and questions (no extras)
  - Purpose: To test your ability to read an academic text quickly to locate the main topics or sub-topics and understand their relationship.
Part 2 - Listening
- Duration: 10-13 minutes
- Input: A structured semi-authentic academic lecture on a topic of general academic interest
- Question Types:
  - Part 1: Completing a set of student lecture notes from the first half
  - Part 2: Taking notes on key ideas from the second half and answering open-ended questions
- Purpose:
  - Identify relevant details from a lecture (statistics or short information)
  - Identify the main ideas of a lecture
- Note: 3 minutes preparation time before the lecture and 15 minutes for answering/checking after.
Part 3 - Writing
- Duration: 65 minutes (15 minutes compulsory planning + 50 minutes writing)
- Question Types:
  - Two paragraphs (total 250-300 words)
  - No fixed essay formula, focus on using academic writing styles (problems, solutions, causes, effects, similarities, differences)
  - A list of ideas is provided (optional to use)
- Purpose:
  - Test ability to construct and develop coherent, logical ideas relevant to the question
  - Test accurate and appropriate language use
Part 4 - Speaking
- Duration:
  - 2 people: 6 minutes
  - 3 people: 9 minutes
  - 4 people: 12 minutes
- Question Types:
  - A question or set of questions on a topic of general academic interest
  - A list of ideas is provided (optional to use)
  - 5 minutes to prepare
- Purpose:
  - Test ability to express and develop coherent ideas logically
  - Test ability to interact with others in a seminar situation

SOURCE_URL: https://unibradfordac.sharepoint.com/sites/career-and-employability-services-intranet/SitePages/Graduate-Labour-Market-Information.aspx

Career and Employability Services
Graduate Labour Market Information
A knowledge of the labour market can help you answer questions such as:
- What jobs are out there?
- Who are the main employers in your field?
- What jobs are likely to be on the increase and where are they?
- Which areas of work are easier to get into?
- What skills do you need for now and the future?
Understanding what is going on with jobs and employers is called labour market information intelligence
The labour market
The labour market is constantly changing and evolving as a wide range of factors can have an effect on each industry and town or city, such as:
- new technologies (for example the automation of tasks)
- infrastructure (e.g. the HS2 rail link)
- politics and government policies (such as the UK leaving the EU)
- the ageing population (creating opportunities in care, plus the need for replacement of retiring workers)
- skills shortages in certain roles.
Also bear in mind that some industries are quite localised to a certain region, for example many banking and finance organisations have centres in London or Leeds.
Researching the industry you are interested in and being realistic about whether you are prepared to travel and relocate for work can help to focus in on suitable employers for your graduate career.
Below is more information about employment in the Bradford and the Leeds City region, plus further reading on researching the labour market to help with your career plans.
Graduate job options
If you are looking for graduate-level employment after graduation, these jobs generally fall into two categories:
1. Graduate training schemes: where organisations specifically recruit graduates straight from university, and recruitment usually begins in the autumn term of your final year. Graduate schemes typically offer a structured career path, training and development and a good starting salary. These are highly competitive and can have a rigorous application process, including assessment centres selection tests. Many graduate schemes require you to be prepared to move around the country (or even internationally) to experience different departments of the organisation as part of your training.
2. Direct job entry: graduate jobs require a degree from the start, either because the degree gives you the specific knowledge and qualifications to perform the role, or because employers value the skills and experience gained from a university education. You will be expected to learn on the job, and there may be less formal training than with a graduate scheme. These roles are generally widely advertised on job sites and in the press, usually with a timescale of around four weeks, and are not restricted to recent graduates. The starting salary may be lower than with a graduate scheme, but it is likely it will rise as you progress and gain experience.
Graduate schemes are more popular in certain industries, such as engineering and finance - a quick online search will give you an idea of what programmes are available in your subject area
About Bradford
Bradford is one city covering four towns - Shipley, Keighley, Bingley and Bradford. Within the four towns are 70 neighbourhoods and over half a million people. There are 15,000 businesses and an economy worth £8.3 billion, which is the 11th largest in the UK. Bradford is placed fifth for manufacturing and has branded itself the Producer City.
In Bradford, the biggest employers are the City Of Bradford Metropolitan District Council, the University of Bradford, Bradford College and the NHS, which together employ around a third of the workforce.
Morrisons also employ around 5000 people in Bradford, including at its headquarters in Thornbury.
The largest single sector is retail which employs 1 in 10 workers, and the opening of the Broadway retail and leisure development in November 2015 has added an estimated 2500 jobs in this area.
Note: The bigger the employer, the more opportunities on offer and increased chances that a job might come up.
Key companies in Bradford
- Yorkshire Building Society
- Morrisons
- UKAR
- DENSO
- KONE
- Hallmark
- Sonoco
- BorgWarner
- OTTO
- Gordons LLP
- Kelda Water
- BASF

- Provident Finance Group

- Arris

Many of these companies have offered job opportunities for Bradford graduates so are well worth investigating.
There are also some smaller Bradford-based companies that are worth considering, including:
- Radio Design based in Shipley, they were nominated for a Queens award for engineering innovation in 2011.
- Emerald leading independent publishers of global research based in Bingley and with offices worldwide.
- Knightsbridge Furniture
- Klinger sealing products for oil, gas, chemical industries
- Seabrook Crisps
Smaller organisations like these are more likely to offer direct-entry roles rather than graduate schemes.

SOURCE_URL: https://unibradfordac.sharepoint.com/sites/career-and-employability-services-intranet/SitePages/Graduate-jobs.aspx

Career and Employability Services
Graduate jobs
Lots of graduate job opportunities are advertised from the autumn of your final year for programmes that begin in the summer, so it's important to plan ahead to make sure you are maximising your chances of success in finding a role you're happy with. However, many graduate schemes accept graduates one or two years after finishing and there are other routes into graduate-level employment.
Types of graduate employment
- Graduate jobs are those which usually require a degree from the start, either because the degree gives you the specific knowledge and qualifications to do a job, or because employers value the skills and experience gained from a university education.
- Graduate schemes are training programmes over a set period, usually one to two years, which offer work experience and training within a company with the opportunity for ongoing employment afterwards (providing you pass the training).
- Internships offer work experience and training but unlike graduate schemes do not necessarily end in a formal job offer. However, they can be a good way to improve your employability skills and find out whether a job is right for you without signing up for a long period of time. Be aware that some internships are unpaid; think carefully about applying for these. See more on the law on unpaid internships on TARGETjobs.
Below is a range of sources for job opportunities arranged by sector and your faculty. Please feel free to come and see us to find out more.
Getting started
There's a few things you can do to begin your search
- Take a look at our jobs database
- You can book an appointment with us to talk about your options
- Check out our Facebook page for our Monday's Graduate Jobs & News

- Guardian Jobs

- LinkedIn
- Local and regional press, e.g. Yorkshire Post, Telegraph and Argus
- Magnet.me
- Reed
- Totaljobs
Graduate-focused sites:
- All About Careers
- Bright Network
- Careermap
- Debut
- Graduate Recruitment Bureau
- GraduateJobs.Com
- Graduateland
- Highered
- Milkround
- Prospects
- TARGETjobs
- The Times Top 100 Graduate Employers
- Yorkshire Graduates
- Jobs Go Public
Researching your career
As well as searching via job websites, looking at Labour Market Information (LMI) can help you make realistic plans for your career by giving you a better understanding of what kinds of jobs are available and where they are.
You can also search for businesses and organisations by location and sector using resources such as Kompass, and Crunchbase is a platform for finding business information about private and public companies.

SOURCE_URL: http://unibradfordac.sharepoint.com/sites/registry-and-student-admin-intranet/SitePages/Programme-Administration-Teams.aspx

Registry and Student Administration
Undergraduate and Postgraduate Taught Courses
Programme Administration Teams support the delivery of your courses, ensuring that relevant administrative processes are managed correctly and helping to resolve problems. We primarily manage:
- Enrolling students on the correct modules for their programme of study.
- Processing and release of results to students.
- Processing applications for changes to modules, programmes or modes of study.
- Processing applications for extensions or extenuating circumstances.
- Ensuring access to assignment submission points on Canvas.
- Servicing Boards of Examiners and Assessment Committees.
Semester Dates 2024/5
You must ensure you are available for classes, or assessments, on these dates:
Semester Dates
- Semester 1
  - Teaching weeks: Monday 23 September 2024 – Friday 13 December 2024
  - Exam period: Monday 6 January 2025 – Friday 17 January 2025
- Semester 2
  - Teaching weeks: Monday 20 January 2025 – Friday 11 April 2025
  - Exam period: Monday 28 April 2025 – Friday 9 May 2025
- Semester 3 (For applicable courses only)
  - Teaching weeks: Monday 19 May 2025 – Friday 8 August 2025
  - Exam period: Monday 11 August 2025 – Friday 22 August 2025
- Supplementary Assessment
  - Teaching weeks: Not applicable
  - Exam period: Monday 21 July 2025 – Friday 1 August 2025
  - Note: This period includes reassessments for Extenuating Circumstances, failed modules, and coursework resubmissions.
If you believe you will need to be absent from classes during Teaching Weeks you should follow the Absence Reporting guidance.
If you believe you will need to be absent during an Exam Period you should speak to your PAT as soon as possible. You may need to apply for extenuating circumstances if you will miss an examination, however there are only limited situations in which this can be approved.
Changes to optional modules
Where your programme contains optional modules, you may opt to make changes to these, but you must do so by the relevant deadline.
These deadlines are set to ensure you have not missed too much teaching on your new module, and are in a position to be able to catch up. These deadlines are dependent on when your module is running:
- SEM1 or ACYR: Friday 4th October 2024 at 12: 00 noon.
- SEM2, NSYR or FLYR: Friday 31st January 2025 at 12: 00 noon.
- SEM3: Friday 30th May 2025 at 12: 00 noon.
Boards of Examiners and Results Release 2024/5
Once assessments have taken place these must be marked, undergo second consideration by another member of staff, and review by the relevant External Examiner.
Once this has taken place, module results go to the relevant Assessment Committees for review.
Finally, Boards of Examiners confirm an overall decision on a student's award, or progression to the next stage of study. As such, release and approval of results often takes a number of weeks.
Key release dates for the 2024/5 academic year are below. Results will be released on or before these dates:
- 2023/4 Academic year results (Taught Postgraduate Students finishing in September 2024): Monday 18th November 2024, by 17: 00.

SOURCE_URL: http://unibradfordac.sharepoint.com/sites/registry-and-student-admin-intranet/SitePages/Programme-Administration-FAQa.aspx

Registry and Student Administration
Programme Administration
Programme Administration - frequently asked questions
I want to know how to contact a Programme Leader
To find a Programme Leader you can:
- Check your Programme Handbook on Canvas (if you need your current Programme Leader)
- Contact your Faculty's Programme Administration Team by email
- Ask staff at MyBradford
If you want to change programmes, remember you need to get approval from the leader of the programme you want to change onto.
I want to change my programme
If you are considering changing your Programme, it is important to first of all consult your Personal Academic Tutor (PAT). They will be able to give you initial guidance.
You should also then discuss the possibility with the Programme Leader of the programme you would like to move onto to understand what is, and isn't, possible.
You should be aware that:
- It is not always possible to change programme, there are specific entry requirements for many programmes that anyone wishing to study it must meet.
- If you wish to change your programme during the current Academic Year, you will need to make your request (with Programme Leader approval) before the end of the first week of teaching. If not, you will have missed too much of the course to be able to change now and will have to start your new programme in the next Academic Year.
- Please be aware that, even where a request is submitted within the first week of teaching, there is no guarantee it will be possible to change during the current year.
- If you are studying an Undergraduate course, and have completed one or more stages of study, you should be aware that in most cases you will need to restart in Stage 1 of a new programme and should consider the funding implications if you choose to do this.
- If you are studying on a Student Visa you must make sure to speak to the Visa Support Team as changing your programme may have implications for your visa sponsorship.
Further information, and the relevant forms, are available here: Transfers, Suspensions and Withdrawals (sharepoint.com)
I want to change one of my modules
Some modules are compulsory, and you will not be able to make changes to them. Some modules are optional, and you will be able to select from a number of different options - if you have already made a selection on e:Vision, and now wish to change this, you should e-mail your Faculty Programme Administration Team. 
Please be aware that there are deadlines to make changes and no changes may be made after these dates. In addition, some modules may not have sufficient space to accommodate additional students.
If you have not yet selected your modules on e:Vision you should make sure to sign in and make a selection as soon as possible. If you are struggling, or experiencing problems doing this, you should either email your Faculty Programme Administration Team (including a screenshot of your problem) or attend a drop-in at MyBradford with your Faculty Team.
I want to check which modules I am currently enrolled on
To check which modules you are currently enrolled on, you should sign in to e:Vision and go to the 'Modules and Enrolments' page from the menu. This will show you a list of your enrolled modules.
Normally, Taught Postgraduate students should have a total of 180 credits, including their Dissertation module. Undergraduate students normally study 120 credits per year.
Please note: Students starting a Taught Postgraduate Programme in January will not have their final semester's (September - December) modules shown on their record initially. These will be added later in the year, and you will receive an email if you need to select any optional modules.
Do I have to select optional modules?
If your programme includes optional modules to select then, following the instructions on e:Vision, you must select the correct number of credits from the available options. If you fail to do so then you risk not completing enough credits and being unable to graduate.
Please note: Not all programmes include optional modules. If your programme does not include optional modules, you will be automatically enrolled on all required compulsory modules and will not be asked to complete selections on e:Vision.
If you are unsure which modules you should be studying, you can find this information in your Programme Handbook on Canvas. You can check the modules you are enrolled on via e:Vision
If you believe there is an error in the modules you are enrolled on, you must contact your Faculty Programme Administration Team by email as soon as possible to resolve this.
What does my Board of Examiners Decision mean?
See the guide to Boards of Examiners for result release dates and what different decisions mean.
If you have read this and are still unclear, please email your Faculty Programme Administration Team or attend one of our Results Clinics.
What happens if I have not paid my tuition fees?
Students who do not pay tuition fees by agreed deadlines are contacted by the University's Credit Control Team to request payment. Students who do not resolve this may have access to University systems removed, or be suspended from their studies. More information on these processes can be found on our Composite Fee Debt pages.

SOURCE_URL: http://unibradfordac.sharepoint.com/sites/student-casework-intranet/SitePages/Attendance-Misconduct.aspx

Student Casework
Attendance Misconduct
What is attendance misconduct
Attendance misconduct is the term that we use to describe any misconduct in relation to attendance of academic sessions such as lectures, seminars etc. particularly in relation to swiping in and not attending the session.
Attendance misconduct includes:
- Swiping in but then leaving immediately or after a short period (often referred to as "swipe and run");
- Using other people's cards to swipe them in even though they are not attending the lecture; and
- Asking others to swipe you in even though you are not attending the lecture.
These matters all constitute unacceptable conduct in accordance with Ordinance 16: Conduct of Student Members of the University, which sets out examples of unacceptable conduct under the paragraphs 3.12 (behaviour that uses dishonesty) and 3.20 (failure to comply with a reasonable request or instruction).
What do we expect from students?
The University expects that students will attend all their timetabled sessions and will swipe into each session themselves. This is made clear in a number of different areas including in the Student Handbook which states:
As a University of Bradford student, you are expected to attend all scheduled learning activities, and submit all assessed work in line with the timescales published by your programme. The University is committed to supporting students in maximising their academic achievements. Part of this commitment is met by monitoring student attendance and engagement as outlined in the Student Attendance and Engagement Policy.
Students should also be made aware of the University’s attendance requirements through programme handbooks and in initial briefings, introductory lectures and other face to face interactions at the beginning of each academic year.
About the process
Attendance Misconduct Process is intended to provide a simple means of dealing with the types of misconduct set out above. Cases are dealt with in a set manner under the Student Disciplinary Policy in an escalating process.
The process doesn't prevent a School from having their own internal process e.g. if it members of staff wish to give a student a verbal warning prior to invoking the Student Disciplinary Procedure. In such cases, a note should be added to the student’s e:Vision record by the member of staff.
Following any informal School-specific arrangements, the following process should be used:
1. The first time a case is reported, the student will be issued with an informal warning at Stage One of the Student Disciplinary Procedure;
2. The second time a student is reported, the matter will be investigated at Stage Two Procedure. If the student is found to have committed attendance misconduct, the standard outcome will be a formal warning;
3. The third case will result in a further investigation. If the student is found to have committed attendance misconduct for a third time, the standard outcome will be escalation to a Panel Hearing under Stage Three of the Procedure which could result in the suspension or expulsion of the student.

SOURCE_URL: http://unibradfordac.sharepoint.com/sites/timetabling-intranet/SitePages/STA_Student_Attendance.aspx

Timetabling, Attendance and Room Bookings
Student Engagement & Attendance
Attendance Reports
PATs, Programme and Module Leaders receive a weekly report via email containing links to a database of student attendance - for more information on this please visit the Weekly Reports page.
Office for Students Info
The University is required to continue to make regular reports about its activities to the Office for Students (OfS), the Home Office, professional bodies that accredit our programmes and graduates, the Higher Education Statistics Agency (HESA), the NHS, and the Student Loans Company. Monitoring attendance allows us to complete these reports.
How is student attendance recorded?
Undergraduate & Postgraduate Taught:
- The University provides the student with a student card.
- The student swipes into their timetabled sessions.
- Card readers are fixed near teaching room entrances (lecture theatres, labs, seminar rooms).
- If a student swipes their card more 15 minutes early or late, they will not receive a present mark.
- From September 2024, students will not be able to sign in using a manual register if they forget their card. Manual register marks should only be collected in rooms without a Tacim scanner.
Research Students:
- Specific information on engagement requirements can be found on the PGR sharepoint page.
If the room you are teaching in doesn't have a card reader then please use a manual register.
Paper Check In Form
Please download the form, take it with you, and complete before the end of your teaching session.
It should then be returned to the Programme Admin Team at the end of the teaching day - EDT, FMLSS & FLS in Richmond C21, or FHS in Horton A1.07, where the relevant team will update the attendance for that session.
Swiping Misconduct
Students should be aware that activities such as the below may constitute fraudulent behaviour under Ordinance 16 of the University and result in sanctions under Attendance Misconduct:
- Swiping in but then leaving immediately or after a short period (often referred to as "swipe and run");
- Using other people's cards to swipe them in even though they are not attending the lecture; and
- Asking others to swipe you in even though you are not attending the lecture.
What happens if a student has low engagement?
Students are monitored using the Student Engagement Process. This is a three stage approach which aims to re-engage students and challenge low attendance.
Stage 1 letters are sent out via the Student Engagement team. PATs should then contact students at Stage 1 and discuss their attendance. This must be recorded in Contact Notes on E:Vision for auit purposes. PATs and Programme Teams are able to escalate students to stage 2 when serious concerns arise.
Low attendance and engagement may lead to:
- Student withdrawal recommendations.
- UKVI student visas may lose sponsorship which affects graduate visa routes.
- Impact on student loans, fees, and other financial aspects.
Professionally accredited programmes such as nursing, have specific guidance on attendance requirements. Students can consult their programme handbook or programme team.
Placement Attendance is not recorded through the card swipe in machines on campus. Any absences or issues with attendance must be reported to the placement team.
Student support
Alongside the pressures that students may experience with their studies, students may be dealing with financial, family or personal difficulties.
The University offers various kinds of support including a counselling service, support for students with disabilities, financial and visa advice.
Students can be directed to the Student Life & Wellbeing Services page on the Sharepoint which details the different ways they can get support.

Fees and Finance Team
Our mission is to support you by managing any tuition fees or scholarships attached to your account. We are here to help you understand and navigate your tuition fees, explain fee charges, and support you through the scholarship process.
1. Scholarships
We offer a variety of scholarship opportunities to support students in achieving their educational goals. Our team assists in the application process and provides guidance on eligibility and other essential criteria.
Our scholarships can be defined by either applied for or automatically applied. Our applied for scholarships require a written application, whereas our automatically applied for scholarships are based on a set of pre-defined criteria and are applied once you enrol at the University.
You are strongly encouraged to visit our scholarships webpage link where you will get more of an insight into the type of bursaries and scholarships the University offers.
2. Tuition Fees
It is important that you understand your tuition fee charges as they’re often the most considerable outgoing that you will be faced with whilst at University. The fees team provide invoices to each student and are available to explain fee charges if there is any uncertainty. Should you suspend your study or withdraw, the fees team will make the respective changes to your tuition fees, ensuring that they are accurate and compliant with the University’s Fee Liability Policy.
How to Contact Us
Our team is here to help! Whether you have questions about your tuition fees or need assistance with scholarship applications feel free to reach out to us.
Fees Team: sas-fees@bradford.ac.uk
Scholarships Team: scholarships@bradford.ac.uk
Bursaries Team: bursaries@bradford.ac.uk

Student Casework
What happens when the University agrees to settle on my complaint?
What happens when the University agrees to settle on my complaint?
When your complaint is successful, we will look to see how we can put things right for you. This may mean we offer you an apology, we review or change our procedures or offer you a compensation payment or a tuition fee refund. If we offer you a compensation payment or tuition fee refund, we will normally ask you to sign a Settlement Letter, to confirm that you accept our offer as a resolution to your complaint.
Sometimes, we recognise that what we have done wrong has resulted in a more serious impact and in these cases, we may offer a more substantial compensation payment or tuition fee refund. If this is the case, we will normally ask you to sign a legal document called a Settlement Agreement.
Please see our Frequently Asked Questions below for further guidance.
Why am I being asked to sign a Settlement Agreement?
If what we have done wrong has resulted in a more serious impact, we will normally ask students to sign a Settlement Agreement. We look carefully at each complaint to determine whether a Settlement Agreement is appropriate. Please see the list below for some examples of instances when we may send a student a Settlement Agreement:
- Compensation payments or tuition fee refunds with a value greater than £10,000
- Cases of any type of discrimination, for example harassment, bullying or sexual misconduct
- Failure to support a student’s needs as a disabled student
- Failure to educate
- The student has already taken legal advice
- Issues relating to consumer protection
We ask you to sign the agreement to ensure you understand the legal consequences and what it means in terms of the contents of the Settlement Agreement.
Who can help me understand the Settlement Agreement?
You will need to hire a solicitor who will be able to provide you with legal advice. You can find a solicitor through The Law Society’s website – Find a Solicitor. If you need further support to find a solicitor, you can contact the Students’ Union, by emailing ubu-advice@bradford.ac.uk or Telephone (01274) 233300. They do not have a preferred supplier and cannot recommend a solicitor to you, but they can help you to understand how to find one.
We can support you with the cost of hiring a solicitor up to the maximum value of £500 including VAT. Please email your solicitor’s invoice to complaintsandappeals@bradford.ac.uk and we will arrange to make the payment directly to your solicitors.
Please note that any compensation payments will be made directly to your solicitor.
Who can be my witness?
The student will need to sign the Settlement Agreement, and will need a witness to sign this too. They must witness the student signing the agreement and also provide their name, job title and address. Normally a student’s solicitor would sign as the witness.

SOURCE_URL: http://unibradfordac.sharepoint.com/sites/student-casework-intranet/SitePages/Student-Conduct--Frequently-asked-questions.aspx

Student Casework
Student Conduct: Frequently asked questions
Who can I get support from if I am going through the Student Disciplinary or Student Fitness to Practise Procedure?
The Students’ Union are available to support you through this process, and can attend any meeting associated with the allegations and can represent you at a Stage Three or appeal hearing if needed. Students with disabilities or those experiencing health concerns can also contact Disability Services or the appropriate Student Support team for help and advice.
Can I appeal the outcome of the case?
If the case has been considered at Stage Three and you’re not satisfied with the outcome, you can request that an appeal be considered. However, a request to appeal has to be submitted within 10 days of you being notified of the decision, and one of three grounds for appeal has to be satisfied:
- That there was a lack of procedural fairness.
- That new evidence is now available which was not available at the time of the panel hearing. Any new evidence must be submitted along with the request to appeal.
- That the decision made by the panel was manifestly unreasonable.
If the University Secretary determines that the appeal is valid an appeal panel will be convened, chaired by a Deputy Vice-Chancellor or Pro-Vice Chancellor, with two other senior members of staff. The panel will determine whether to reject or uphold the appeal.
What happens if I’m still not satisfied with the outcome of the case?
If you are unhappy with the outcome of the Student Disciplinary Procedure or Student Fitness to Practise Procedure, you may seek to have this considered by the Office of the Independent Adjudicator for Higher Education (OIA). This is done through the OIA Complaints Form, which may be obtained from the OIA, or through MyOIA. However, the OIA will normally expect any submissions to have sought to resolve the issue using the University’s internal process, including the appeal process described above.
The Scheme Application Form must include a copy of the Completion of Procedures letter and should be received by the OIA no later than 12 months after the date of this letter.

SOURCE_URL: https://unibradfordac.sharepoint.com/sites/registry-and-student-admin-intranet/SitePages/Tuition-Debt.aspx

Registry and Student Administration
Composite Fee Debt
Information for students
The University’s expectation is that students pay composite fees on time, in line with the standard payment terms and plans offered by the University. If a student is experiencing financial difficulties, they must notify us as soon as possible so we can support them to make payment.
Composite fees include tuition fees and bench fees, together with any examination, registration, or other fees payable in respect of the programme for which the student is enrolled.
Paying in instalments
Self-funding students can choose to pay their composite fees by instalment in line with the University’s payment plans only. The payment plan options available to the student are detailed in the Applicant Information document sent with the Offer Letter. Where this has not been provided, the University’s Payment Plan terms as detailed in the Composite Fee Liability Policy will apply, based on the students (re-)enrolment period for their programme of study.
Payment plans are not available for composite fee charges of £500.00 or under or short courses (duration of 20 weeks or under).
What happens when students do not pay their fees
Where a payment is not made by the required date, a first written reminder will be issued by email to advise the payment is overdue and the balance should be paid within 14 days.
If payment remains overdue or an alternative payment plan has not been agreed with the Credit Control team, a further reminder requesting payment within 7 days will be issued. The student will be advised their account will be sanctioned if payment is not forthcoming.
A final reminder will be issued by e-mail 7 days after the second reminder to request payment within 7 days and to confirm the student account is now formally being considered for sanctioning.
If payment is still not received the sanction will be applied, and the student will be notified of this action in writing by email. Debtor status will be assigned to the student's account and all IT and Library access will be removed. Where arrears are settled in full, or an accepted payment plan is agreed, IT and Library access will normally be reinstated within one working day.
If the arrears are still not settled after a further period of 14 days, further written notification of the sanction will be sent to the student by email that their studies will be suspended due to outstanding debt. Once arrears are settled in full, (or an accepted payment plan is agreed), Credit Control will notify the student's Faculty Programme / Postgraduate Research Administration team to request the student’s account is re-instated.
Debtor status (loss of IT and Library access)
Students are expected to complete payments as they have agreed to, or to agree an acceptable payment plan with the Credit Control Team if there are grounds to do so. When students fail to keep to these arrangements their student account is change to Debtor status. This means that sanctions will be applied and students will lose all IT and Library access. Their student ID will cease to function for granting access to certain areas of campus (e.g. the Library).
While a student remains at Debtor status the following will apply:
- Attending classes - students are required to continue attending classes as normal, and may still be escalated through attendance monitoring processes as normal. Please continue to swipe in.
- Submitting coursework - students will be unable to submit coursework assignments via Canvas and the assessment will be will be regarded a non-submission. The attempt status of the module will be incremented and if students are permitted another attempt their mark will be capped.
- On-campus examinations - students will not be permitted to attend on-campus examinations and the assessment will be regarded as an absence with no good cause. The attempt status of the module will be incremented and if students are permitted another attempt their mark will be capped.
- Extensions or extenuating circumstances may not be requested on the basis of removal of access due to Debtor status.
- Continuing to the next stage of study - students will not be permitted to continue to the next stage of their studies or to re-enrol in any subsequent academic year.
- Graduation - students who are due to graduate must clear, in full, your outstanding fee balance no later than 1 June if you are due to graduate in July and 1 November if you are due to graduate in December. If you have an outstanding balance of less than £499 by this date, you will be permitted to graduate but will not receive your certificate and / or transcript. If you do not make payment by this date you will not be permitted to graduate and your graduation will be deferred to the next set of ceremonies.
- Certificates and transcripts - until students have paid their fees in full, their certificate and / or transcript will be withheld.
If a student has missed too much of their course due to the Debtor status, we may recommend that they suspend their studies and return at the next entry point for their programme. We would make this recommendation if we believe students would be severely disadvantaged if they continued on the course.
Please note that if students have taken all their coursework and examinations attempts, and were unable to complete their assessments due Debtor status due to non-payment of fees, students may be withdrawn from their course.
Debtor status (suspended students)
Students are expected to complete payments as they have agreed to, or to agree an acceptable payment plan with the Credit Control Team if there are grounds to do so. When students fail to keep to these arrangements their student account is changed to Debtor status and sanctions will be applied. If after students lose all IT and Library access, and they do not settle their arrears in full, or an accepted payment plan is agreed, they will be suspended from their course.
A suspension means that students take a complete break from study. They will not be able to attend classes or use any of the University facilities or resources, for example the Library or their IT account. Depending on your circumstances you may be able to continue to access our Counselling and Mental Health Services.
Students who are suspended from their course should note:
- It may not always be possible to return to study immediately.
- Where a student has missed too much of their course, they may be required to remain suspended and return to study at the next entry point for their programme.
- We cannot always guarantee the same modules or content from the previous year will be available.
- If a coursework deadline or an on-campus examination takes place whilst students are suspended, the attempt status of the module will not be incremented. This means they will be permitted to take the assessment at the same attempt.
- Fees, bursaries, scholarships, loans, maintenance grants - may be impacted and students should consider how this may affect them carefully.
- If you live in student accommodation, you may need to move out but this will depend on the contents of your tenancy agreement as different accommodation providers have different rules for suspended students. Please reach out to our Student Life Team for further advice.
- Where a student is studying on a student visa, we must report the suspension to UK Visas and Immigration (UKVI) and the will cancel the student’s visa. If this is the case, it will be necessary for the student to return home. When students wish to resume the course, they will have to apply for a new CAS and visa.
- Once a suspension is reported to UKVI students will not normally be able to resume their studies without returning home. They will then need to apply for a new CAS and visa for the next available resumption point for their programme (for taught students, normally the following September).
- The maximum period of suspension is 12 months.
- If after 12 months, students have not settled the arrears in full, or an accepted payment plan is agreed, and the student has returned to study, students will be withdrawn from the course.
Support for students with financial problems
Students who are struggling with financial difficulties can seek advice and support from the Student Life Team, and may be able to request a grant from the Student Support Fund (SSF) if students are expecting short-term financial hardship and have insufficient funds to cover their essential costs such as rent and food. Students must be aware, however, that the SSF cannot help students to pay their tuition fees.

SOURCE_URL: https://unibradfordac.sharepoint.com/sites/student-life-service-intranet/SitePages/Support-with-Money.aspx

Student Life and Wellbeing Services
Support with money
Our friendly and supportive Student Life Advisers are here to offer advice and guidance to students on a wide range of money matters. If you have a question about managing your money, get in contact with the team.
We can help you with:
- Student Finance queries
- Benefits
- Budgeting and money management
- Childcare support queries
- Income maximisation
- Hardship support
- Accessing support with food
We hold a daily drop-in on a Monday to Friday at the MyBradford Desk in Student Central between 10:00am and 3:00pm.
You can also contact us online, via email money@bradford.ac.uk or call us on 01274 233218
