SOURCE_DOC: horizon.pdf

IT Services
Horizon
A guide for how to use Horizon to access learning and teaching software applications from anywhere, at any time, via any device.
What is Horizon?
The Horizon service makes it possible to access learning and teaching software applications from anywhere, at any time, via any device - bringing convenience and flexibility for you, for timetabling, and in the use of space on campus.
Software applications are installed within Horizon and are accessed 'on demand' as and when needed. All the processing work is done by Horizon, providing a high-speed, consistent learning and teaching desktop experience.
You can access Horizon from student cluster computers, lecture theatre and GTA (general teaching area) computers, and from your own personal computer.
Note: You don’t have to use Horizon to access general online services (like Canvas, e:Vision, Timetabling, and Microsoft 365 online services) – you can access these directly from the University Portal and https://www.microsoft365.com/apps.
Access Horizon from a student cluster computer
1. Sign into a cluster computer with your University username with @bradford.ac.uk appended - for example: jrblogg2@bradford.ac.uk - and your University password.
The computer desktop will load. On this desktop you can only use the web browser (Edge). To access specialist software, Microsoft Office, and your OneDrive files, use Horizon. To do this:
2. Double click on the Horizon icon on the desktop, or from the tile in the Start menu.
3. If you are prompted to approve your sign in request, go ahead and approve it (for example via an app notification on your mobile device).
4. If the virtual desktop does not automatically load at this point, double click on it. Note: Most people will only have access to one virtual desktop. If you see multiple virtual desktops and you aren’t sure which one to use, please ask your module leader.
5. Once the virtual desktop loads, hover your mouse over the top part of the screen to see the Horizon menu. Here you can: 
  a. Pin the menu (so it always remains on screen) or unpin it (this will auto hide at the top of the screen. If the menu is hidden, you can retrieve it simply by hovering your mouse at the top of the screen).
  b. See the name of the virtual desktop you’re connected to.
  c. Minimise the menu by using the arrows button.
  d. Move the position of the menu by clicking and dragging the 10 dots at the left hand side.
  e. Exit the fullscreen mode by selecting the Exit Fullscreen button.
  f. Access more settings by selecting the ellipses (three dots) and then Settings.
  g. If you need to Ctrl+Alt+Del, use the Ctrl+Alt+Del button on the menu (manually pressing Ctrl+Alt+Del on your keyboard affects the physical computer, not the virtual desktop).
See the Using Horizon section to learn how to access software and your files, and how to log out properly afterwards.
Access Horizon from a lecture theatre / GTA computer
For this first phase of Horizon VDI, lecture theatre and GTA computers (that connect to AV equipment) have not been fully reimaged. Everything that is already installed on the physical computer remains installed.
However, in addition, you can access specialist software applications for teaching by accessing Horizon on these computers. To do this:
1. Double click on the Horizon icon on the desktop.
2. If you are prompted to approve your sign in request, go ahead and approve it (for example via an app notification on your mobile device).
3. If the virtual desktop does not automatically load at this point, double click on it (most people will only have access to one).
4. Once the virtual desktop loads, hover your mouse over the top part of the screen to see the Horizon menu. Here you can:
  a. Pin the menu (so it always remains on screen) or unpin it (this will auto hide at the top of the screen. If the menu is hidden, you can retrieve it simply by hovering your mouse at the top of the screen).
  b. See the name of the virtual desktop you’re connected to.
  c. Minimise the menu by using the arrows button.
  d. Move the position of the menu by clicking and dragging the 10 dots at the left hand side.
  e. Exit the fullscreen mode by selecting the Exit Fullscreen button.
  f. Access more settings by selecting the ellipses (three dots) and then Settings.
  g. If you need to Ctrl+Alt+Del, use the Ctrl+Alt+Del button on the menu (manually pressing Ctrl+Alt+Del on your keyboard affects the physical computer, not the virtual desktop).
5. See the Using Horizon section to learn how to access software and your files, and how to log out properly afterwards.
Access Horizon from your own personal computer
You can access Horizon simply via your browser, or you install the Horizon client onto your computer if you wish. Instructions for both ways are included below.
Installing the client is the recommended method for the best experience.
Note: Your computer must have a reasonable internet connection.
Option one - via your browser
1. Enter the following URL in your browser: https://horizon.bradford.ac.uk (or, you can select the Horizon link on the left menu of the University Portal).
2. Select VMWare Horizon HTML Access.
3. If you are prompted to sign in, enter your University username with @bradford.ac.uk appended - for example, jrblogg2@bradford.ac.uk - and select Next. (If you are not prompted to sign in, skip to step 4 below).
On the next screen, enter your University password and then select Sign in.
4. If you are prompted to approve your sign in request, go ahead and approve it (for example via an app notification on your mobile device).
5. If the virtual desktop does not automatically load at this point, double click on it. Note: Most people will only have access to one virtual desktop. If you see multiple virtual desktops and you aren’t sure which one to use, please ask your module leader.
6. Once the virtual desktop loads, select the pop out tab at the left of the screen for additional options, including multi-monitor support (via Settings).
Note: the virtual desktop will open in fullscreen mode. You can quit fullscreen mode by selecting Quit fullscreen from the menu, or by pressing (and holding) the Esc key on your keyboard.
See the Using Horizon section to learn how to access software and your files, and how to log out properly afterwards.
Option two – by installing the Horizon client
Note:
- If you are a member of staff with a University-provided Windows 10 computer, install the Horizon client from the Software Center in your Start menu. (If you already have the client, and installed it prior to September 2023, you’ll need to install the latest version by going to the Start menu - Software Centre, select the Horizon Client application and then Install).
- If you find that you are unable to download the client, please contact IT Servicedesk.
Install the Windows client
1. Download the Horizon client for Windows.
2. Once the client is downloaded, open the file (select Yes if you’re asked if you want to allow the application to make changes).
3. Follow the prompts on screen to agree and install the client.
4. Restart your computer when prompted.
5. After restarting your computer, run the VMWare Horizon client from your Start menu.
6. Select Add Server.
7. In the box that appears, enter horizon.bradford.ac.uk and then select Connect.
8. If you are prompted to sign in, enter your University username with @bradford.ac.uk appended - for example, jrblogg2@bradford.ac.uk - and select Next. (If you don’t see this screen, skip to step 9 below).
On the next screen, enter your University password and then select Sign in.
9. If you are prompted to approve your sign in request, go ahead and approve it (for example via an app notification on your mobile device).
10. If asked, check the Always allow horizon.bradford.ac.uk to open links of this type in the associated app box and then select Open.
11. Enter your University username with @bradford.ac.uk appended - for example, jrblogg2@bradford.ac.uk - and your password, and then select Login.
12. If the virtual desktop does not automatically load at this point, double click on it. Note: Most people will only have access to one virtual desktop. If you see multiple virtual desktops and you aren’t sure which one to use, please ask your module leader.
If you have multiple monitors, you’ll have the option to use fullscreen mode.
You’ll have full access to the desktop, which is created when you sign in and is reset once you log out.
13. Once the virtual desktop loads, hover your mouse over the top part of the screen to see the Horizon menu. Here you can:
  a. Pin the menu (so it always remains on screen) or unpin it (this will auto hide at the top of the screen. If the menu is hidden, you can retrieve it simply by hovering your mouse at the top of the screen).
  b. See the name of the virtual desktop you’re connected to.
  c. Minimise the menu by using the arrows button.
  d. Move the position of the menu by clicking and dragging the 10 dots at the left hand side.
  e. Exit the fullscreen mode by selecting the Exit Fullscreen button.
  f. Access more settings by selecting the ellipses (three dots) and then Settings.
  g. If you need to Ctrl+Alt+Del, use the Ctrl+Alt+Del button on the menu (manually pressing Ctrl+Alt+Del on your keyboard affects the physical computer, not the virtual desktop).
See the Using Horizon section to learn how to access software, and how to log out properly afterwards.
Install the Mac client
1. Download the Horizon client for MacOS.
2. Once the client is downloaded, select the VMware-Horizon-Client file on the Dock.
3. Select the Omnissa Horizon Client.pkg.
4. Select Continue and then Continue again.
5. Select Install.
6. You will get a prompt to install the software using Fingerprint or Password (depending on the model of your device).
7. Once installed, select Close.
8. Open your Applications folder and double click on the VMware Horizon Client (the app can be added to the application dock by dragging the Horizon application onto the application dock at the bottom of your screen).
9. At the ‘Unable to access Accessibility and Input Monitoring’ prompt, select OK.
10. Select + in the top right corner.
11. In the box that appears, enter horizon.bradford.ac.uk and then select Connect.
12. If you are prompted to sign in, enter your University username with @bradford.ac.uk appended - for example, jrblogg2@bradford.ac.uk - and select Next. (If you are not prompted to sign in, skip to step 12 below).
On the next screen, enter your University password and then select Sign in.
13. If you are prompted to approve your sign in request, go ahead and approve it (for example via an app notification on your mobile device).
14. In the login box, enter your University username with @bradford.ac.uk appended - for example, jrblogg2@bradford.ac.uk - and your password, and select Login.
15. Select Allow at the ‘Do you want to allow this page to open VMWare Horizon Client’ prompt.
16. If the virtual desktop does not automatically load at this point, double click on it. Note: Most people will only have access to one virtual desktop. If you see multiple virtual desktops and you aren’t sure which one to use, please ask your module leader.
17. If you have multiple monitors, you’ll have the option to use fullscreen mode.
You’ll have full access to the desktop which is created when you sign in and is reset once you log out.
18. Once the virtual desktop loads, hover your mouse over the top part of the screen to see the Horizon menu. Here you can:
  a. Pin the menu (so it always remains on screen) or unpin it (this will auto hide at the top of the screen. If the menu is hidden, you can retrieve it simply by hovering your mouse at the top of the screen).
  b. See the name of the virtual desktop you’re connected to.
  c. Minimise the menu by using the arrows button.
  d. Move the position of the menu by clicking and dragging the 10 dots at the left hand side.
  e. Exit the fullscreen mode by selecting the Exit Fullscreen button.
  f. Access more settings by selecting the ellipses (three dots) and then Settings.
  g. If you need to Ctrl+Alt+Del, use the Ctrl+Alt+Del button on the menu (manually pressing Ctrl+Alt+Del on your keyboard affects the physical computer, not the virtual desktop).
See the Using Horizon section to learn how to access software and your files, and how to log out properly afterwards.
Using Horizon
Finding general services
- Tiles: Use the tiles in the Start menu (Windows button) at the bottom left of the virtual desktop window to access the University Portal, intranet sites, Canvas, Microsoft 365 services, browsers, Microsoft Office applications, and your OneDrive (file storage).
- Email: You can access your email by opening the Microsoft 365 tile and selecting Outlook.
- Your files: You can access your OneDrive either from the OneDrive tile in the Start menu, or from the yellow File Explorer on the taskbar.
Accessing specialist software applications
Note: Software applications are assigned to you depending on your Faculty’s requirements. If you are missing an application, please contact IT Services.
Find software applications in the A-Z list on the Start menu (Windows button) at the bottom left of the virtual desktop window.
Note: If Horizon is in non-fullscreen mode you’ll see two sets of Start menus and taskbars: one for the physical computer you’re using (outer), and one for the virtual desktop (inner). You need to be in the ‘inner’ Start menu to see the list of apps.
A small number of software applications are delivered in a slightly different way to how the majority are delivered. This means that for some applications when you open them you may see a pop up in the bottom right of the screen. Please do not close this pop up as it will stop the app from opening.
Printing
You can print as normal from within Horizon. Go to File – Print – and select the relevant printer. To use to a printer on campus, select PaperCut from the list displayed.
Saving files
You can save files as normal from within Horizon. Go to File – Save As – and then select either your OneDrive, or a collaborative space (SharePoint group) from the Sites option.
Inactivity and auto sign out
- If you are ‘inactive’ (you don’t use your mouse or keyboard) within the virtual desktop for 15 minutes, the virtual desktop will automatically sign you out.
- If you ‘disconnect’ (by selecting the X on the menu on the Horizon client, or by closing the browser on the Horizon web-based virtual desktop), the virtual desktop will remain active for 15 minutes after which it will automatically sign out. If you reconnect to the same virtual desktop during that time, your session will still be active. In addition, if you remain in the virtual desktop for longer than 10 hours, a warning will be displayed before it automatically closes.
How to log out
Open the Start menu on the virtual desktop and select the Log out tile.
Important: If you’re using a student cluster or lecture theatre / GTA computer you must also log out of the physical computer before leaving (again, via the Start menu - Log out tile).
Help
If you have any issues with connecting to or using Horizon, please contact IT Services.

SOURCE_DOC: installing-office-365.pdf

IT Services
Installing Microsoft 365 apps
Students and staff can install Microsoft 365 apps (Outlook, OneDrive for Business, Word, Excel, PowerPoint, and others) free of charge on up to five personally owned devices. When you leave the University, your subscription will end.
PC or Mac
1. In your browser, go to https://www.microsoft365.com 
2. Sign in with your University username with @bradford.ac.uk appended - for example: jrblogg2@bradford.ac.uk - and your University password.
3. At the top right, select Install apps and then select Microsoft 365 apps from the menu.
4. Follow the on-screen instructions to install the apps.
5. Once installed, open any of the Microsoft 365 apps. You’ll be asked to provide your email address, but instead you must enter your University username with @bradford.ac.ukappended – for example jrblogg2@bradford.ac.uk. This will activate your licence for all the apps.
Android or iOS
Search for and download Office apps individually from:
- Google Play for Android devices, including Chromebooks.
- App Store for iOS devices.
The first time you run each app, you’ll need to enter your University credentials (your username with @bradford.ac.ukappended – for example jrblogg2@bradford.ac.uk – and your password).

IT Services
NVivo
NVivo is software that supports qualitative and mixed methods research. It helps you organize, analyse, and find insights in unstructured, or qualitative data like interviews, open-ended survey responses, articles, social media, and web content.
Availability
- NVivo is available from the Start menu on University general cluster computers. Note: It is not available on the Engineering and Informatics cluster computers.
- Students and staff can install NVivo on personal computers using the instructions below.
Installing NVivo
Note: You will first need to apply for an authorisation code. This code will be emailed to you and will be valid from 1 August to 31 July the following year (to renew your licence, please complete the same form).
Once you’ve received the code, follow the steps below:
1. Download this NVivo12.x64 installer.
2. Open the folder it downloaded to, and double click on the application file.
3. Select the language and then OK.
4. Wait for the extraction to complete and then select Next.
5. Accept the terms of the license agreement and select Next.
6. Select Next to continue - you can choose if you want a shortcut on your desktop.
7. Uncheck the boxes which are not relevant in the Supplementary Add-ons window (if you use Google Chrome then deselect Internet Explorer) and then select Next.
8. Select Install and wait for this process to complete – this can take several minutes.
9. Select Finish.
10. Enter the licence key you received and then select Next.
11. Complete the activation form and then select Activate.
12. Select OK once your licence has been activated.
13. Enter your name and initials. Remove the check from the ‘Participate in the Customer Experience Improvement Program’ box, and then select OK.
NVivo should now launch.
Running multiple versions
You can install the most recent version and run that alongside an older version; however, you'll need to obtain a renewal activation code for the older version. If you do not have any compelling reason to run an older version, we would advise that you run only the most current version.

SOURCE_DOC: onedrive-quick-guide.pdf

IT Services
OneDrive quick guide
This guide is intended for staff and students who are using OneDrive (via their University Microsoft 365 account) to store, access and share University files.
OneDrive is cloud storage that you can access anywhere with an internet connection. You get 100GB of storage space.
Access OneDrive
1. Go to https://www.microsoft365.com/apps and sign in with your University username (with '@bradford.ac.uk appended, for example jrblogg2@bradford.ac.uk) and password.
2. Select OneDrive from the list of apps displayed.
Note: The above describes how to access OneDrive via the web. On University computers you can also access OneDrive directly via File Explorer by selecting OneDrive – University of Bradford from the folder list on the left.
Create a folder
1. Select New and then select Folder.
2. Enter a name and select Create.
Note that you also have options to create a Word document, Excel workbook, a link, and more.
Upload files or folders
1. Select New and then either Files upload or Folder upload. (Please be patient if you are uploading a several files at the same time, or particularly large files. Make sure that everything has uploaded correctly before closing your browser).
2. Navigate to the file or folder on your computer and select Open (for a file), or Upload (for a folder). You can also drag and drop files from Explorer into OneDrive.
Right click on a file once it’s uploaded to get options to delete, move, copy, rename etc.
Note: on University computers the following folders on the computer will automatically be synced up to your OneDrive: ‘Desktop’, ‘Documents’, and ‘Pictures’. Please don’t delete or move these folders from your OneDrive.
Save a file to OneDrive
Note: Saving a file to OneDrive via the method described below is only applicable for University computers.
1. From the application you are working in (for example, Microsoft Word), select File – Save As.
2. Select OneDrive – University of Bradford (your username will appear underneath.
3. Select a subfolder within OneDrive if necessary.
Share files with others
1. Right click on the file you want to share and then select Share.
2. In the panel that opens, enter the name of the person, or people you want to share with and select them. (There are also options in this panel to allow the recipients to be able to edit the file, rather than just view it).
3. Type a message if you wish, and then select Send. The recipients will receive an email inviting them to view the file, along with a link.
Important:
- Be aware that when you leave the University, anyone with whom you’ve shared any files or folders with will no longer be able to access them.
- Any business-critical files that need to be accessible to others should be stored in an Office 365
Group or a team (in Microsoft Teams) instead of your OneDrive.
- Periodically review your OneDrive folders and files and remove any access you’ve given for files when they no longer need to be shared.
View files shared with you
1. On the left sidebar in OneDrive select Shared. This shows files others have shared with you.
Restore a previous version
1. Right click on the file and select Version history. This shows all the saved versions.
2. Select the down arrow next to the version you wish to restore to confirm.
Delete and recover a file
1. Right click on the file and select Delete. To recover a file:
2. On the sidebar on the left, select Recycle bin.
3. Right click on the file and select Restore. This will return it to your OneDrive storage.
Important: Deleted items are moved to OneDrive's Recycle bin and retained for 90 days (or until the bin is emptied). After this, files cannot be recovered.
Move files between folders
1. Right click on the file and select Move to.
2. On the right sidebar, select Your OneDrive, then select the destination folder and select Move here.
Learn more
See Microsoft's OneDrive guidance.

SOURCE_URL: https://unibradfordac.sharepoint.com/sites/it-services-intranet/documents
SOURCE_DOC: online-safety-tips.pdf

IT Services
Online safety tips
Passwords
- Use a strong password and, where two factor authentication is available, it's advisable to use it.
- Never save passwords.
- Never share passwords.
- Change your passwords frequently.
- Don't reuse old passwords.
- Don't record passwords where someone else could find them.
- Don't use the same password for different services - for example, online banking and social networks.
- Ensure nobody is watching when you enter your password.
- Consider creating a password safe.
- See the University's password advice.
Phishing, emails, and attachments
- Unfortunately, it’s common to get phishing emails. Phishing is a security threat. Don't be taken in by fake emails claiming you need to update your email account or that it's run out of space, or that some foreign banker, minister, or general's daughter needs your help for "$42,000,000 FORTY TWO MILLION US DOLARS!".
- If your details are requested – for example, by a bank, do not select links in the email, or reply; the email is likely to be fraudulent. If in doubt, independently navigate to their legitimate website using their known web address.
- Be careful with email links. Hover over them to check the web address that you will go to is actually the same as what is written in the link. If in doubt, open a web browser and manually type in what you know as the correct web address for the site, or Google it!
- Don't download attachments from sources you don't recognise - these often contain viruses.
- When downloading attachments from sources you do recognise, run a scan for threats for opening them.
Websites and social networking
- When logging in to a web site or providing secure information, such as credit card details, ensure you are entering them on a secure site. You can do this by looking for the padlock symbol in the address bar in your web browser.
- Never share your personal details (date of birth, home address, bank details etc) via email or social networking sites. It can lead to identity theft or worse.
- Be careful of any information you do share, and who you share it with.
- Anything you put on the web can escape into the public domain.
- Beware of what information can be gleaned about you and by whom from what you post, both text and images. For example, your home address, photos outside your front door, where you are now and how often you go there (for example, if you check in at a location). Many devices now embed GPS coordinates in the photographs they take!
Antivirus and updates
- Ensure your devices have the latest updates and antivirus installed.
- Ensure your antivirus updates its signatures frequently.
Downloads
Make sure you are downloading legal content from legitimate sites and trusted sources. Otherwise, you may download more than you were expecting to – for example, malware.
Shared computer
If you share your computer with other members of your family, make sure they have their own accounts.
This will prevent:
- Personal information and passwords being remembered by your browser and used by others.
- Children accessing age-inappropriate content accidentally. Consider using the Microsoft Windows family safety features. Younger users may find the CBBC stay safe online page helpful.
Phones and tablets
Help keep data on your phone, tablet or other mobile device safe with our mobile security advice for students, and mobile security advice for staff.
Also, see the University's Mobile Device Policy and Terms of Use.
Reporting data breaches and phishing at the University
- Report data breaches via the security breach form within ServiceNow as soon as possible.
- Report phishing using the buttons in Outlook.

SOURCE_DOC: opening-and-sending-large-files.pdf

IT Services
Opening and sending large files
There is a 25 MB limit on messages that can be sent and received through Outlook. If you want to send a file (or files) that exceed this limit, you can use either file compression or the ZendTo service:
- File compression can be used on multiple files or a single large file. The compressed folder produced may then be small enough to be sent through email.
- The ZendTo service enable you to simply dropped off and picked up files without the need to do anything to them first.
Note: Sensitive or confidential files must be encrypted with a password.
Option 1: File compression
Creating a compressed (zipped) folder enables you to store large or multiple files in a format that requires less space. This makes downloading files from the web and emailing attachments faster and easier.
Compressing files
Right click on the file(s) to be compressed, select Send to and then select Compressed (Zipped) Folder.
This will create a zipped folder in the same location as the file(s) it contains. If you want to rename the compressed folder, right click on it and select Rename.
Extracting files from a compressed folder
Extracting a single file
This method is for extracting a single file from a folder containing one or more files.
Note: Software sent as a compressed folder containing multiple files should not be extracted in this way. If you don’t know what’s in the compressed folder, use this method to view the contents.
1. Double click on the compressed folder. The folder will open in a new window.
2. Drag the file from the compressed folder to a new location in the navigation pane on the left of the window.
Extracting all files within a compressed folder
1. Right click on the compressed folder and select Extract All….
2. In the ‘Select a Destination and Extract Files’ window, a proposed folder into which the files will be extracted appears. If you want to change this, select Browse.
3. Check the Show extracted files when complete box and select Extract. A progress bar may appear. When complete the extracted files will be displayed in their destination folder.
Deleting a compressed folder
After files have been extracted, a copy of the compressed folder remains. To delete it, right click on the folder and select Delete.
Option 2: ZendTo
ZendTo is a web based service for dropping off and collecting files and compressed folders up to 10GB. Files can be dropped off for members of the University as well as non-members. All dropped off files are hosted within the University.
Note: Sensitive or confidential files must be encrypted with a password.
Drop-off
1. Log in to ZendTo with your University username and password and select Drop-off.
2. On the next screen, enter your organisation and select Next.
3. Select the plus button to add the recipient’s name and email address. Multiple recipients can be added.
4. Add a short note if required and then select Browse to find and select the file to be uploaded. Multiple files can be added if the total does not exceed 10GB.
5. Ensure that all details are correct and select Drop-off Files.
6. When the file is successfully uploaded, a summary page will display with a claim ID and passcode which will be sent in the email to the recipient.
Note: Files are automatically deleted 14 days after upload. To delete them sooner, select the file and then select Delete Drop-off.
Request a drop-off
To request non-members of the University to drop off a file for you, log in and click on Request a Drop-off. This will make the process quicker for the other person.
Pick-up
1. When you receive an email from ZendTo, select the link. You should only do this if you recognise the sender. Alternatively, log in to ZendTo and select Pick-Up.
2. Enter the claim ID and passcode within in the ZendTo email and select Pick-up the File(s).

SOURCE_URL: https://unibradfordac.sharepoint.com/sites/it-services-intranet/documents
SOURCE_DOC: password-policy.pdf

IT Services
Password Policy
1. Introduction
1.1 This document forms the password policy and guidance for staff, students and third parties.
1.2 The policy has been drawn from the controls referenced in the University’s IT Acceptable use Policy and guidance from the National Cyber Security Centre. This is to ensure that passwords used to access computer resources are carefully chosen, kept, and updated in line with the University Acceptable standards.
1.3 The University’s IT Acceptable Use Policy states that all users are responsible for all activity that takes place under their username and must not allow anyone else to access IT resources using their username and password. Users must therefore take all necessary steps to protect and maintain the security of their passwords, to ensure confidentiality, integrity, and availability of the University’s resources.
1.4 This policy dictates the minimum that a user must do to conform to this requirement when selecting and updating a password.
1.5 The University uses ‘single sign on’ where possible so that a user can authenticate seamlessly against multiple services.
1.6 Core IT systems and services such as email and remote access will be protected by multi factor authentication (MFA).
1.7 All University IT systems use password authentication as a minimum.
2. Scope
2.1 This policy applies to students, staff, temporary workers, associates, and other workers at the University, including all personnel affiliated with third parties such as contractors / consultants, and emeritus users who have been provided with a University IT account.
2.2 This policy applies to standard users. Additional requirements for privileged users / administrators, and system specific accounts, are defined in a controls document approved and managed by the IT Services department.
3. Policy statements
3.1 All University users and third parties must:
- set a unique password for their University account
- not re-use the password
- not use their University password for any other services, whether personal or work / study related
3.2 All University users and third parties must protect their password and not share them with other users. The use of shared generic accounts will only be permitted following approval by IT Services, and in exceptional cases only.
3.3 Passwords shall be at least 12 characters in length, although longer passwords or passphrases are recommended.
3.4 Passwords must not contain the following:
- name or account username
- date of birth
- address (or any part of it)
- name of relatives or pets
- other personally identifiable information
3.5 If a University user knows or suspects that their password has been compromised, they must immediately notify IT Services, and change their password.
3.6 New passwords must not be the same as previously used passwords. Where possible, technical controls will be used to enforce this restriction.
3.7 A password change will be enforced without notice when in response to security incidents and emerging risks or where IT monitoring detects suspicious activity.
3.8 Passwords must not be based on commonly used passwords – for example, ‘qwerty1234’, ‘pa55word1234’ etc. Where possible, technical controls should be used to enforce this restriction.
3.9 Passphrases, including groups of random words are recommended as good strong password choices – for example, ‘Mybr0th3r1sta11’.
4. Multi factor authentication (MFA)
4.1 MFA protects user accounts and information by combining a password with an added factor – this includes, but is not limited to, a smartphone authenticator app, a one-time password or pin (OTP), an SMS text code, and a hardware token / key.
4.2 All University users and third parties must enrol in University MFA
processes, where they are deployed, to protect their account.
4.3 IT advice and guidance on password and MFA can be found on the IT Services intranet site.
5. Enforcement and sanctions
5.1 The University reserves the right to audit compliance with the policy from time to time. The breach of this policy shall be dealt with in accordance with the University’s Disciplinary Policy.

--- SOURCE SEPARATOR ---

SOURCE_URL: https://unibradfordac.sharepoint.com/sites/it-services-intranet/documents
SOURCE_DOC: research-data-management-it-security-guidance.pdf

It Service
Research data management
IT security guidance
1. Introduction and purpose
The purpose of this document is to set out University of Bradford’s guidance for members of the University engaging in research in relation to the security of data, and to signpost researchers to relevant specific guidance. This document complements the Research Data Management Policy.
2. What is research data management?
Research data management (RDM) is a model which helps us to store, preserve and share data collected during a research project. The current definition of Research Data Management can be found in the Research Data Management Policy.
3. What are data?
Research Data: is the evidence that underpins the answer to the research question and can be used to validate findings. This might be quantitative information or qualitative statements collected by researchers in the course of their work by experimentation, observation, modelling, interview or other methods, or information derived from existing evidence. Research data may take the form of numbers, symbols, text, images or sounds, including computer code, annotated fieldwork observations, or a descriptive record of a physical sample. Appropriately stored and secured data is necessary for compliance with data protection legislation e.g. the General Data Protection rules (GDPR) Security Rule; A key principle of the UK GDPR is that personal data should be processed and secured by means of appropriate technical and organisational measures.
When data is properly stored, it can be quickly and easily accessed by appropriate persons when required.
All data is valuable and should be appropriately protected depending on how sensitive the data is.
4. Scope
This document applies to all staff and students at the University of Bradford engaged in research activities. It also applies to anyone working in the University, including casual workers, visiting or emeritus researchers, associates, honorary contract holders, consultants, agents, volunteers, or contractors undertaking research under the auspices of the University of Bradford, using the University’s facilities, on the University’s premises anywhere in the world, or elsewhere on behalf of the University.
This document also applies in full to undergraduates and taught postgraduates whose research findings are included in published research outputs. The University of Bradford will provide training support, and in turn expect all students to adhere to the principles of good Research Data management and academic integrity.
5. Glossary
Encryption is the process of converting data into a code, especially to prevent unauthorized access.
Information Store is where information is held / stored, for example, paper records in a filing cabinet, data held in IT systems, records stored onsite and offsite (e.g., in the Cloud), approved encrypted removable media. This list is not exhaustive.
6. Information Owners.
Information Owners are accountable for information held within the Information Store including the approval of any processing activities. In the University, Data Stewards or Principal Investigators are types of Information Owner. Where the information in an Information Store is created through many processing activities and accessed by many users (such as the University’s research studies), the Information Owner may delegate responsibility for the processing activities to a ‘Processing Activity Owner’. Information Owners may include, for example, the creators of databases, spreadsheets, computer code or physical sample collections (this list is not intended to be exhaustive) where they may also be the Processing Activity Owner. The Research Data Management Policy document describes the wider accountabilities of Information Owners / Principal Investigators.
7. Intellectual Property Rights
Intellectual Property Rights (IPR) are subject to the University’s Intellectual Property Policy and guidance. IPR might also be defined through further agreements e.g., grants, consortium agreements, or commercial agreements.
Where no external agreement exists, the University generally has ownership of Research Data generated during research undertaken by Researchers.
8. Data protection legislation
The Data Protection Act (2018) and UK GDPR apply to all personal data collected and processed as part of a research project. Personal data is any data which could identify a living individual such as a name, a picture of an identifiable face, a National Insurance number etc. Personal data only ceases to become personal data when it is anonymised, not when it is pseudonymised. A list of student registration numbers is personal data because anybody with appropriate access to the correct systems can work out who an individual is from that data.
Data which identifies factors such as medical history, religious or political beliefs, or sexual orientation is particularly sensitive and is termed "special category data".
When you collect, process, and store personal data, there are various principles and safeguards that must be complied with. A legal basis for collecting personal data must also be demonstrated.
Anonymisation of personal data is a process of removing personally identifiable details from the data sets so that no individual person can be identified, directly or indirectly. When a person cannot be re-identified the information is no longer considered to be personal data and the UK’s data protection legislation ceases to apply.
9. Handling research data
Research Data should be held in an Information Store and made available for use in a suitable repository or archiving system, and whenever possible, provided with persistent identifiers. It is important to preserve the integrity of Research Data.
Research Data should be stored in an accurate, complete, unadulterated, and reliable manner. Furthermore, they should be identifiable, accessible, traceable, re-usable, and whenever possible, interoperable, and made available in a timely way with as few restrictions as possible.
Research Data should be assigned a licence for open use in compliance with legal, ethical, and contractual requirements, and providing no funder, third-party or contractual rights prohibit it.
Research Data should be of future interest and the records accompanying research projects should therefore also be archived. Data collected as part of the management and routine operations of a research.
Research Data is a legitimate product of research and should be cited as such, adhering to scholarly norms; re-used data must be explicitly traceable and original sources acknowledged.
Research software and systems should be verified and validated to ensure they meet specifications and that they fulfil their intended purpose.
Organise files and data for access and analysis to maintain correctness and reproducibility of research.
The minimum archive durations for Research Data and records are subject to the University’s recommended practices for destruction of data. If Research Data and records are to be deleted or destroyed, either after expiration of the required archive duration or for legal or ethical reasons, such action should be carried out only after considering all legal and ethical perspectives. The interests and contractual stipulations of third-party funders and other stakeholders, employees and partner participants in particular, as well as the aspects of confidentiality and security, must be taken into consideration when decisions about retention and destruction are made. Any action taken must be documented and be accessible for possible future audit.
10. Research data security
Use strong passwords. Make sure they are stored securely away from the research data which they are protecting. And do not share or be careless with your password. Password advice is available from IT Services: Your IT account (SharePoint).
Ensure that you lock your device if you leave it temporarily unattended, e.g., use the Windows key +L.
Keep physical copies of research data in secure environments where only those who are lawfully entitled to access it can do so.
Remember that identifiable personal information can be in any form (including digital, paper, audio, and video).
Consider digitising physical copies where appropriate as a backup or a replacement for paper copies.
Collect only what personal data you need and retain it only for as long as you need to, do not keep longer than appropriate.
Consider whether to do a Data Protection Impact Assessment (DPIA) to ensure GDPR requirements are met where there is a possibility that individual rights might be put at risk.
Make sure that you and the University can deliver on any specific data security arrangements prior to applying for funding or otherwise entering commitments (seek guidance if you are not sure).
Remotely access data from a device that was not provided by University of Bradford only where you have anti- virus, anti-spyware, and firewall. Research data must be stored on university owned infrastructure and / or University cloud storage.
Use an Information Store, for example on-premises IT infrastructure or University procured cloud services for data storage and Teams / SharePoint folders for data sharing. Use removable media only where there is a clear justification and appropriate security arrangements are in place. Do not download and store research data to your own PC.
Encryption can be used to secure research data, and you should contact IT Services for guidance on the level of encryption required for your research before data is collected.
Consider appropriate backup of research data.
Do not use unapproved Cloud storage; use only University procured cloud storage.
For archiving of research Data see the Research Data Management Policy.
Where Research data is in hard copy form, it should be secured in locked cabinets, with access only by those who have a legitimate right to see the data.
11. Roles and responsibilities
Responsibilities for Principal Investigators, Faculty and The University are detailed in the Research Data Management Policy.
12. Further information
Researchers who believe that the security of a UoB device may be compromised, or that a data breach may have occurred, must inform IT Servicedesk immediately by calling 01274 233333 or by logging a ticket via ServiceNow.
Queries relating to this research data management IT security guidance can be sent to Funmi Inije: o.inije@bradford.ac.uk.
13. Reference documents
- Research Data Management Policy (RDM)
- The University Guide on Data Protection: Data Protection (SharePoint)
- ISO/IEC 2040
- The Data Protection Act 2018 (DPA)
- The UK General Data Protection Regulation(UK GDPR)
- Universities UK (UUK)

--- SOURCE SEPARATOR ---

SOURCE_DOC: spss.pdf

IT Services
SPSS
About SPSS
SPSS is a computer application for statistical analysis. Analysis can be done in both interactive and non-interactive modes.
SPSS is available from the Start menu on cluster computers. Students and staff can also install SPSS on their personal computers. For this, you need to request a licence key. See the Installing SPSS section below.
Licence
The licence is valid for a year under the current agreement and expires on 31 July of each year. When installing SPSS on your personal computers, please select the following licence option:
- Site licence: used on standalone University PCs and personal computers. Site licence maintenance (license installation, renewal) is the responsibility of the user concerned.
Installing SPSS
To download SPSS on your own computer you will need:
-an Authorisation Code: this is valid from 1 August to 31 July the following year. To receive the code, please complete this online form (you’ll need to sign in with your University username – for example jrblogg2 - and your password) and the code will be emailed to you. If you want to renew your licence, please complete the same form. Once you have the code, follow the instructions below.
a Lock Code: this is only required if your computer does not have an internet connection and is specific to the computer on which you are going to install SPSS. Without this you will not be able to obtain the license from the supplier of SPSS. Please refer to the SPSS Installation guide.
Instructions for Windows 10 S operating system
Note: if your computer runs the Windows 10 S operating system, you would need to switch to Windows 10 Home before you can install SPSS.
Important: While Windows 10 in S mode is built for security and performance, it is unfortunately not easy to go back to S mode without wiping the entire operating system. If you wish to switch to Windows 10 Home, please follow the steps below first, and then follow the steps in the ‘Instructions for Windows’ section below. (If your computer does not run the Windows 10 S operating system, you can skip these instructions and go to the ‘Instructions for Windows’ section below).
1. Go to the Start button at the bottom left of your screen, and then Settings (just above the power icon).
2. Select Update & Security in the Settings app.
3. Select Activation, and then Go to the Store.
4. Select Get and then Install when ready to proceed.
5. You do not need to restart the PC for it to take effect. Now follow the ‘Instructions for Windows’ steps below.
Instructions for Windows
1. Go to this zip file[https://unibradfordac.sharepoint.com/:u:/r/sites/it-services-intranet/Software/Statistics_28_Win_64bit.exe].
2. When asked what you'd like to do, select Save.
3. Once the file has downloaded, select Open Folder.
4. Double click on the .exe file.
5. When the InstallShield Wizard opens, select Next.
6. Select I accept the terms in the license agreement and select Next.
7. Continue to follow the Wizard, selecting the default option on each screen and selecting I accept the terms of the License Agreement, and select Next until the final dialog box asking you to Install.
8. Once the installation has completed, make sure Start SPSS Statistics License Authorization Wizard now is selected and select Finish.
9. Ensure Authorized user licence is selected (make a note of the Lock Code that appears on screen, you may need it later) and select Next.
10. Enter the long authorization code you were given and then select Next.
11. Once the authorization has succeeded, select Next and Finish.
Your download is complete. You may be asked to reboot your computer before using SPSS.
Instructions for Mac
1. Open Safari (other browsers work, but follow slightly different steps).
2. Go to this .pkg file[https://unibradfordac.sharepoint.com/sites/it-services-intranet/Software/SPSS_Statistics_Client_27_Mac.pkg]. The file will start to download. It may take several minutes, so watch the download bar.
3. Open Finder, go to the Download directory and double select .dmg.
4. On the SPSS_Statistics_Installer screen double click on SPSS_Statistics_Installer.
5. A screen called ‘Verifying SPSS_Statistics_Installer’ will briefly pop up and once the application has downloaded, it will say "SPSS_Statistics_Installer is an application downloaded and whether you want to open it". Select Open.
6. When it asks for a password, type in the password for your Mac and then select Install Helper. You may see the following message: ‘iCloud Drive may not work properly. Please check the iCloud preference pane. – Couldn’t communicate with a helper application’. Usually you can ignore this error message.
7. Continue to follow the Wizard, selecting the default option on each screen and selecting I accept the terms of the License Agreement and select Next until the final dialog box asking you to Install.
8. On the Python Essentials screen, select Yes and select Next.
9. Once the installation has completed, make sure Start SPSS Statistics License Authorization Wizard now is selected and select Done. The License Status screen should automatically launch after a few minutes.
10. If it doesn’t launch automatically, open Launchpad, select IBM (Other) and then select SPSS Statistics Licence Authorization Wizard.
11. On the License Status screen, select Next.
12. Ensure Authorized user licence is selected (make a note of the Lock Code that appears on screen, you may need it later) and select Next.
13. Enter the long authorization code you were given and then select Next.
14. Once the authorization has succeeded, select Next and Finish.
Your download is complete. You may be asked to reboot your computer before using SPSS.
Running multiple versions
It is possible to run multiple versions of SPSS on your computer if you have sufficient disk space. You can install the most recent version and run that alongside any older version. You will need to obtain a renewal activation code for the older version. However, if you do not have any compelling reason to run an older version, we would advise you to run only the most current version.

MyBradford
Council Tax and Full-time Students
If you are a full-time student, you can qualify to be disregarded (not counted) for council tax purposes. Depending on who else lives with you, you may qualify for a discount or not have to pay any council tax at all.
Who is classed as a full-time student?
If you are in higher education, your course will have to be at least one academic or calendar year long and it is a requirement of your course that you normally undertake an average of 21 hours per week of study, tuition or work experience for at least 24 weeks of each academic or calendar year. Please note that is based on the course requirements and not how long you actually spend studying
Can I qualify for a discount or an exemption?
If all the occupants of the property aged 18 or over qualify as either students, severely mentally impaired or are a student’s spouse/dependent from overseas and the terms of their leave to enter or remain in the UK prevent them from either working or claiming public funds (benefits), then you may qualify for an exemption (nothing to pay) for council tax.
If all but one of the occupants aged 18 or over are disregarded (not counted) for council tax purposes, then a discount of 25% can apply.
Properties with two or more occupants who are not disregarded do not qualify for any discount.
More information about discounts and exemptions can be found on our website at www.bradford.gov.uk/counciltax
Applying for a student discount or exemption
You will need a Council Tax Reference number to apply. If you have not yet registered for council tax, the quickest way to do this is visit www.bradford.gov.uk/counciltaxforms and complete the Tell us about a change of address form. To make the process even quicker you should select the paperless billing option on the online form and you will receive your bill by email once the bill is produced. Once you have your bill with the reference number on, you will then be able to complete the student application form.
If you have already received a council tax bill in your name or you are living with someone who has a bill in their name, then you can use the reference number on that bill.
The student application form can be found by either scanning the QR code on this leaflet or by visiting www.bradford.gov.uk/studentdiscount where you will need to complete the online form.
If you have a spouse or dependent living with you and they are on a visa which prevents them from either working or claiming public funds, please upload images of both sides of their Residency Permit card showing the restrictions.
Queries
If you have any further questions or queries about discounts and exemptions for students, please visit www.bradford.gov.uk/studentdiscount or contact our Student Disregards Team on 01274 437715.

SOURCE_DOC: Bradford%20Wash%20Guide.pdf

Placements Office
Caring for Your New Uniform
Garment Care Advice
For the best laundering results we recommend the following:
- Before wear, wash dark and bright colours separately from whites in cold water or on a cool setting to enhance colour stability.
- To aid colour retention, turn the garment inside out prior to washing. For the best results the garment should be ironed on the reverse side until all moisture is gone to prevent creasing after ironing cool down. Garments should be aired before folding for storage.
- Synthetics and cottons can be tumble dried on a low temperature only, and pressed when slightly damp (Remember – Cotton can shrink if tumble dried to a complete dry state).
- Washer dryers dry at a considerably higher temperature than conventional tumble dryers and therefore the instructions in the manufacturer’s manual must be adhered to. This will avoid damage or shrinkage to the garment.
- Washing powders and liquids containing chlorine or bleach cleaning agents should be avoided to prevent deterioration to the colour or construction of the fabric. It is strongly advised that non-bio-logical washing agents are used as biological agents can aﬀect colour stability and cause running or fading.
Difficult Stain Removal
Blood
Blot with concentrated common starch paste and rinse from the reverse side with mild soapy water.
Ink
Immerse in cold water.
Lipstick
Rub white bread over the area with a ﬁrm gentle motion.
Iodine
Treat with cold water followed by methylated spirit.

SOURCE_DOC: Guidance Needle Stick and body fluid splash incidents - UofB.pdf

Placements Office
Needle Stick and body fluid splash incidents
The University’s student Occupational Health Service is not equipped to provide emergency support; is not operational 24 hours per day and cannot provide Post Exposure Prophylaxis treatment. The responsibility of the initial support to a student who has sustained a needle stick or body fluid splash incident lies with the NHS via Accident & Emergency (A&E) or host Occupational Health Department.
The University’s’ student Occupational Health Service will support any necessary follow up after NHS risk assessment, post incident blood tests and Post Exposure Prophylaxis, if required.
The following process should be adhered to:
- The student subject to the incident should report this to their manager, e.g. supervisor or ward manager.
- The details of the incident should be recorded along with details of the student/source patient, including contact numbers and email address.
- The person that sustained the injury should be directed to A&E / host NHS Occupational Health without delay for assessment to include initial bloods, which will act as a baseline and saved for future testing of blood borne viruses. This is urgent in case assessment deems it necessary to commence Post Exposure Prophylaxis treatment.
- If the source patient consents, their blood should also be taken for testing.
The University Occupational Health Service can support any required follow-up of the student.
- The student should contact the University’s student Occupational Health Service (telephone 01530 239190) after attending A&E or host NHS Occupational Health. It is helpful for the University’s student Occupational Health Service to be provided with a copy of the incident form and any risk assessments undertaken (this can be faxed to 08450 775513).
- The University’s student Occupational Health Service will then contact the student to review and advise and to collect bloods at 6 weeks, 12 weeks and 24 week bloods as required.
- The person will be informed of the blood test results with support and onward referral if required. The University’s student Occupational Health Service will provide support and signpost the student to other support services, where required.

SOURCE_DOC: UofBradford Immunity Programme v1-4 April 23.pdf

Placements Office
Occupational Health
Information Leaflet – Vaccines and Blood Tests
Nursing, Occupational Therapy, Physiotherapy & Radiography
The immunity requirements for these courses are as follows:
- Tuberculosis
- Varicella
- Measles and Rubella
- Hepatitis B*
*Immunisation for social workers only for those exposed to unfixed human blood or tissues.
Midwifery, Paramedic & Physician’s Associates
The immunity requirements for these courses is the same as those above, with additional blood tests to test for infection as follows:
- Hepatitis C
- HIV
- Hepatitis B Surface Antigen
Pharmacy
The immunity requirements for Pharmacy students is as follows:
- Measles and Rubella
Information You Should Know
Dignity, respect and confidentiality is of the upmost importance in this process, in this regard please note:
You will not be required to undress but you will need to expose your arm for the blood test/vaccine. It is recommended that you wear clothing which will allow for this procedure.
Should you wish to discuss a matter in private please inform a member of staff at the earliest opportunity or contact Occupational Health in advance of the session.
We use registered nurses and trained phlebotomists who will adhere to relevant guidance, including Higher Educational Occupational Physicians/Practitioners (HEOPS), the Green Book and World Health Organisation (WHO) guidance.
Vaccines
Vaccines are given to provide immunity and to protect you and others whilst you are on placement/training. Vaccination dates are scheduled into your timetable. You must attend these scheduled vaccination sessions so that you are cleared for placement. Failure to do so is likely to result in a delay in commencement of your placement.
Please note: in the event of a vaccine shortage alternative provisions will be put in place.
Blood Tests
Blood tests screen for diseases/immunity as required for your placement/training. The tests include:
- Tuberculosis (test for infection)
- Hepatitis B surface antigen (test for infection)
- HIV (test for infection)
- Hepatitis C (test for infection)
- Hepatitis B surface antibodies (test for immunity following vaccination)
- Varicella (test for immunity)
Your blood test results will be sent directly back to Occupational Health and any further tests or follow- up will be determined and actioned accordingly.
Hepatitis B Vaccine
Hepatitis B is transmitted from blood and bodily fluids.
A course of Hepatitis B consists of three vaccines given over a six-month period with the following intervals: 0 (first attendance), 1 month and 6 months.
Sometimes an accelerated course is given which consists of: 1st, 2nd and 3rd vaccinations each one month apart, followed by a booster at 12 months. Both courses are followed by a blood test conducted after four weeks of completion of the course. This blood test is to check whether you have developed enough antibodies to the vaccine. Occasionally more doses of the vaccine need to be given, as advised by Occupational Health.
You will not be protected against Hepatitis B until you have fully completed the course. It is therefore important that you attend all vaccination appointments.
Varicella
This vaccine protects against varicella (commonly known as chicken pox). An initial immunity check is conducted via a blood test unless you have confirmed evidence of your disease history. If you are not immune, you will be required to have the varicella vaccinations.
A course of 2 vaccinations will be given four weeks apart. It is important to complete the course for full protection.
The varicella vaccine is live and therefore cannot be given to pregnant women as it may harm the foetus. It also cannot be given to people who are immunosuppressed, either due to disease (including HIV) or medication. You may not develop full immunity until six weeks after the second vaccination. You must avoid medications containing salicylate, (e.g. aspirin) for six weeks following vaccination, and females must avoid pregnancy for three months following each vaccination.
Tuberculosis (BCG)
This vaccine protects against Tuberculosis (TB) and is for students not previously been vaccinated. The TB vaccine is live and therefore cannot be given to pregnant women as it may harm the foetus. The TB vaccine cannot be given to people who are immunosuppressed, either due to disease (including HIV) or medication.
You will require a blood test (IGRA). If, based on the blood test results, you require the vaccine it must be administered within 3 months of the blood test. If you have no intention of having the vaccine, please state before the blood test is undertaken.
Measles & Rubella
It is a requirement of all clinical courses to have immunity to measles and rubella. You will therefore need to provide evidence of this to the Occupational Health Department as one of the following: disease history, blood test results to show positive immunity or 2 MMR vaccines (or equivalent individual vaccines).
UK students should check with their General Practitioner (GP) to find out if they have been vaccinated. If you have not been vaccinated these are available through the NHS via your GP.
If you cannot obtain evidence of immunity, you will be required to have 2 MMR vaccines given four weeks apart.
Please note: if you do not know whether you have been vaccinated for MMR, having more than two doses will not cause you any harm.
International students
If you are an international student, you should try to obtain evidence of vaccinations prior to travelling to the UK. Depending on the University course you will be required to have UK laboratory validated blood tests to verify immunity to certain diseases.
If you have any questions, please do not hesitate to contact the Occupationa Health Department on: 01530 239190
